# Claims ledger — every experiment, every claim, source-linked

Exhaustive pass over both branches (`main`, `feature/two-scale-latent-dynamics-paper`
+ its open PR `two-scale-real-fix`) plus the archived pre-defense material in
`../files/` and `../archive/`. Every row is a claim someone actually wrote
down somewhere in this project, with where it comes from and how solid it is.

Status key:
- **Verified-live** — I re-derived this myself (ran code, recomputed from a
  CSV, checked git objects), not just read a claim about it.
- **Trusted-cached** — genuine result, but only re-derivable in principle;
  I read the committed CSV/code and the process evidence is consistent
  (see `guide.md` §6), but did not re-run it myself (needs GPU/Kaggle).
- **Contradicted** — a later experiment in the same repo overturns or
  qualifies this claim.
- **Confirmed-fabricated** — not a real result; proven so, either by the
  team itself or by me independently.
- **Unimplemented** — code exists as a stub/placeholder only.

---

## A. Theory (archived, `files/`)

| # | Claim | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Strict contraction (ρ<1) ⇒ unique fixed point, memory of h_0 decays as cᵗ, max distinguishable history `T_max ≤ log(D/ε)/log(1/c)` | `files/contraction_proof.md` | **Verified-live** — checked the algebra myself (Banach fixed point + the log-inequality manipulation); it's correct, standard, and the derivation holds |
| A2 | "Pushing a model to always settle backfires on tasks needing running state/counting" | `files/contraction_proof.md` §5, `presentation.md` (archived) | Verified-live *as a mathematical consequence of A1*; **not yet connected empirically** to a measured ρ(∂ₕR) on real Huginn — nobody has computed the model's actual spectral radius (flagged as future work in main's README) |
| A3 | Literature survey of ~30 papers (TDA, spectral/dynamical, grokking) mapped to candidate metrics | `files/theoretical_framework.md` | Unverified as a literature review (I didn't re-check every cited paper) but internally coherent; explicitly self-flagged by its author as a live/incomplete doc ("this my inintal search i will ubdate this file") |
| A4 | Baseline TRM (64D, synthetic counting): 100% in-distribution (unconstrained) vs 90% (β=5.0 contracting); both 0% at length-45 extrapolation | `presentation.md` (archived), `src/train_tiny_recursive.py` | **Verified-live, 2026-07-17: actually ran it** (CPU, ~1 min/run, isolated `.venv` at repo top level with torch+numpy). Unconstrained (β=0): 100.00% in-dist / 0.00% extrapolation, reproduced. Contracting (β=5.0): **run-to-run noise** — got 100%/100%/90% in-distribution across 3 repeated runs (the script sets no random seed anywhere, so this varies), extrapolation robustly 0.00% in every run of either condition. The presentation's "90%" is a real, reproducible-in-principle outcome for that condition, just not the *typical* one in my 3 runs (2/3 gave 100%) — not fabricated, just noisy and unseeded. Worth adding a fixed seed if this baseline is used again. This toy model is a different codebase from the Huginn work (`src/` at repo top level, not `Geometry-of-Reasoning-Trajectories/`) and the team has since moved on to Huginn directly. |
| A5 | `task.md`'s "Develop synthetic task suite (counting, parity, state-tracking FSA)" — checked off as done | `src/synthetic_tasks.py`, `task.md` (archived) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-17: all three run without crashing** (parity, FSA also trained/evaluated via `train_tiny_recursive.py`). Parity: 100% in-dist, 46.75% extrapolation (reasonable — parity is a genuinely hard extrapolation task). **FSA looks broken as a task, not just hard**: 30% in-distribution (barely above the 33% random-guess floor for 3 classes — the model isn't learning the FSA at all) yet **50.75% extrapolation — higher than in-distribution**, which shouldn't happen for a task that's supposed to get harder with length. **Correction, 2026-07-18: my original hypothesis (label-distribution concentration at length 45) was checked directly and is wrong** — the label distribution is nearly identical at length 15 vs 45 (~50%/24%/25% split at both, majority-class baseline 49.6% vs 48.7%). The real anomaly is different and arguably worse: the trained model's **in-distribution accuracy (30%) is below the trivial majority-class baseline (49.6%)** — it's actively doing worse than guessing the most common class every time — while extrapolation accuracy (50.75%) lands close to *that same* ~49% baseline. So "extrapolation beats in-distribution" isn't a length-extrapolation artifact; it's that the model learns something actively counterproductive in-distribution (for reasons not diagnosed here — possibly too few epochs for a harder task, or an architecture mismatch specific to 3-state FSA tracking) and regresses toward baseline-ish behavior out of distribution. If this baseline is revisited, don't reuse either my original or this explanation without further digging — the training dynamics themselves need investigating, not just the label distribution. |
| A6 | H3 direct empirical test (new, 2026-07-18): does measured ρ(∂ₕR) actually predict state-tracking failure, and does it matter which architecture class is tested? | `src/spectral.py` (power-iteration operator-norm estimator, self-tested against known-answer linear/nonlinear maps, 5/5 pass), `src/h3_validation.py` (depth-recurrent sweep), `src/sequential_h3_validation.py` (recurrent-over-time sweep), `src/h3_results/FINDINGS.md` | **Verified-live — a real, novel finding, not in any prior version of this ledger.** On the recurrent-**depth** toy model (same architecture pattern as Huginn: full context re-injected every step, nothing sequential): count information stays linearly decodable from the hidden state (probe R²≥0.996) across the *entire* β sweep (0 to 20), even at ρ≈0.25-0.33 (unambiguously contracting) — H3's predicted mechanism does **not** show up. On a recurrent-**over-time** counterpart built specifically to match the architecture the theorem's own §5 worked example assumes (classic Elman RNN, one token per step, no re-injected context — the running count only exists if the hidden state carries it forward): the SAME regularization produces a sharp phase transition — probe R²=0.79-0.86 at β=0 (mildly expanding, ρ≈1.46) crashes to ≈0.00 at *any* β≥0.5 (ρ collapses to 0.01-0.14) and stays there through β=20. A direct ρ-matched-band comparison (ρ∈[0.15,0.6] on both models) has 9 depth-recurrent runs at mean R²=0.9998 and **zero** sequential-model runs in that band at all (its ρ jumps straight past it, consistent with the theorem's own `T_max ∝ 1/log(1/c)` predicting a cliff, not a slope). **Reasoning, not proof:** the likely explanation is that contraction only erases dependence on the arbitrary starting point h_0, and recurrent-depth architectures (this toy one and, per Geiping et al.'s own path-independence design, Huginn) never encode task info via h_0 in the first place — task info is re-supplied via context at every step, so there's nothing for contraction to erase. If this generalizes, it sharpens H3's applied claim from "contraction destroys state-tracking" to "...for architectures where state-tracking depends on surviving h_0, which recurrent-depth transformers with full-context re-injection are not obviously an instance of" — a real, citable clarification for the paper's H3 section, but derived and tested in one session, not independently reviewed; the team should scrutinize the reasoning itself before relying on it. |

## B. `main` branch — H1/H2/H3 on real Huginn-3.5B, answer token

| # | Claim | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | E1 (PARARULE-Plus d=2–5, n=10/depth): all 40 trajectories settle; winding~depth rho=+0.20 (n.s., N=4 per-level), steps~depth rho=+0.80 (n.s., N=4) | `results/pararule.csv`, README | **Trusted-cached**, and now **partially blocked from re-verification** — `scripts/run_pararule.py` cannot currently run from scratch (missing `traj_geom.data.loaders`, see `guide.md` §5); I can and did recompute the Spearman numbers directly from the committed CSV, which match the README's claim exactly |
| B2 | E2 (synthetic counting): all trajectories settle; winding~length correlation "eaten by prompt length" (confounded) | `results/counting.csv` | **Verified-live**, and worth being precise about *which* number this refers to: per-level (the project's own canonical stat) `|winding|~n_ops` is actually rho=+0.943 (N=6, **significant**) and `steps~n_ops` rho=+0.928 (**significant**) — but that's expected and uninformative here, since `n_ops` and `seq_len` are collinear by construction for this task (recomputed directly, confirmed). The claim "eaten by length" refers to the *per-row, length-partial* correlation, which drops to rho=+0.036, p=0.85. **MAJOR CORRECTION, 2026-07-19: that partial correlation is mathematically degenerate and is not evidence of anything.** Checked directly against `results/counting.csv`: `seq_len` has *exactly one distinct value per `n_ops` level* (n_ops 2→16 tokens, 4→22, 8→34, 16→58, 24→82, 32→106; zero within-level variation across all 5 seeds), so after ranking, `rankdata(n_ops)` and `rankdata(seq_len)` are **literally the same array** (max abs difference = 0.0, verified). `partial_spearman` residualizes `n_ops`-ranks against `seq_len`-ranks, which leaves floating-point noise on the order of 4e-15, and then correlates `winding` against that noise. The resulting rho=+0.036/p=0.85 is undefined-in-practice, not a clean null. Root cause is the task template: seeds vary *which* words appear ("Add 1." vs "Subtract 1.") but never *how many tokens* they occupy. **What survives:** the qualitative claim (depth and length are inseparable in this task) is not merely still true but *more strongly* true — they're perfectly confounded by construction. The correct evidence for it is the perfect collinearity itself, not this statistic. **What must be retired:** any statement that the control was run and came back clean. See also D10. |
| B3 | E3 "killer experiment" (`run_dissociation.py`): length-matched track (needs accumulation) vs local (last-step only), single init seed, N_OPS=(4,8,16,24,32,48) → N=6 levels | `results/dissociation.csv` (5 task-seeds), `results/dissociation_15seed.csv` (15 task-seeds) | **Corrected, 2026-07-17 — my own earlier version of this row was wrong.** Re-ran both scripts myself: 5-seed gives track steps~n_ops rho=+0.812 (N=6, crit=0.886, **n.s.**); 15-seed gives rho=+0.714 (N=6, **n.s.**). Neither clears significance at N=6 — I had previously (incorrectly) attributed a "+0.84, N=10, p<.05" figure to this script; that number actually belongs to B3c below (a different script, different N_OPS grid). Apologies for the earlier mislabel — this is why independent re-derivation matters even for your own prior notes. Local also n.s. both versions; one extra wrinkle: 5-seed local **winding**~n_ops is rho=−0.943 (N=6, **significant**) — the sign is negative and, like B6's maxtask winding result, was never checked against a length-partial, so treat as likely length-confounded rather than a real effect, not yet verified either way. |
| B3c | Multi-init robustness (`run_dissociation_multiinit.py`, N_OPS=(2,4,6,8,12,16,24,32,48,64) → N=10 levels × 6 task-seeds × 5 init-seeds): pooled track steps~n_ops rho=+0.842 (N=10, crit=0.648, **significant**), but per-init-seed rho ranges **0.17–0.87**; local pooled rho=−0.207 (n.s.), per-init-seed range −0.41…+0.28 | `results/dissoc_multiinit.csv` | **Verified-live**, re-ran myself, matches README exactly. **This is the only one of the three dissociation-family results that reaches the project's own significance bar at all** — and its own within-experiment robustness check (the per-init-seed spread) is what qualifies it. Cite the per-init-seed range, not just the pooled N=10 rho, and don't conflate this with B3 (different script, different CSV, different N_OPS grid — a mistake I made in an earlier pass). |
| B4 | E4 phase/force-loop: loop/drift regimes appear when compute budget (num_steps) is starved to 16; vanish by 24+ | `results/phase.csv`, `results/forceloop.csv` | **Verified-live, quantified 2026-07-18** (previously just "Trusted-cached"): reprocessed `forceloop.csv` directly — at `num_steps=16`, unsettled (loop+drift) fraction rises **1/8 (n_ops=8) → 7/8 (n_ops=24) → 6/8 (n_ops=48)**; Fisher exact test on the 8-vs-24 contingency table gives **p=0.0101**. Overall at `num_steps=16`: 82 settle / 13 loop / 1 drift across the full sweep; 100% settle at `num_steps≥24`. **This is probably the project's single cleanest positive result** — fully reproducible (synthetic task, no missing loader, no PARARULE dependency), a real significance test behind it, independent of every problem afflicting the two-scale branch — and it is currently **absent from README.md's own Results section entirely**. Add it. Note this also means the ledger's own "Bottom line" H1 sentence below ("only settle actually observed") needs its caveat read literally — loop/drift *are* observed in this project's own data, just only under an artificially starved compute budget, not during "natural" full-budget runs. **2026-07-24: now has a real reproducing script** — `scripts/run_forceloop.py`'s `main()` runs the actual Fisher exact test (previously this table was only ever computed by hand and typed into this ledger); confirmed to reproduce every number above exactly, with a regression test (`tests/test_forceloop.py`) pinning it against the cached CSV so it can't silently drift. |
| B5 | E5 generalization: parity/"switch" task replicates the counting state-holding trend (steps~length rho=+0.78, N=6, below the N=6 significance threshold of 0.886 — **so also n.s., not confirmed** despite the direction matching) | `results/switch.csv` | **Verified-live** (re-ran `python -m scripts.run_switch` against the cached CSV, 2026-07-17): confirmed exactly — steps~n_ops rho=+0.783 n.s., **and** winding~n_ops rho=−0.086 n.s. too. Both metrics null for switch; clean, matches the README's framing. |
| B6 | Same, running-maximum task does **not** replicate (rho=−0.77, N=6, also n.s.) — state-holding effect is not universal across all state-tracking tasks | `results/maxtask.csv` | **Correction, 2026-07-17: this row was incomplete.** Re-ran `run_maxtask.py` against the cached CSV myself: steps~n_ops rho=−0.771 n.s. as stated, **but winding~n_ops rho=+0.943 (N=6, crit=0.886) — significant**, and neither the README nor this ledger's prior version mentioned it. Unlike `run_counting.py`/`run_pararule.py`, `run_maxtask.py` (and `run_switch.py`) never compute a `partial_spearman(winding, n_ops, seq_len)` — and `n_ops` (digit-stream length) is just as collinear with `seq_len` here as it is in the counting task (B2), so this "sig" result is likely the same length confound, not a real maxtask-specific effect — but that's now an inference, not something the code actually checked. Don't call maxtask a "clean negative control" — it's an *incomplete* one: steps failed to replicate, winding was never properly tested for the length confound. **Update, 2026-07-19: the check has now been added and run** (`scripts/backfill_seq_len.py` reconstructs the missing `seq_len` column with no GPU — the task generators are pure functions of `(n_ops, seed)`, so the exact prompts behind every committed row can be regenerated and tokenized; local branch `feat/close-known-gaps`). Result: `winding~n_ops partial|L` = rho=−0.008, p=0.954 for maxtask, rho=+0.044, p=0.740 for switch. **But these are degenerate for exactly the reason B2 documents** — maxtask's `seq_len` is also a strict one-value-per-level function of `n_ops` (4→15 tokens, 8→19, 16→27, 24→35, 32→43, 48→59; every digit 1–9 is one token), rank-correlation exactly 1.0, so the partial correlation again correlates against ~1e-14 noise. **Net: maxtask's significant winding~n_ops (rho=+0.943, N=6) is still entirely unexplained** — not confirmed as a length artifact, not validated as real. Resolving it needs a task template where token count can vary independently of `n_ops`, not another statistic. See D10. |
| B7 | Counting-task accuracy (via `generate_with_adaptive_compute`): 38% at length 2, **0% at length ≥ 8** | `results/counting_accuracy.csv` | Trusted-cached — critical caveat for B2/B3: geometry is being regressed against a task the model is *failing* at the lengths that matter most |
| B8 | H1 persistent homology on 15 banked trajectories: max normalized H1 persistence ≈0.003 (ns=64) / ≈0.000 (ns=16) | `results/homology.csv` | **Verified-live** — re-ran `scripts/run_homology.py` directly (needed `uv sync --extra tda`), got settle(ns=64) mean=0.003, tight(ns=16) mean=0.0, exact match. Aside: `ripser` prints a "more columns than rows, did you mean to transpose?" warning on every single call here — benign false positive (each trajectory genuinely is T≈16-64 points in a 5280-dim space, that's correct orientation for this use case, not a real bug), but worth knowing before a future contributor "fixes" it. On N=15 single-curve trajectories, H1≈0 is expected *by construction* regardless (a 1D curve embedded in high-D space has essentially no robust independent loop feature at this sample size) — **not evidence against looping**, just underpowered, and the README says so explicitly and correctly |
| B9 | "No looping observed" is not a valid conclusion — Blayney et al. report only ~0.02% of tokens (up to 2.81% on question tokens w/ their prompt) are non-fixed-point; at that rate, 15–40 trajectories give an expected ≈0 hits even if looping is real | README, citing arXiv:2604.11791 | **Web-verified, 2026-07-17 session: the paper is real** (Blayney, Arroyo, Obando-Ceron, Castro, Courville, Bronstein, Dong — "A Mechanistic Analysis of Looped Reasoning Language Models," submitted 2026-04-13, arxiv.org/abs/2604.11791) and its Appendix C is literally titled "Non-Fixed-Point Limiting Behavior," with subsection "C.1 How Frequent is Non-Fixed-Point Behavior?" (sub-conditions "Long Persona," "Long Persona (Padded)," "Short Math" — consistent with the README's "up to 2.81% on question tokens w/ their system-prompt" claim of condition-dependent rates). Footnote 1 of the paper's Section 4 reads, close to verbatim: *"this is rare, the vast majority of tokens reach a fixed-point."* **Could not independently re-extract the exact 0.02%/2.81% digits** — automated fetches of the arXiv HTML truncated before Appendix C's body text. Citation is real and structurally accurate; treat the specific percentages as trusted-but-not-digit-verified until someone opens the actual PDF. The power-argument arithmetic itself (0.02% × 40 ≈ 0.008 expected hits) I verified independently regardless. **RESOLVED 2026-07-24 — downloaded the real PDF and read Appendix C/Tables 3-4 directly** (WebFetch truncates this paper's HTML; the PDF read cleanly via the PDF-reading tool). Both digits confirmed exactly, but **with a critical distinction the README/this row's own paraphrase blurs**: Table 3 reports **per-TOKEN** non-fixed-point rates (Huginn: 0.02% no-system-prompt, 0.05% Long-Persona-Padded, **0.14%** Long Persona, 0.01% Short Math); Table 4 reports a **different quantity** — per-EXAMPLE rates, "% of examples where >=1 question token shows the behavior anywhere" (Huginn: 0.76% no-system-prompt, 0.83% padded, **2.81%** Long Persona, 0.45% Short Math). **2.81% is not a per-token rate at all** — it is the chance that *some* token among many in one example shows the behavior, which is mechanically much higher than any single token's own probability. The correct per-token ceiling to use in any expected-loops arithmetic is **0.14%** (Long Persona, Table 3), not 2.81%. This project's own `power_and_preregistration.md` (written 2026-07-24, same day, before this re-check) used 2.81% as if it were a per-token rate — **now corrected there** (see that doc's changelog) using 0.14%. Also newly available: Table 3/4 break "non-fixed-point" into Orbit/Slider/Unknown sub-categories — Huginn's **Orbit-specifically** per-token rate is 0.01% (no-prompt) to 0.13% (Long Persona), the more precise analogue to this project's own winding-loop metric than the broader "non-fixed-point" umbrella (which also counts sliders, a different limiting behavior this project's `classify_shape` calls "drift," not "loop"). Algorithm 1 (their detection method) also gives this project a directly comparable, independently-designed classifier: FFT-based orbit detection with threshold τ=0.05, fixed-point-fraction ρ=0.9 — worth comparing against `classify_shape`'s own settle/loop/drift thresholds. |
| B10 | Objective convergence diagnostics (Pappone/Movahedi-style): step size shrinks ×52 by the end of the trajectory; `conv_rate<0` for 9/9 sampled trajectories; drift-to-loop ratio ≈12; mean adjacent-step cosine ≈−0.28 (oscillatory-but-convergent) | `results/convergence.csv` | **Verified-live** — re-ran `scripts/run_convergence.py`. The "9/9" is the ns=64 subgroup specifically (9 of the 15 banked trajectories: `track_n48_ns64`, `local_n48_ns64`, `track_n4_ns64`, ×3 inits each) — confirmed its exact stats: shrink mean=0.0195 (1/0.0195≈51.3, matches "×52"), cos=−0.276 (matches "≈−0.28"), dlr=12.01 (matches "≈12"). Bonus, not in the README: **all 15** banked trajectories (including the 6 ns=16 ones) have conv_rate<0, a strictly stronger finding than the "9/9" the README quotes — the ns=16 group just has a much smaller shrink factor (mean 0.097, ×10 not ×52) and a *positive* mean cosine (+0.258, non-oscillatory), consistent with those being the tighter-compute-budget trajectories from the phase/forceloop sweep where some loop rather than cleanly settle. |
| B11 | `analysis/plots.py::scatter_winding_vs_depth` / `plot_pca_trajectory` | source file | **Unimplemented** — both raise `NotImplementedError`; the actual figures in `figures/` were produced by ad hoc plotting code inline in the `run_*.py` scripts, not through this module |
| B12 | `run_contrast.py` (n=1 easy vs hard prompt, |winding| by seed) | source file | **Self-flagged as superseded** by its own docstring — "TEASER, NOT a test of H2" — don't cite its numbers as evidence |

## C. Two-scale branch (`feature/two-scale-latent-dynamics-paper` + unmerged PR `two-scale-real-fix`)

| # | Claim | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | "accel rho=0.997, orth=−0.5" — a deck presented this as a real Huginn finding | cited in `results/FINDINGS.txt` and PR #1 body | **Confirmed-fabricated — by me, independently, not just taking the team's word for it.** I pulled `results/two_scale_analysis.csv` (still committed, 40 rows) and recomputed the per-depth means myself: `mean_acceleration` by depth = **4.89, 5.84, 6.70, 7.45** for depth 2/3/4/5 — a suspiciously smooth near-linear progression — and `mean_orthogonality` sits at **−0.496 to −0.500 for every single row regardless of depth**, exactly matching the "orth=−0.5" figure. That flatness is the signature of `run_two_scale_analysis.py`'s generator (`np.random.randn(steps, 5280) * decay`, decay = `exp(-t/(5+2·depth))`, i.e. depth only rescales a decay constant, so orthogonality of consecutive random-noise steps is structurally pinned near −0.5 by construction, independent of any real depth effect). Not real Huginn output. |
| C2 | Both fabricated artifacts are **still physically present in the repo**, unmerged branch: `results/two_scale_analysis.csv`, `results/two_scale_placeholder.csv`, `figures/two_scale_analysis.png`, `figures/two_scale_results.png` — kept intentionally ("Kept on purpose so the diff shows the placeholder → real-data correction", per the script's docstring) | `git ls-tree origin/two-scale-real-fix -- results/ figures/` | **Verified-live.** This is a residual risk, not a claim per se: nothing at the file-system level stops someone from opening `figures/two_scale_results.png` or citing `two_scale_analysis.csv` by accident — the only thing standing between a reader and the fake numbers is `docs/two_scale.md`'s prose warning. Worth deleting or moving to an `archive/fabricated/` path with an obvious name before this branch is ever merged or shown to the curator. |
| C3 | Real band test (`run_two_scale_depth.py`): length-matched adjacent-depth pairs (2-3, 3-4, 4-5), CONTENT-token acceleration depth-coef\|len = **+1.30**, 95% CI [1.05, 1.55], p<1e-4; per-band +1.57 / +1.27 / +1.18 | `results/band.csv`, `docs/two_scale.md`, `tests/test_two_scale.py` | **Trusted-cached, point estimate independently reproduced — but with a newly found, significant methodological caveat (2026-07-17 session, see below).** I re-fit `accel_content ~ hi + seq_len + C(adj)` myself with statsmodels (HC1 robust SE), completely independent of `analysis/band.py`'s own `np.linalg.lstsq` implementation: coef(hi)=+1.2974, 95% CI [+1.039, +1.556], p=6.6e-23 — matches the project's own bootstrap almost exactly. **Not a bug in the regression code.** But: **the "length-matched" design does not actually hold in this CSV.** `_length_match()` pairs rows by *exact* `seq_len` equality, which is only possible if the two groups share seq_len values — instead, per band, the `hi=0` and `hi=1` `seq_len` ranges are **completely disjoint, with a real gap**: band 2-3 hi=0∈[162,187] vs hi=1∈[149,159] (gap=3 tokens), 3-4: [199,226] vs [185,194] (gap=5), 4-5: [239,278] vs [220,228] (gap=11). Zero shared seq_len values in any band. Pooled point-biserial r(hi, seq_len) = −0.337, p=5e-11 — depth and length are still meaningfully confounded *within* each nominal band, in the opposite direction from what you'd naively expect (higher depth → *shorter* prompt here). This is logically inconsistent with `_length_match()`'s own matching logic (exact dict-key equality on `seq_len` cannot produce non-overlapping partner sets) — **most likely explanation: the `band.py`/`run_two_scale_depth.py` code currently committed on `two-scale-real-fix` is not the exact code that generated the committed `band.csv`.** Practical consequence: the linear `seq_len` control in the regression is now extrapolating across a length gap rather than interpolating within a shared range, which is more fragile than the design claims. Sensitivity check: adding an `hi:seq_len` interaction term drops the `hi` coefficient from +1.30 to +1.09 and weakens it to p=0.025 (HC1) — could be genuine sensitivity to functional form, or just multicollinearity from adding a near-collinear interaction term (hi and seq_len are already r=−0.337 correlated); I can't distinguish those from the data alone. **Not independently re-derivable from scratch either way** — `run_two_scale_depth.py` needs the missing `traj_geom.data.loaders.load_pararule_real` (see `guide.md` §5). **Update, 2026-07-18: the generation code/notebook has now been located, and it does not vindicate the result — it confirms the problem from the source.** `notebooks/02_two_scale.ipynb` (already committed on this branch, `8efc31d "added notebook"`) cell 14 ("CENTERPIECE: length-matched depth test") is the real generating code, and it turns out `analysis/band.py`'s reconstructed `_length_match()` isn't what ran — the actual design restricts each side to a *pool-level overlapping seq_len range* and independently samples up to 60 per side from within it, no per-example pairing at all. Its own printed output: `len_lo=171 len_hi=153` (band 2-3), `210 vs 189` (3-4), `253 vs 224` (4-5) — the same 18/21/29-token gaps found from the CSV alone, same direction, and these are the numbers that produced the exact published per-band coefficients (+1.565/+1.266/+1.184), confirming this notebook run is the actual source. The notebook's own closing cell states its own bar in plain language: "If depth-coef|len is clearly >0 ... with len_lo~len_hi → acceleration tracks depth beyond length." **By the design author's own stated criterion and own printed numbers, it doesn't clear that bar.** This is no longer a provenance mystery to resolve — it's a directly observed property of the honest, non-fabricated generating procedure, self-documented as falling short of its own success condition. Independent statistical re-analysis (separate from the notebook) also found the collinearity is worse than first reported: within-band point-biserial r(hi,seq_len) = **−0.89 to −0.90** (not the pooled −0.337 above — that understates it substantially), condition number 6088, and the seq_len coefficient's sign flips between a seq_len-only model (−0.024) and the model with hi included (+0.019), a textbook suppressor-variable symptom. A within-band permutation test still finds signal (p=0/2000) and a boundary-restricted 10-vs-10 comparison per band stays significant (p<0.001) — so treat this as "real signal, don't trust the exact point estimate," not "fake." The reconstructed loader at `docs/proposed_patches/two_scale_branch_data_loaders.py` is now confirmed **wrong** (guessed the wrong HF dataset — see D-section and `infra.md` §7) and should not be applied; read the notebook directly if you need this function. Recommend: either rerun with a matcher that actually pairs individual examples within a length tolerance (not just a pool-level range restriction), or downgrade the paper's language from "length-matched" to "length-band-restricted, not achieving len_lo≈len_hi in the realized sample," and lean on the interaction-corrected +1.09/p=0.025 as the more defensible number. |
| C4 | Same test, ANSWER token: depth-coef\|len ≈ +0.04, 95% CI [−0.60, 0.53] — null | same | Trusted-cached — consistent with B1 (main branch's own answer-token-only H2 test also found null); read together, this is the strongest evidence in the whole project that **the depth signal lives on content tokens, not the answer token** |
| C5 | Full depth range (no length-matching), `run_two_scale_real.py`: accel_content by depth 2–5 = 18.00, 18.42, 18.53, 18.89; per-level rho=+1.00 (N=4, but depth and seq_len are collinear on the full range so this number alone is not interpretable as a pure depth effect — the doc says so itself) | `results/pararule_depth.csv`, `docs/two_scale.md` | Trusted-cached, self-qualified — correctly deferred to C3 (the band test) as the clean version of this claim |
| C6 | Counting track/local scored with two-scale acceleration: **no dissociation** — content acceleration rises with n_ops for both track and local (length-confounded, not a state-holding-specific effect); answer-token acceleration negatively correlates with length for both, n.s. | `results/counting_two_scale.csv`, `docs/two_scale.md` | Trusted-cached — this is a **negative result reported honestly**: the two-scale metric family does *not* replicate the main branch's B3 dissociation design's positive finding when applied to the same track/local task |
| C7 | `two_scale_allpos` (vectorized) numerically matches `compute_two_scale_metrics` (David's original per-position loop code) to 1e-9 | `tests/test_two_scale.py::test_two_scale_allpos_equals_per_position` | **Verified-live** — read the implementation of both (`metrics/two_scale.py`); the vectorized version is a straightforward numpy refactor of the same formula (mean adjacent-step acceleration/cosine), the equivalence claim is correct by inspection, and there's a real test asserting it |
| C11 | The "acceleration"/"orthogonality" metrics claim fidelity to Pappone et al. | `metrics/two_scale.py` docstring: "from Pappone et al." | **Web-verified, 2026-07-17: faithful to the paper's own equations.** Fetched arXiv:2509.23314's HTML and confirmed the paper defines Δ(k):=h(k+1)−h(k) (its Eq. 1), acceleration a(k):=‖Δ(k)−Δ(k−1)‖₂ (its Eq. 4) — an exact match to `compute_acceleration` — and orthogonality as cos∠(Δ(k),Δ(k−1)) — an exact match to `compute_step_orthogonality`. The paper's own early-exit rule ("a(k)<τ for two consecutive steps") also matches `exit_step_by_acceleration`'s two-consecutive-hits logic exactly. **One fidelity gap worth flagging:** the paper also defines a *normalized* variant, â(k) = ‖Δ(k)−Δ(k−1)‖ / (‖Δ(k)‖+‖Δ(k−1)‖+ε) (its Eq. 5), which controls for a position's raw step-size magnitude; the repo only implements the raw (unnormalized) `a(k)`, not â(k). Not a bug — the paper's Eq. 4 is a legitimate metric on its own — but it means content-token acceleration (C3/C4) could in principle be partly driven by raw step-size differences across token positions rather than purely by "turning," in a way the normalized variant would control for. Worth trying â(k) as a robustness check on the band-test result. |

Given the team's demonstrated pattern — strong burst velocity on experiments (an entire H1-H3 suite in one ~23-hour push), near-zero integration/writeup activity since — this is plausibly the project's largest remaining schedule risk, independent of which specific number ends up being cited. |
| D10 | The length-confound control (`partial_spearman` against `seq_len`) is a working control wherever it's applied | `results/counting.csv`, `results/switch.csv`, `results/maxtask.csv`, `src/traj_geom/analysis/correlate.py::partial_spearman` | **Contradicted, 2026-07-19 — a project-wide methodology defect, not a single experiment's problem.** In *every* synthetic task in this project, prompt length is a strict deterministic function of the difficulty variable: each `n_ops` level maps to exactly one `seq_len` value, with zero within-level variation across seeds (counting: 2→16, 4→22, 8→34, 16→58, 24→82, 32→106; switch: 4→20, 8→28, 16→44, 24→60, 32→76, 48→108; maxtask: 4→15, 8→19, 16→27, 24→35, 32→43, 48→59). Rank-correlation(`n_ops`, `seq_len`) = **exactly 1.0** in all three. Cause: the random seed varies *which* tokens appear but never *how many* — "Add 1."/"Subtract 1." are the same token length, so are "Flip."/"Wait.", and every digit 1–9 is one token. Consequence: `rankdata(n_ops)` and `rankdata(seq_len)` are identical arrays (max abs diff 0.0), residualizing one against the other leaves only floating-point noise (measured max|residual|: 4.0e-15 counting, 2.1e-14 switch, 2.8e-14 maxtask), and `partial_spearman` then correlates the metric against that noise. **Every length-partial correlation reported anywhere in this project on a synthetic task is therefore uninterpretable** — this covers B2 (counting) and B6 (maxtask/switch). It does *not* affect E1/PARARULE-Plus (natural-language prompts, length genuinely varies within depth) or the two-scale band test (which uses a different, regression-based length control on real text). **The qualitative conclusions these controls were cited for remain correct and are in fact strengthened** — depth and length are perfectly confounded by construction, so they're inseparable — but the statistic offered as evidence never actually ran. **Fix is a design change, not a statistic:** vary prompt length independently of difficulty (padding, or phrasings of deliberately differing token length), or use a matched-condition dissociation like E3. Worth noting E3 is exactly this design done right, which is why it remains the project's best-constructed experiment. |
| D11 | `make_three_scale_task` (the fix for D10) — first real result, 2026-07-23: does `\|winding\|` actually track `active_len` (the state-changing content D10 says nothing else in this project can isolate) independently of length? | `results/three_scale.csv` @ commit `c01c3a2` (180 rows, real Huginn-3.5B, Kaggle T4, all configs succeeded), `scripts/run_three_scale.py` @ same commit — numbers below only trustworthy as-is if `run_three_scale.py`'s `compute()` hasn't materially changed since; check `git log c01c3a2..HEAD -- scripts/run_three_scale.py` before citing (see `architecture_state.md`'s verification-practice section) | **Verified-live, first-time real data — and it leans against the project's central hypothesis, not for it.** `\|winding\| ~ active_len`: rho=+0.127, **p=0.088, not significant**. `\|winding\| ~ neutral_len` (zeros, processed but state-inert): rho=−0.022, p=0.77 — correctly near-zero, as designed. `\|winding\| ~ irrelevant_len` (filler words needing zero processing): rho=**−0.460**, **p=8.2e-11, highly significant** — the one variable this design predicted should show *nothing* has by far the strongest, most significant effect, and in the opposite direction from active_len's (weak positive) sign. `\|winding\| ~ seq_len` (total): rho=−0.304, p=3.3e-05. **Read plainly: on the one task in this project that actually decouples length from difficulty, winding number tracks irrelevant padding far more than it tracks the state-changing content that's supposed to be the whole point.** This does not confirm H2's optimistic framing; if anything it's a genuine negative data point, and a specific, concrete one — not "inconclusive by construction" like B2/B6, an actual result that came back the wrong way. **`steps_settle` shows the identical pattern, more strongly, checked immediately after**: `~active_len` rho=+0.189 (p=0.011, weakly significant), `~neutral_len` rho=+0.311 (p=2.2e-05), `~irrelevant_len` rho=+0.480 (p=8.9e-12), `~seq_len` rho=+0.618 (p=2.3e-20) — two independent metrics now agree that raw length (especially the irrelevant kind) dominates over the theoretically-important state-changing content. That convergence across metrics makes this harder to dismiss as noise in one statistic. Not yet checked: `winding_null_test` (does even the weak `active_len` signal clear a chance baseline) — blocked here because `three_scale.csv` stores only summary stats, not the raw per-step trajectories the null test needs; would require a fresh extraction run saving `.npy` trajectories, not attempted. As it stands this is the strongest real-data caution flag the project has produced against its own central hypothesis — don't fold it into the existing narrative as if it's more support for "geometry tracks depth." **REFINED 2026-07-24 (verified by multivariate re-analysis + adversarial review; see `project_plan.md` §0.4/§9):** (1) The shipped `run_three_scale.py` reports only per-row `spearman()`, not the canonical per-level stat and not the partial control the task's own docstring says it exists for. The correct **multivariate rank regression** (control all three length scales simultaneously — a statistic the codebase lacks; I implemented and ran it) gives active_len β=+0.130 **p=0.056** (marginal), neutral_len −0.023 p=0.74 (null, as designed), irrelevant_len −0.488 **p=6.9e-11** (strong negative) — so the negative-leaning reading *survives* proper control. A subagent's alarming "`winding~active_len | seq_len`=+0.318 flips it positive" is an **artifact of partialling out the composite `seq_len`** (=active+neutral+irrelevant); it does not survive the correct multivariate analysis. (2) **Deeper: D11 is not a decoupled H2 test at all.** `make_three_scale_task` places `irrelevant_len` as a *prefix* filler block, so it lengthens total context *and pushes the answer token to a later absolute position*; the three scales are rank-orthogonal to each other but each is monotone in total `seq_len` and answer-token position. So "winding tracks irrelevant_len (−0.46)" is fully consistent with **winding tracking total prompt length / absolute answer-token position** — precisely the artifact the task was meant to defeat. Honest reading: **winding is a length/position artifact here, not a reasoning-content signal**, and D11 must not be cited as a clean H2 test until the task is redesigned (constant total length, constant answer position, vary only active/neutral ratio, single-token capped answer). **2026-07-24: the multivariate control is now real code, not an inline ad hoc script** — `multivariate_rank_control()` in `src/traj_geom/analysis/correlate.py`, tested (`tests/test_correlate.py` pins these exact numbers against the cached CSV) and wired into `run_three_scale.py`'s `main()`, confirmed to reproduce this row's β/p values exactly. |
| D12 | The `extract_trajectory(return_logits=True)` coda-reconstruction self-check (`validate_logits=True`) — does it actually pass on real hardware? And separately: is the V6 correctness probe's "always -1" result real? | `src/traj_geom/extraction/hook.py::_replicate_coda_head`; `scripts/diag_v6_token_gap.py` (the diagnostic that found round 3's result was itself an artifact) | **Coda reconstruction: Verified-live, 2026-07-23, two rounds needed, holds up.** Round 1's fix (adding coda layers back in) was conceptually right but incomplete: `validate_logits` correctly rejected all 24/24 real prompts (consistent ~1.7-1.9 max abs logit diff), catching a second bug — `iterate_forward()` itself applies `ln_f` before returning, so coda needs to run on an already-normalized state (`ln_f → coda → ln_f → lm_head`, not `coda → ln_f → lm_head`); found by re-fetching `raven_modeling_minimal.py` directly and confirming verbatim, not by guessing. Round 2, re-run: `validate_logits` passed cleanly for all 24/24 real prompts, zero RuntimeErrors. **This part is solid** — the coda reconstruction is confirmed correct on real hardware, unblocks the whole V6 logit-lens line. **Round 3's "correct_at_step=-1 everywhere" result: Confirmed-fabricated-by-omission — real bug in the probe itself, not a finding about the model.** `diag_v6_token_gap.py` dumped real top-5 token probabilities and found the target token was computed from the *bare* answer string ("2"), while the prompt's actual natural continuation (no trailing space after "A:") tokenizes as a *space-prefixed* token (" 2") — for one depth=2 case, the bare '2' sat at rank 27 (prob 0.0023) while ' 2' was in the top-5 (prob ~0.065), an order of magnitude more likely and a completely different token ID. Every prior run checked the wrong token, guaranteed to undercount correctness. Fixed: tokenize `" " + ans`, not `ans`. **Corrected rerun done 2026-07-23 (Kaggle T4, real, now in `results/v6_correctness_probe.csv`)**: the fix moved the result from "always -1" to **4/24 hits — and every single hit is `correct_at_step=1` with `target=0`**. Read plainly: the model's argmax lands on `' 0'` at the *first* unroll, but that only counts as "correct" when the true answer happens to be 0. `diag_v6_token_gap.py` independently showed `' 0'`/`' 1'` dominating the top-5 at every step for every prompt (prob ~0.2-0.26 each) with the true target buried at rank 15-280. So this is not the model computing the count — it's a **generic small-number prior** (favoring 0/1) that coincides with the label iff the label is 0. The strict first-token-argmax criterion therefore measures "when does the model's answer-token prior match the label," not "when does it reason to the right count." Consistent with `counting_accuracy.csv`'s collapse (0% at n_ops≥8, generation+regex check) and with the project-wide finding that Huginn doesn't solve synthetic counting. Lesson: two consecutive "confirmed correct" milestones (coda fix passing validation, then a suspicious all-negative result) BOTH needed independent scrutiny — the all-negative one was a bare-token artifact, and even the corrected version needed token-level inspection to read honestly rather than as "4 successes." A usable correctness-timing signal needs a better criterion (top-k membership, or full generation + eval.py's regex parser per unroll), not strict single-token argmax. **MAJOR UPDATE 2026-07-24 — the top-k criterion was implemented and run, and it substantially revises the "small-number prior, doesn't count" reading.** `run_v6_correctness_probe.py` now distinguishes single-token answers (0-9, the only honestly-verifiable subset — 13/24 rows) from multi-token answers (negative or >=10, 11/24 rows, where a leading-token check confirms only the sign/leading digit) and records `topk_correct_at_step` (top-5 membership) alongside strict `correct_at_step` (argmax). Real Kaggle T4 run, independently re-verified from the raw CSV: **on the single-token subset, the correct answer is in the model's top-5 prediction 13/13 times (100%)** — 11 of those by unroll step 1, one at step 0, one at step 6 (the hardest case, target=4). Strict argmax finds it only 4/13 times (all four are target=0, matching the original small-number-prior reading). **9 of the 13 rows have the target in top-5 but never as the #1 argmax** — concretely, e.g. depth=2 seed=1 (target=2): top-5 catches it at step 1, argmax never does across all 64 unrolls. **Honest reading, holding both readings at once**: the model is NOT simply guessing 0/1 and getting lucky when the label happens to be 0 — it reliably has the correct count as a live top-5 candidate, almost always within the first unroll. But it also does not reliably rank that candidate #1: a generic small-number bias (favoring 0/1/2) competes at the very top and usually wins the argmax slot even when the correct answer is right there in contention. The original "small-number prior, not counting" framing was too strong; "the model tracks the count well enough to shortlist it, but a separate token-frequency prior usually wins the final pick" is the more accurate, more interesting story. N=13 is still small (this is 8 depths x 3 seeds, most depths contributing 1-2 single-token rows by chance of the random answer landing in 0-9) — not powered for a precise rate, but 13/13 is a strong signal at any reasonable N. |
| D13 | Project-wide BH-FDR correction (project_plan.md §0.2/§15 item 1) — do the project's ~50 uncorrected correlation tests survive multiple-comparisons correction? Specifically the two "prime target" unexplained hits the plan flagged as likely casualties: maxtask `winding~n_ops` +0.943, dissociation `local winding~n_ops` −0.943 | `scripts/run_fdr_correction.py` @ this commit; `results/fdr_correction.csv` (46 tests, 9 experiments, all recomputed from cached CSVs, 0-GPU) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24 — the plan's own prediction was wrong; both flagged tests SURVIVE FDR, but for a different reason both are still not trustworthy.** 46 correlation tests collected (22 counting/maxtask/switch/pararule/dissociation/three_scale/forceloop tests already had a real p-value from `spearman()`/`partial_spearman()`; the rest are `spearman_by_level`'s per-level test, which discards its p-value in favour of a critical-value table — recomputed here via `spearmanr` on the group means, same computation, p exposed). Raw p<0.05: 22/46. Survives project-wide BH-FDR (α=0.05): **20/46** — only 2 casualties, both `dissociation_5seed`'s marginal `steps_settle~n_ops [per-level]` (p=0.0499, q=0.104). Survives per-experiment-family BH-FDR: identical 20/46 (the two family definitions agree here, so this particular question doesn't turn on that judgment call — project_plan.md §12 point 5 still stands as a forward commitment for future tests, not resolved by this coincidence). **The two prime targets both survive**: maxtask `winding~n_ops [per-level]` rho=+0.943, p=0.0048, q=0.0123 (survives); dissociation `local winding~n_ops [per-level]` (5-seed) rho=−0.943, p=0.0048, q=0.0123 (survives, identical p because both are the N=6, |rho|=0.943 extremal case). **FDR-survival does not mean these are real depth-tracking signals** — two independent, stronger problems remain untouched by any multiple-comparisons correction: (1) maxtask's `n_ops` is rank-correlated 1.0 with `seq_len` (D10) — the guarded `partial_spearman` call on it raises for exactly this reason, so "winding~n_ops" here is indistinguishable from "winding~seq_len," full stop, no correction fixes a perfect confound; (2) dissociation's "local −0.943" **fails to replicate at 3× the sample size** — the 15-seed rerun on the same task design gives rho=−0.543, p=0.266 (an actual FDR casualty there, q=0.330), and even flips sign in the per-row statistic (+0.054 vs −0.348). Read plainly: the 5-seed "significant" hit was a small-N artifact, not something FDR correction alone would have caught (N=6 per-level is too coarse for BH to distinguish signal from a lucky draw) — the 15-seed replication is what actually kills it, not the correction. **Honest summary: BH-FDR removed 2 of 22 raw-significant tests; the two headline "unexplained" anomalies survive FDR but are independently explained away by D10 (maxtask) and non-replication (dissociation) — multiple-comparisons correction was the smaller problem, not the whole story.** |
| D14 | Phase 1.2 positive control (project_plan.md §Phase 1.2) — does this project's extraction pipeline see genuine winding loops at all under Blayney et al.'s own literature-confirmed loop-inducing condition ("Long Persona" system prompt), given this project has observed ZERO real loops anywhere outside the artificially-starved `forceloop.csv` sweep (D13's own count: 1,198/1,198 settle)? | `scripts/diag_blayney_repro.py` @ commit `f1c7940`; `results/blayney_repro.csv` (7,122 rows: 24 GSM8K examples × 2 conditions × all token positions, real Huginn-3.5B, Kaggle T4, all configs succeeded) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24 — YES, and the rate matches the literature closely.** Independently re-derived from the raw CSV (not just trusting the printed summary): `long_persona` condition, 5,445 (token,example) pairs, **7 loops** (0 drift), non-settle fraction **0.1286%** — Blayney's own Table 3 per-token rate for this exact system prompt on Huginn-0125 is 0.14% (`claims_ledger.md` B9); the two numbers agree to within sampling noise at this N. `no_system_prompt` (baseline) condition, 1,677 pairs, **1 loop**, non-settle fraction **0.0596%** — Blayney's baseline is 0.02%; only 1 event, so this is not a precise estimate, but 1/1677 is not surprising under either rate (P(≥1 event | Poisson(0.34)) ≈ 29%). **This is the first time this project has directly observed and quantified real (non-starved-budget) winding loops anywhere** — a genuinely new, positive, decisive result, not just a rate-matching exercise: it confirms (a) the extraction/`classify_shape` pipeline correctly detects the same phenomenon Blayney's independent methodology found, on the same real model, and (b) Huginn genuinely does loop under known conditions, at rates consistent with the literature, resolving the "is this a pipeline bug or a genuine absence" question Phase 1.2 was designed to answer. **Qualitatively new too**: the loop windings observed here span |winding| 0.30–7.18 — several are **multiple full turns** (previously every loop this project had seen, all from `forceloop.csv`, was |winding|≈0.65, under one turn). Five of the eight loop events cluster on a single GSM8K example (idx=18, `long_persona` condition) at adjacent token positions, suggesting a genuinely orbiting region of that specific prompt, not scattered noise. Caveat: N=24 examples is a pipeline sanity check (project_plan.md Phase 1.2's own framing), not a powered experiment — see `power_and_preregistration.md` for the N needed for a properly powered H1/H2 test at these rates; this result validates the pipeline and the phenomenon's existence, it does not itself constitute a powered H2 test. **Deepened 2026-07-24, same day**: re-tokenized example 18's actual prompt to identify what token 165 (the strongest loop, winding=1.22, first of the 5-token cluster) actually is: the word **" makes"**, in "Claire **makes** a 3 egg omelet every morning for breakfast" — the adjacent looping positions (167,169,171,174) are " 3", " ome"(let), " every", " breakfast". **" makes" is not a coincidence** — it is one of the two specific tokens Geiping et al. (2502.05171, §"Iteration Trajectories," already read in full this session, see project_plan.md §13) explicitly name as orbit-prone: *"We often also observe the use of orbits on tokens such as 'makes' (see Figure 16) or 'thinks' that determine the structure of the response."* This project's own pipeline, on a different prompt, a different question, independently reproduced an orbit on the exact same named token. This is a stronger, more specific validation than the aggregate rate-match above — not just "the same phenomenon at the same rate," but "the same *token* orbits." Also notable: example 18 is the *shortest* prompt in the sample (195 tokens vs mean 227) — the orbit is not explained by length. The question itself has a periodic structure ("every morning," repeated over "4 weeks") — a real, testable-but-unverified hypothesis (N=1) that orbit-prone tokens may correlate with periodic/repeated structure in the problem, not yet checked systematically. Per-example loop rate here (3/24 = 12.5%) also runs notably higher than Blayney's own per-example orbit rate (Table 4: 2.50% long_persona) even though per-token rates agree closely — an unresolved discrepancy, possibly `classify_shape`'s thresholds vs their FFT-based Algorithm 1 disagreeing near the boundary, possibly small-N noise; flagged, not explained. **Checked further 2026-07-24, same day, whether this is plausibly just small-N noise**: under Blayney's own long_persona per-example rate (2.5%) as the null, `P(>=3 of 24 examples orbit) = 0.021` (exact binomial) — below the conventional 0.05 "surprising" threshold, i.e. **not comfortably explained by sampling noise alone**. The 95% Wilson CI on the observed 3/24 is [4.3%, 31.0%], which excludes 2.5%. (`no_system_prompt`'s 1/24 is closer to consistent: CI [0.7%, 20.2%] vs Blayney's own ~0.53% no-prompt orbit rate, technically excluded but only barely, plausibly noise at this N.) **Honest state: the long_persona per-example discrepancy looks like more than noise, but this project cannot currently distinguish "different orbit-detection method" from "genuine architecture/prompt-distribution difference" from "still noise at N=24"** — doing so needs either (a) implementing Blayney's own Algorithm 1 (FFT-based, τ=0.05, ρ=0.9) and running it on this project's own saved raw trajectories for a direct method comparison, which is blocked because `diag_blayney_repro.py` only persists summary stats (winding/shape/steps_settle), not the raw per-step paths Algorithm 1 needs — or (b) a larger N. Neither attempted here; this is as far as the question can be pushed without more GPU spend. |
| D15 | First genuinely clean (unconfounded) H2 test — `make_three_scale_modk_task` sweep, total_len constant by construction (unlike D11's `three_scale.csv`): does `\|winding\|` track `active_len` once the D11 prefix confound is actually removed, not just capped? | `scripts/run_three_scale_modk.py` @ commit `f1c7940`; `results/three_scale_modk.csv` (126 rows: 7 active_len × 3 irrelevant_len × 2 modulus × 3 seeds, real Huginn-3.5B, Kaggle T4, all configs succeeded) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24 — clean null.** Independently re-derived from the raw CSV: `seq_len` is exactly constant, 42 tokens, std=0.0 across all 126 rows — confirms the task design's core guarantee held in practice, not just in the generator's own unit tests. `\|winding\| ~ active_len`: pooled per-row rho=−0.088 (p=0.33, n.s.); canonical per-level, modulus=2: rho=+0.179 (N=7, crit=0.786, n.s.); modulus=5: rho=−0.036 (N=7, crit=0.786, n.s.). **No significant winding~active_len relationship at either modulus, with the prefix/position confound genuinely eliminated** — this is the project's first H2 test where a null result cannot be explained away by D11's confound, and it still comes back null. Consistent with, and now on cleaner footing than, D11's own negative reading. Secondary, unplanned observation, **followed up and properly combined 2026-07-24, same day**: `steps_settle ~ active_len` is strongly negative at both moduli (rho=−0.775 mod=2, rho=−0.750 mod=5). Checked the *exact* p-values (not just the discrete crit-value table): scipy `spearmanr` gives p=0.0408 (mod=2) and p=0.0522 (mod=5) — mod=2 alone is actually **below 0.05 by the standard asymptotic p-value**, contradicting the crit-table's "n.s." verdict (`crit=0.786 > 0.775`). Root cause found: mod=2's 7 group-means include a **genuine tie** (active_len=0 and active_len=9 both average to 16.888889 steps) — the project's `_SPEARMAN_CRIT_P05` table is built from exact permutation enumeration assuming **no ties**, so it doesn't strictly apply here; scipy's p-value does account for the tie (a documented, real gap in `spearman_by_level`/`fmt_by_level`'s applicability, not previously known — see `project_plan.md` §13). mod=5 has no ties, both exact-permutation (p=0.066) and scipy's asymptotic p (0.052) agree it's not significant alone. **The two moduli are independent replications of the same directional hypothesis** (different modulus, different seeds, same sign and similar magnitude both times) — combined via Fisher's method (`scipy.stats.combine_pvalues`, the standard way to legitimately pool independent p-values without needing raw-data access): **p=0.0152, clearly significant.** A naive per-row pool across both moduli (ignoring the repeated-measures structure, N=126 rows instead of N=7 levels — inflated N, not the canonical statistic, kept only for comparison) gives rho=−0.270, p=0.0022. **Honest read: this is a real, replicated, non-trivial finding** — more 1s (active_len) makes the trajectory settle *faster*, not slower, robust across two independent modulus conditions — not yet explained (counterintuitive if "more state to hold" were expected to cost more compute), not yet chased into a mechanism. Still N=7 per modulus, still not independently powered per the project's own `power_and_preregistration.md` standard; the Fisher-combined result is the most defensible single number, not a green light to treat this as fully confirmed. **REPLICATED AND STRENGTHENED 2026-07-24, same day, at properly-powered N.** `run_three_scale_modk.py --extended` (N=15 active_len levels vs the original 7, `total_len=36` vs 24, same 2 moduli x 3 seeds): `results/three_scale_modk_extended.csv`, 270 rows, real Huginn-3.5B, Kaggle T4, all configs succeeded, `seq_len` confirmed exactly constant at 54 tokens (std=0.0) across every row — same design guarantee held at the larger scale. Independently re-derived: **winding~active_len stays null, more decisively than before** — mod=2 rho=+0.043 (p=0.880), mod=5 rho=−0.461 (p=0.084, still short of 0.05 alone), Fisher-combined p=0.266 (clearly not significant; at N=15 this project's own power curve gives ~44-57% power to detect a real rho=0.5 effect, so a null here is a real absence, not just "still underpowered"). **steps_settle~active_len replicates and gets substantially stronger**: mod=2 rho=−0.549 (p=0.034, individually significant now), mod=5 rho=−0.739 (p=0.0016, individually significant, essentially unchanged from the N=7 run's −0.750), **Fisher-combined p=0.0006** (vs the original N=7 run's p=0.015 — an order of magnitude stronger, not weaker, at higher N, exactly what a real effect should do). **Honest updated read: the winding-null part of D15 is now on solid, well-powered footing — a genuine negative result, not an underpowered one. The steps_settle effect (more active_len -> faster settling) is a real, replicated, strengthening finding, independently confirmed at 2 moduli x 2 sample sizes (N=7 and N=15) x 4 total independent tests, still mechanistically unexplained.** Regression test pins both CSVs' exact values. |
| D16 | **Cross-task synthesis (the strongest single anti-H2 datapoint): does `\|winding\| ~ difficulty` have a consistent sign/magnitude across the project's independent tasks?** Never assembled as one comparison before 2026-07-24. | All per-level Spearman rhos recomputed from the raw `results/*.csv` (counting, switch, maxtask, pararule, dissociation{,_15seed}, three_scale, three_scale_modk{,_extended}); cross-checked by an independent adversarial-verify agent | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24 (workflow-grounded + adversarially re-derived, HOLDS).** Per-level `\|winding\|~difficulty` rho: counting **+0.943**, switch −0.086, maxtask **+0.943**, pararule +0.200, dissociation-track(5s) +0.771, three_scale(active_len) −0.400, modk-N7 +0.107, modk-N15 −0.125. **No consistent sign or magnitude.** The only two significant hits (counting, maxtask, both +0.943, N=6) are D10-length-confounded (`rank-corr(n_ops,seq_len)=1.0`); **every genuinely length-clean test (three_scale, both modk runs) is null-to-negative.** Adversarial-verify correction folded in: the shorthand "n_ops==seq_len" is imprecise — the values differ (counting `seq_len=3·n_ops+10`, maxtask `seq_len=n_ops+11`), but `seq_len` is a strictly monotone function of `n_ops` so **rank-correlation is exactly 1.0** and per-level rho against difficulty equals per-level rho against length; the confound conclusion is unchanged. Read together this is the clearest evidence against H2 in the project: winding's "positive" results appear only where length and difficulty cannot be separated. |
| D17 | **The honesty headline: is the model even solving the counting task at the depths where `winding`/`steps_settle` "rise with reasoning depth"? And does the geometry separate correct from incorrect answers?** | `results/counting_accuracy.csv` (56 rows; accuracy scored by *generation + regex parse* of the model's actual output, not the logit lens); `tests/test_counting_accuracy.py` pins these numbers | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24 (independently recomputed; surfaced by the completeness-critic pass, confirmed by direct recompute).** Accuracy by n_ops: **37.5% (n_ops=2), 25% (4), 0% (8), 0% (16), 12.5% (24), 0% (32), 0% (48)** — the model essentially fails counting at exactly n_ops≥8, the levels where D16's winding "signal" appears. Within this file the geometry does **not** separate correct from incorrect trajectories: point-biserial `winding~correct` r=−0.087 (p=0.53, n.s.); `steps_settle~correct` r=−0.295 (**p=0.027, significant — and NEGATIVE**, i.e. steps_settle is *higher* for wrong answers, the opposite of a "more compute → better reasoning" reading). **Consequence: the winding~n_ops correlations (D16) are a length effect measured on trajectories that produce wrong answers — they cannot be presented as "geometry reflects reasoning." This reframing, not the isolated +0.943, is the honest headline.** |
| D18 | **Cross-task pattern in `steps_settle~difficulty`: is the sign explained by whether a task's answer accumulates vs. saturates/wraps?** | Per-level rhos recomputed from the same `results/*.csv` as D16; adversarially re-derived (HOLDS) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24.** `steps_settle~difficulty` per-level rho is **positive on every task whose answer monotonically accumulates** — counting +0.928, switch +0.783, pararule +0.800, dissociation-track +0.812, three_scale(active_len) +1.000 — and **negative on every task whose answer saturates or wraps**: maxtask (running max) −0.771, modk-N7 −0.739, modk-N15 −0.664 (both modk pooled over moduli; the modk effect is Fisher-p=0.0006 at N=15, D15). So *settling speed* tracks the answer's structure (accumulate → settles slower with length; saturate/cyclic → faster), a systematic phenomenological finding, mechanism unknown. Caveat: this is `steps_settle`, an internal-dynamics proxy, not accuracy — and D17 shows steps_settle does not track correctness. Related but confounded lead: `count_ones` shows `mean_normed_accel~n_ops = −0.976` (p=3e-5, N=8, `full_synthetic_experiments.csv`) — the tightest monotone relationship anywhere in the project — but count_ones has the same D10 length confound (rank-corr n_ops~seq_len = 1.0), so it too is inseparable from a length effect. |
| D19 | **Init-robustness ranking: which trajectory metrics are driven by the computation vs. by the random hidden-state init (`seed`)?** The project's headline numbers all fix `seed=0`; this is the one dataset that varies it. | `results/dissoc_multiinit.csv` (600 rows = 6 n_ops × 2 kinds × 6 task-seeds × 5 init-seeds; adds `contraction`, `lyap` columns); adversarially re-derived (HOLDS) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24 (novel analysis, not previously computed).** For each metric, compared **within-config std** (across the 5 init-seeds at fixed n_ops/kind/task_seed — pure init noise) against **between-config std** (std of the config-means — the signal H1/H2 depend on). Ratios: **winding 0.026/0.053 = 0.50** (most init-stable), **steps_settle 0.677/0.805 = 0.84** (borderline — noise nearly matches signal, consistent with this file's own docstring caveat), **contraction 0.00105/0.00061 = 1.72** (init-**noise-dominated**: noise EXCEEDS signal). Direct implication: any `contraction~difficulty` correlation elsewhere should be trusted *less* than a winding one; winding is comparatively the most init-stable metric — double-edged, since init-stability is also consistent with Geiping's path-independence finding (the geometry could be an architecture property, not a computation signal — an open concern, `project_plan.md` §10 item 6). |
| D20 | **Two structural facts a truly-clean report must disclose: (a) where the Blayney loops actually sit in the prompt; (b) a residual confound in the "clean" modk design itself.** | (a) `results/blayney_repro.csv`; (b) `results/three_scale_modk_extended.csv`; both adversarially re-derived (both correct after fixing two stated constants) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-24.** **(a) Blayney loop position [novel]:** all 7 Long-Persona loop events sit at relative position (`token_position/(n_tokens−1)`) **0.819–0.897** — the last ~fifth of the prompt; **zero** loops in the first two-thirds; per-third counts **[0,0,7]**. (Adversarial-verify correction: the lower bound is 0.819, not 0.82 — one event, idx15, is at 0.8192, marginally below.) Loops occur essentially only in the question-specific tail, never in the fixed persona boilerplate — consistent with, and sharper than, the " makes"-token case (D14). **(b) modk residual confound [newly found]:** because `neutral_len = total_len − active_len − irrelevant_len` with total_len fixed, at any fixed active_len the counts `irrelevant_len` and `neutral_len` are **exactly perfectly negatively dependent** (correlation −1.0 within every active_len group — verified for all 270 rows). So a joint 3-way rank regression's "`winding~irrelevant_len`" coefficient (β=−0.21, p=4e-5) is mathematically inseparable from −(the neutral_len coefficient), and the large "`winding~modulus`" term (β=+0.69, p=6e-29) almost certainly reflects the question text literally changing ("modulo 2" vs "modulo 5"), not difficulty. (Adversarial-verify correction: the fixed total_len is 36, not 54; the tokenized `seq_len` is the constant-at-54 quantity.) Milder than D11's prefix confound, but real — even the task built to defeat confounds has a residual one; a further redesign (vary active/neutral ratio without making irrelevant/neutral perfectly anti-dependent) is future work. |
| D21 | **Adversarial audit of the project-state recap itself (2026-07-25): do the summary claims survive independent re-derivation?** A 7-agent workflow re-checked each load-bearing recap claim against raw data/code/git, with instructions to refute rather than confirm. | The workflow's per-cluster verdicts (H2, correctness, H1, H3, steps_settle, inventory/infra, docs/accountability); every number re-recomputed from `results/*.csv`, `src/`, and `git` | **Verified-live, 2026-07-25 — most claims CONFIRMED, four real errors found and fixed.** CONFIRMED unchanged: H2's "every significant winding~difficulty *positive* is length-confounded" (only counting/maxtask are significant positives, both rank-corr(n_ops,seq_len)=1.0 exactly; every length-clean test null-to-negative); the modk N=15 winding null (Fisher p=0.2662); `Add 1.`/`Subtract 1.` both exactly 3 tokens; Blayney 0.1286% per-token; contraction init-noise ratio 1.715; homology max persistence 0.009; H3 toy R²≥0.996 vs collapse; FDR 46/22/20. **ERRORS FOUND (all now corrected):** (1) **"0% at n_ops≥8" is false** — n_ops=24 is **12.5%** (1/8); correct statement is "0% at n_ops∈{8,16,32,48}, 12.5% at 24," overall accuracy 6/56=10.7%. (The doc `methods_and_findings.md` already said "~0% (0–12.5%)" correctly — the *recap* regressed it.) (2) **"clean working tree" was false** — three untracked entries (`dataset-metadata.json`, `kernel-metadata.json`, `results/figures/`), all pre-session Kaggle-CLI scaffolding; now gitignored with a comment explaining what they are. (3) **"56 tests pin every real-data number" overclaimed** — 56 tests do pass, but many are synthetic unit tests, and `full_synthetic_experiments.csv` had a **ledger-cited figure (D18's count_ones accel −0.976) with no pinning test**; `smoke_new_tasks.csv` had neither test nor ledger entry. Fixed: added `tests/test_full_synthetic_experiments.py` (3 tests pinning −0.976, projection −0.190, the rank-corr=1.0 confound, and the 80-row shape). (4) **"the winding null test was never run" imprecise** — it *is* implemented and *is* exercised on synthetic data in `tests/test_winding_synthetic.py`; what has never happened is running it on **real Huginn trajectories** (blocked: raw paths not saved). Also noted, not an error: the "36 commits" figure was correct when written but drifted to 37 with a later commit; and the accumulate-vs-saturate dichotomy has a third category the phrasing didn't name — `dissociation-local` (a last-step *retrieval* task, neither accumulating nor saturating) is also negative (−0.812), so the sharper rule is "positive iff the answer monotonically accumulates; all non-accumulating task types are negative." **Method note: an adversarial audit of a summary caught four errors that a careful self-review had missed, including one where the summary contradicted the project's own already-correct documentation — evidence that summaries need the same verification discipline as primary claims.** |
| D22 | **The winding metric adjudicated against a matched-random-walk null — the foundational check the project had declared impossible.** Does the |winding| this project measures reflect genuine rotation, or is it an artifact of settling geometry? | `scripts/run_winding_null.py` @ this commit; `results/winding_null.csv` (140 real Huginn trajectories × 200 surrogates each); raw paths in `results/trajectories/*.npy` (untracked/local) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-25. Two findings: the blocker was false, and the metric fails the test.** **(1) The "impossible" premise was wrong.** The project's docs (D11, `architecture_state.md`, `methods_and_findings.md` VIII.2) repeatedly state the null test cannot be run because raw per-step paths are not saved. In fact **140 real Huginn trajectories with full raw `[T, 5280]` paths are banked in `results/trajectories/`** (count_ones + projection, n_ops ∈ {2,4,8,16,24,32,48,64}, 5 init seeds, num_steps ∈ {64,128}; 140/140 array lengths match their filenames). The check was runnable all along, at zero GPU cost. **(2) Observed winding does not beat chance — it is *below* it.** Against 200 matched random walks per trajectory (same per-step displacement magnitudes, directions randomized on the hypersphere, same per-trajectory 2-D PCA): **82.1% of real trajectories wind LESS than their own null**; median z = **−5.61** (mean −9.83); **66.4% have p = 1.000** (every single surrogate wound at least as much). Mean observed 0.6246 vs mean null 0.6604. Only **9/140 (6.4%)** survive BH-FDR as winding *more* than chance — barely above the 5% false-positive rate, so a small genuine minority exists but the dominant direction is *under*-winding. **Mechanism, verified:** these trajectories settle at step ≈14 (mean `steps_to_settle` 13.7, max 16) yet steps 64–128 still carry 11.4% of total path length as residual jitter at the fixed point. A random walk converts that jitter into accumulated angle; the real path vibrates in place without net rotation. Hence the gap widens with compute budget exactly as predicted: z = **−1.62 at num_steps=64** → **−15.99 at num_steps=128**, while *observed* winding stays flat (0.6242 → 0.6250) and the *null* rises (0.6284 → 0.6845). **(3) Why the number is always ≈0.62.** `winding_number` measures angle swept about the trajectory's own centroid; for a settling path the centroid sits near the endpoint (distance-to-centroid collapses 58.6 → 3.2, an 18× inward collapse), so the collapse itself sweeps ~0.6 turns for essentially any convergent path regardless of content. Consistent with this, |winding| is near-**constant project-wide on answer tokens**: counting std 0.016, switch 0.017, **modk-N=15 std 0.005** (270 rows, 15 difficulty levels, range 0.59–0.62), three_scale 0.035; only the all-token `blayney_repro` run varies genuinely (std 0.450, range 0.07–7.18). **CONSEQUENCE FOR H2 — a sharpening, not a reversal.** The prior framing ("winding does not track depth; clean null at adequate power," D15/D16) is too generous to the metric: on answer-token trajectories **winding has almost no variance to correlate with anything** (std 0.005 on the best-powered sweep), and what variance exists does not exceed a step-size-matched null. Honest statement: **H2 as operationalized was largely untestable on the traced positions — not tested and refuted.** Every winding-based claim in this project (H1 shape counts partially, all winding~difficulty correlations) inherits this caveat. **CAVEATS.** (a) The banked trajectories have **undocumented provenance**: no producer script, no manifest, no record of model revision/prompt/token-position, untracked in git, and ~~the `track_*`/`local_*` files ... **no longer exist** (a different vintage)~~ **CORRECTED 2026-07-25 by D24: this was false.** All 15 `track_*`/`local_*` files, plus a `manifest.csv` recording `kind`/`n_ops`/`num_steps`/`init_seed`, are present in the **top-level `trajectories/`** directory (not `results/trajectories/`). They are moreover the project's *only* genuine multi-initialization raw paths, and the only data able to measure map contraction or path-independence. They are adequate for a *methodological* verdict on the metric, not for attaching to a specific experimental condition. (b) They are count_ones/projection, not the main answer-token sweeps — though their winding distribution (mean 0.6246, std 0.0385) sits inside every tracked CSV's (0.602–0.661), which is the cross-check used to justify generalizing. (c) PCA is *not* the culprit: top-2 PCs retain 80.1% of variance (min 67.9%), and angular unwrapping is unambiguous (0.08% of steps exceed π/2) — the problem is the centroid-as-rotation-center on a convergent path, not the projection. |
| D23 | **The recurrent state is architecturally confined to a sphere — which invalidates the rotation-center, the null model, and the "two-phase" reading of the trajectories.** Follow-up to D22: is the diagnosed failure of `winding_of` really about "the centroid of a collapsing path", and is the transient/jitter split real? | `raven_modeling_minimal.py` @ pinned revision `bb6621b65e90b6a4b9b29ef88dc83866d450470c` (`SandwichBlock.forward` L533-536, `initialize_state` L802-811, `RavenConfig.init_values` L86-91); `results/trajectories/*.npy` re-measured landmark-free | **Verified-live, 2026-07-25. Four findings, all read from the model source or measured without thresholds.** **(1) The state lives on a sphere, by construction.** Every `SandwichBlock` ends in `x = self.norm_4(...)` — an RMSNorm — and the extraction hook sits on `core_block[-1]`, so **every recorded state is the output of an RMSNorm**. Measured: `‖h_t‖ = 76.37` with **0.0073% within-trajectory variation** (min 76.35, max 76.38, 20×128 states). Radius is fixed by the architecture and carries **zero** information; the state space is `S^{n-1}`, not `R^n`. **(2) Therefore `winding_of`'s rotation center is off-manifold.** The Euclidean centroid of points on a sphere lies strictly inside it: `‖centroid‖ = 74.20` vs radius `76.37`, i.e. **2.8% inside the manifold**. The metric measures angle about a point the state can never occupy. This **supersedes D22(3)**: the "18× collapse toward the centroid" was a symptom described in the wrong chart, not the mechanism. **(3) The matched-random-walk null leaves the manifold entirely.** `matched_random_walk` preserves step magnitudes but randomizes directions in ambient `R^n`, so surrogate norms diffuse from 76.37 to **154.76** — a **5.86%** norm deviation vs real **0.0073%**, over 2× the sphere radius. **Every null test in this project (D22's, and all nine variants) has been comparing an on-manifold real path against off-manifold surrogates**, so "beats/loses to the null" partly measures on- vs off-manifold, not dynamics. Any future null must be constrained to the sphere. **(4) "Two-phase" was an artifact of a thresholded landmark; the truth is one exponential plus a floor.** Using `steps_to_settle` (first step below 10% of max displacement) manufactured a split. Landmark-free, `log‖h_t − h*‖` vs `t` (h* = mean of last 20 states) is a **single clean exponential**: per-step contraction **0.871 (R²=0.9994)** over t=1–15 and **0.906 (R²=0.981)** over t=15–40, then **0.999 (R²=0.246)** — no decay and no structure — from t=40. There are not two dynamical regimes; there is exponential convergence at one rate to a **limit set of nonzero diameter ≈0.97** (1.1% of the state norm; **0.63°** angular radius). Independent support: DMD on the tail returns `\|λ\| = 0.868`, matching the directly-fitted 0.871. **(5) Initialization scale is Huginn's own, not ours.** `hook.py:152` calls `model(input_ids=..., num_steps=...)` with no `input_states` and no `init_scale`, so `initialize_state` runs at its native default: per-coordinate std `= sqrt(2/(5·n_embd))·sqrt(n_embd) = sqrt(2/5) = 0.6325` exactly. `‖h_0 − h*‖/‖h*‖ = 1.18` — h_0 sits about one attractor-radius away, i.e. the transient is *not* an artifact of an oversized initialization. **CONSEQUENCES.** (a) **H3 now has a real number on real Huginn for the first time**: the empirical per-step contraction is **ρ ≈ 0.87–0.91 < 1**, closing the gap A2/D21 flagged as never measured — though this is an orbit-fit, and the Jacobian-spectrum measurement (Arnoldi on JVPs of the core block) is still the exact version and still undone. (b) Contraction alone does **not** rule out a *bounded* counter: the limit set has diameter ~1 in 5279 tangent dimensions, ample room to separate 64 states. H3's applied claim should be scoped to *unbounded* registers. (c) All Euclidean/PCA geometry on the transient is in the wrong chart — the transient sweeps **69.7° of geodesic**, which no 2-plane can represent; the tail's 0.63° cap is effectively flat, so tangent-space analysis there is sound. (d) `winding_variants.py`'s module docstring states the superseded D22(3) mechanism and needs correcting. |
| D24 | **Whole-project rigor re-audit: metrics, hyperparameters, arbitrary pre/post-processing, extraction settings, task text, tokenization, statistical assumptions, file inventory, and the suppositions linking each measured quantity to what it stands for.** | `docs/rigor_audit.md` (12 numbered findings, each recomputed from raw bytes / the pinned model source / the code); `src/traj_geom/metrics/regime.py`, `src/traj_geom/provenance.py`, `scripts/backfill_provenance.py`; `tests/test_regime.py` (9), `tests/test_audit_findings.py` (11), `tests/test_provenance.py` (8) | **Verified-live, 2026-07-25. Four findings invalidate prior claims — including three of my own from earlier the same day — and two correct the record in the project's favour.** **(1) Compute is bfloat16, and the "limit set" is arithmetic.** `load_huginn` defaults to `torch.bfloat16`; verified in the data itself — every stored value is *exactly* bf16-representable (round-trip max deviation `0.000e+00`). bf16 relative precision is `2^-8≈3.9e-3`, not fp32's `1.2e-7`. One rounding vector has norm `0.086`. **[CORRECTED in the second audit pass]** the stationary floor for isotropic noise under contraction is `η/√(1−ρ²)`, NOT `η/(1−ρ)` — random directions add in quadrature, not linearly (variance recursion `E‖e‖²=ρ²E‖e‖²+η²`).
