Correct prediction **0.175** vs observed **0.911** = ratio **5.2×**; simulation at ρ=0.871 gives 0.171, confirming the formula. **The originally-reported 0.668 / ratio-1.36 'parameter-free prediction' is RETRACTED.** The floor is set by the slowest modes (`η/√(1−ρ²)` = 0.61 at ρ=0.99, 0.86 at ρ=0.995) and by many rounding events per unroll, not one. **[CLOSED QUANTITATIVELY 2026-07-26]** the residual 5.2× is `η` being a per-UNROLL injection over many arithmetic sites, not one rounding event: an unroll is the adapter + 4 `SandwichBlock`s, each ~10 rounded operations (norms, QKV/out projections, softmax, GatedMLP), so ~40 sites; independent errors add in quadrature, `η ≈ √k·η₁`. Inverting the observed floor gives **k ≈ 27**, squarely in the architectural range. Corrected model: **floor = √k·η₁/√(1−ρ²)**, no free parameters beyond a site count readable from the model definition — a stronger result than the original claim, which got a plausible number from the wrong formula. Secondary and measured: ρ is mode-dependent (**0.73–1.02** across the gap's top 8 principal directions), which raises the floor but is not the main term. The arithmetic conclusion now rests on isotropy AND a quantitative magnitude match. Corroborated by isotropy: tail PCA top-2 explains only **8.8–10.7%**, participation ratio **18–19/20**. **This kills the "≈14-step oscillatory mode"** (DMD was fitting a linear operator to isotropic rounding noise) and every tail-restricted statistic. Falsifiable: one fp32 rerun should shrink the floor by `2^-16` to `≈1.4e-5`. **(2) `consecutive_step_cosine` reports a property of rounding noise.** It averages the *second half*; paths settle at t≈14, so for ns=128 that window is entirely post-floor. By regime: **converging t=0..14 → +0.084**, post-floor t=64..127 → **−0.331** (white-noise increments have lag-1 autocorrelation exactly −0.5). **The project-wide reading "cos<0 ⇒ the path zig-zags inward" is wrong — in the computing regime the path glides.** This retroactively removes the motivating evidence for `winding_variants`' W7/W8/W9 (the estimators remain valid; the claim that reversal occurs in Huginn does not). **(3) `steps_to_settle` measures ρ, not effective compute.** It thresholds at 10% of the *maximum* step, always the h_0 transient, making it a function of the contraction rate alone: `log(0.1)/log(0.8407) = 13.27` predicted vs **13.84** observed over 140 trajectories — a prediction using no task information. This is the exact failure mode the audit was asked to hunt (*X resembles class Y, so Y2 is used to estimate Y1, without checking Y1↔Y2 holds for the actual X*), and it explains the metric's persistently tiny variance. **(4) `full_synthetic_experiments.csv` is NOT the banked trajectories.** **0/80** rows reproduce at either num_steps; CSV |winding| std **1.303** vs banked **0.039**; CSV `steps_settle`=128 everywhere vs recomputed **9–19**. D22's null test was run on the `.npy` files and applied to claims resting on the CSVs — that inference is unsupported. **(5) `init{N}` is the TASK seed, not the init seed.** `make_count_ones_task` derives the bit string from it; proven by collision — seeds 1 and 2 give the same n_ops=2 prompt and their files are **bit-identical**. So `results/trajectories/` holds **zero** multi-init data. (This cost the audit one wrong conclusion — a "Lyapunov ≈ 0, the map does not contract" reading — before the collision test caught it.) **(6) `path_independence`/`convergence_rate` fit through the floor**, understating contraction by **+0.084/step** (x0.9244 whole-range vs x0.8407 early-region); biases all 600 `lyap` values in `dissoc_multiinit.csv`. **(7) H3's premise HOLDS — ρ≈0.84.** Measured correctly from two orbits of the *same prompt, different h_0* using the top-level multi-init paths: converged (ns≥64) groups give ρ **0.85–0.90**, residual gap floor **1.41** (vs the corrected bf16 prediction of 0.175 at that ρ — see the retraction in (1); the gap floor is set by the slowest modes). Three earlier numbers retired: **0.871** (distance to a trajectory's *own* endpoint — near-tautological), **1.000** (measured across *different prompts* — wrong data, see (5)), **0.9244** (floor-diluted). Different inits converge to the same state up to arithmetic precision, **confirming** Geiping et al.'s path-independence. Contraction shows no relation to difficulty (ρ vs n_ops rank-corr **+0.224**, N=5). **Scoping correction: contraction forbids an *unbounded* register, not a *bounded* counter** — the converged neighbourhood spans ~1.4 across 5279 tangent dimensions, ample to separate 64 states. Directly favourable to Barannikov's m=64 Task a/b. **(8) The state is confined to a sphere.** `SandwichBlock.forward` L535 ends in RMSNorm and the hook sits on `core_block[-1]`, so `‖h_t‖ = 76.37 ± 0.007` (**0.0073%** variation). Radius carries no information; `winding_of`'s Euclidean centroid is **off-manifold** (74.20 vs 76.37); and `matched_random_walk` diffuses to **154.76** (5.86% deviation), so **every null test in the project compares an on-manifold path against off-manifold surrogates.** **(9) PCA was nondeterministic** — no `random_state` at any of 4 sites, and sklearn picks randomized SVD for [128,5280]. Severity measured and **benign**: ≤`7.4e-7` relative spread, PC2/PC3 gap ≥0.33 on all 140 (plane never flips). Explains three distinct winding values for bit-identical inputs in the CSV. Fixed with `svd_solver="full"` (exact, deterministic, 45 ms). Sign handling was already correct — every call site wraps `abs(...)`. **(10) Accuracy and geometry use different compute budgets**: `counting_correct` runs `generate_with_adaptive_compute` at `num_steps=32` *with early exit*, geometry uses fixed 16/64/128. The correctness figures and the geometry figures are not directly comparable. **(11) `configs/default.yaml` is stale** — parsed by nothing (its two referents, `extract.py`/`run_mvp.py`, are `TODO` stubs) yet specifies `num_steps: 32` and a pararule dataset matching no experiment in `results/`. **(12) ns=16 groups are not converged** (final inter-orbit gap 6.5–7.1 vs 1.2–1.6 at ns=64); `convergence.csv` includes them. **CROSS-CUTTING.** Findings 1, 2, 3 and 6 are one error in four places: **a statistic averaged over a window that mixes the signal regime with the noise regime.** Trajectories carry ~15 informative steps followed by 50–115 steps of arithmetic. **Remedy shipped**: `metrics/regime.py` locates the floor empirically (not from the bf16 prediction, which would be circular — it needs ρ) and provides floor-aware replacements; validated on real data (cosine **−0.3225 → +0.1390**, contraction bias **−0.068/step** removed). Existing metrics were left bit-compatible so cached CSVs stay reproducible; their bias is documented rather than silently changed. **Provenance remedy**: `provenance.py` writes a sha256'd JSON sidecar + append-only `manifest.jsonl` per artifact, with `task_seed` and `init_seed` as *separate required* fields and a shared `run_id` linking artifacts from one invocation — the single field whose absence made (4) invisible. All **155** existing artifacts backfilled with VERIFIED-vs-RECOVERED evidence levels; 155/155 hashes verify. **What survives unchanged:** the transient (t≲15) is real signal far above the floor and has been systematically under-analysed; prompt information persists indefinitely (different prompts stay **43.1** apart, 33° of arc, flat to 4 decimals over 120 steps); sign handling, the N=4 Spearman fix, and the length-confound documentation are all correct as written. |
| D25 | **Second audit pass: an arithmetic error of mine retracted, the winding null REVERSED under a valid surrogate, H1 found to have never been tested, and two literature attributions broken.** *(The winding reversal recorded here was itself underpowered and is superseded by D28, which ran the calibrated on-manifold null at full scale; the rotation phenomenon is separately restored by D55.)* | `docs/rigor_audit.md` §§16–20; `src/traj_geom/metrics/surrogate.py`, `regime.classify_shape_regime`, `regime.h1_persistence_signal`; `tests/test_surrogate.py` (14), extended `test_regime.py` (15), `test_eval_depth.py` (23). Suite 145 passed. External primary-source survey (`files/ai_docs/Adjudicating Rotation and Winding Estimation…`) used as an adversarial check on D24 and read critically. | **Verified-live, 2026-07-25.** **(1) RETRACTION — my own noise-floor arithmetic was wrong.** D24(1) reported `η/(1−ρ) = 0.668` against observed 0.911, "ratio 1.36, a parameter-free prediction". That formula sums *aligned* displacements. Isotropic rounding adds in **quadrature**: the variance recursion `E‖e‖²=ρ²E‖e‖²+η²` gives stationary RMS `η/√(1−ρ²)` = **0.175**, so the true ratio is **5.2×**, not 1.36×. Confirmed by direct simulation of `e_{t+1}=ρe_t+ξ_t` in 5280 dims (ρ=0.871 → simulated **0.171**). Caught by an external survey and then verified independently rather than accepted. The bf16-floor **conclusion survives** but now rests on ISOTROPY (tail PCA top-2 8.8–10.7%, participation ratio 18–19/20, where 2-D dynamics would give ~2) rather than on magnitude; the floor is set by the slowest-contracting modes (`η/√(1−ρ²)` = 0.61 at ρ=0.99). **(2) THE WINDING NULL: METHOD FIXED, RESULT RETRACTED AS UNDERPOWERED.** [CORRECTED same day, before any downstream use] This entry first claimed the null *reverses*. It does not support that: an 8-trajectory follow-up restricted to num_steps=128 gave a mean excess of **−0.0087**, the OPPOSITE sign to the +0.0031 below, and all three samples (47×40, 20×40, 8×12) are underpowered and unstratified by `num_steps` — which D24(12) already showed matters. **The sign of the winding effect is UNKNOWN pending `scripts/run_manifold_null.py`** (140 trajectories × 100 surrogates, calibration arm, stratified). What survives regardless of sign: **D22's headline rests on an invalid off-manifold null, so that evidence is deleted, not reversed.** Original underpowered numbers, retained for the record: Replacing the off-manifold surrogate (D24(8)) with one preserving the sphere, the radial convergence profile and every consecutive angular step — randomising only rotational freedom, via the spherical law of cosines which fixes `q_t·q_{t+1}` exactly — flips the result: **beats null 8.5% (old) → 66.0% (new)**, mean z **−9.64 → +5.86** (47 trajectories × 40 surrogates). Invariants verified to 1e-14 / 7e-6 / 1e-8 **uniformly across dimension 8→5280**. **Self-calibrated**: feeding a surrogate back in as data, surrogates beat their own null **0/20** against ~5% expected, while real trajectories beat theirs 55% (mean effect +0.0031) — so the excess is not a construction artifact. **D22's headline ("82.1% wind LESS than their null, median z=−5.61") rests on a straw-man null and cannot be cited** — but the replacement result is not yet established either. **CAVEAT, load-bearing:** the effect is small (+0.003 on ~0.64) and this null draws azimuths isotropically, so it does NOT control tangent-space anisotropy. Elsayed & Cunningham (*Nat. Neurosci.* 20:1310, 2017) showed exactly this failure mode for jPCA — apparent rotation fully reproduced by Tensor-Maximum-Entropy surrogates preserving only marginal covariances. Until a covariance-preserving null is run, read this as "more than an isotropic on-manifold null", NOT as evidence of a rotational generator. **(3) H1 WAS NEVER TESTED.** `shapes.gate.classify_shape` returns **"settle" for 140/140** banked trajectories because it compares the last step to the LARGEST step: `s[-1]/s.max()` has max **0.0188** across all 140 against a 0.10 threshold — five times under. "loop" and "drift" are **unreachable**; an instrument with one attainable output cannot test a three-way hypothesis. Its loop branch is independently wrong: the minimum far-pair distance scales like `min_lag × spacing`, so it labels a straight line, a random walk, AND a decaying spiral all "loop" (verified). Fixed by `regime.classify_shape_regime` — settling judged against the noise floor, loops by **chord-to-arc ratio** (exactly 1.0 for a line, →0 for a closed orbit, invariant to sampling density); discriminates all three labels on constructed cases. On real data it still says "settle", but now because paths demonstrably reach their floor within budget. **(4) `h1_persistence` normalises by the transient.** 84–85% of each point cloud is the noise ball (107–109 of 128 points); features live in a ball of diameter 9–15 while the normaliser is the cloud diameter ~93, so the reported **max persistence 0.009** ≈ (noise-ball scale)/(transient extent). `regime.h1_persistence_signal` restricts to the signal regime, but T≪n remains fatal for full-state PH — Perea & Harer require a **scalar observable**, and delay-embedding the full state creates no new information. **(5) TWO CITATIONS BROKEN.** Pappone et al. (arXiv:2509.23314) studies a **GPT-2-scale model, not Huginn**, defines DLR differently from `convergence.drift_to_loop_ratio`, and reports consecutive-step cosine at **+0.5 to +0.65 — positive**, the opposite sign to the −0.276 attributed to it and independently corroborating D24(2). **"Movahedi et al." could not be located at all.** Both corrected in `convergence.py`. **(6) Tokenization, refined.** P(answer) is spread over **disjoint** surface forms — `' 2'`=[402] and `'2'`=[50] are distinct atomic tokens, neither a prefix of the other — so summing is valid and not double-counting; `eval_depth.depth_curve` now marginalises via log-sum-exp. Verified against the real cached tokenizer: variants always share token length (differ only in the first token), so marginalising costs one extra pass and no positional bookkeeping. Magnitude is small (~3.5% of mass, ~0.034 nats). **The larger residual issue, documented not fixed:** teacher-forced scoring measures P(answer is a **prefix** of the continuation), not P(answer exactly) — for gold "2" the model could continue "25". Gold and distractor are affected similarly so the margin is robust, but the absolute log-probs are upper bounds. **(7) No injected noise:** `RavenConfig.test_time_noise = 0` and nothing overrides it, so with h_0 fixed (D24(5)) and bf16 confirmed, **rounding is the only noise source** — which is what makes the floor argument a genuine prediction. **(8) `burn=4` costs 21% of the signal:** the converging regime averages 19.2 steps, so `winding_of` computes a rotation statistic from ~15 informative steps diluted by ~109 steps of rounding. |
| D26 | **H2 tested without any surrogate model, converged-state answer-clustering tested, the last two unaudited metrics closed, and a derivation of what the norm constraint permits a counting register to be.** | `scripts/run_winding_permutation.py` -> `results/winding_permutation.csv` (provenance-tagged); `docs/register_geometry.md`; `docs/backlog_not_done.md` | **Verified-live, 2026-07-26.** **(1) H2 IS NULL UNDER THE ASSUMPTION-FREE TEST.** Permuting `n_ops` labels among *real* trajectories needs no surrogate at all — the data already carries the manifold, the convergence profile, the noise floor, the anisotropy and the bf16 quantisation, and permutation destroys only the winding↔difficulty association. Stratified by (task, num_steps), 20 000 permutations each: `count_ones` ns=128 ρ=+0.786 p=0.0267; `projection` ns=64 ρ=−0.600 p=0.237; `projection` ns=128 ρ=+0.452 p=0.269; `count_ones` ns=64 ρ=+0.143 p=0.807. **No stratum survives BH** (smallest needs p≤0.0125) **or Bonferroni** (0.107), and P(≥1 nominal hit among 4 under the global null) = **0.185** — one hit is exactly what chance gives. Signs also disagree across strata. **Interpretation ceiling, stated deliberately:** because rank-corr(n_ops, seq_len) = 1.000 for every generator (D25/§21), this design can *falsify* H2 but never confirm it; the asymmetry is why it is worth running. **(2) Converged states do NOT cluster by answer.** Within a difficulty level (so prompt length is fixed), the geodesic angle between converged states of same-answer versus different-answer prompts: mean effect **−0.12°**, with 1/13 strata nominally significant against 0.7 expected. A first pass gave +4.17° — that was an **artifact of the n_ops=2 prompt collisions** (identical prompts → bit-identical states → 0.00°, a tautology), excluded here. **CAVEAT: near-zero power** — most strata have only 1–3 same-answer pairs, so this is "no evidence", not "evidence of absence", and a linear probe could still succeed where nearest-neighbour geometry fails. **(3) The last two unaudited metrics DO inherit the regime error.** `mean_normed_accel`: full-path 0.7804 vs signal-regime **0.6611** (+18%). `drift_to_loop_ratio`: full-path 20.667 vs signal-only **3.887** — **inflated 5.32×**, because its denominator (mean step) is dominated by noise-floor steps while its numerator is not. The metric's stated reading ("≫1 = a transient arc to a far point, not a loop") is therefore partly manufactured by arithmetic. **(4) `make_three_scale*` are the designed length-control exception**, not an oversight: they take `active_len`/`irrelevant_len`/`neutral_len` explicitly rather than a single `n_ops`, which is why they were unreachable by the generator sweep. D20's residual confound there still stands. **(5) A TRANSLATION REGISTER IS ARCHITECTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE; A ROTATION ONE IS NOT.** RMSNorm confines states to a shell of relative half-thickness δ≈7.3e-5 about R=76.37. A displacement changes the norm by `v_∥ + |v_⊥|²/(2R)`, so a straight-line register is bounded by `D < R√(2δ) = 0.923` **total**, i.e. **0.0144 per increment** over Barannikov's m=64 — **62× below the bf16 noise floor (≈0.9)**. The bound scales as √δ, so it survives a **1000×** underestimate of the shell (at δ=7.3e-2 it is still 2× below the floor). Rotation costs nothing in norm: a register spanning 30–70° of arc gives 0.63–1.46 chord per increment, at or above the floor. **CONSEQUENCES:** (a) Task a/b should be probed for a fixed-angle **rotation** in a 2-plane, or equivalently a translation in **tangent/log-map coordinates** — which *rescues* Barannikov's framing rather than contradicting it, since the two agree at small angle; a linear probe in raw ambient coordinates read as translation is what fails. (b) If the register is a rotation, winding across **token positions** is proportional to the count — H2 in the right index, whereas this project has only ever measured winding across *unrolls at the answer token*, where the state merely converges. (c) **Task b's balancedness closes the curve**: depth returns to 0, so the state returns to its start and the winding number becomes a genuine element of π₁(ℝ²∖{p})≅ℤ — an integer invariant, curing the open-arc defect that made every winding number in this project a non-invariant real number. This predicts **quantization**, a far stronger test than any correlation. All of §5 is a statement about what the architecture permits, **not** an empirical finding that Huginn counts; the tests are backlog items 1.11–1.13 and are unrun. |
| D27 | **The project's first positive signal about computation (SUGGESTIVE, NOT ESTABLISHED): the answer is linearly decodable from the converged state at fixed prompt length — for one task of two.** | `scripts/run_answer_probe.py` -> `results/answer_probe.csv` (provenance-tagged); ridge-penalty sweep + within-level permutation null + leave-one-level-out | **Verified-live, 2026-07-26. Reported with its caveats because a positive result deserves more scrutiny than a null, not less.** **THE NAIVE VERSION IS WORTHLESS.** A probe on raw converged states gives LOO R²=**0.94** (count_ones) / **0.81** (projection), p<0.005 — but the answer grows with `n_ops` and rank-corr(n_ops, seq_len)=1.000 (D25/§21), so this may simply read prompt length off the state. **THE CONTROLLED VERSION.** Asking whether the state predicts the answer's variation *within* a difficulty level (seq_len constant), with target and features residualised on level: **count_ones R² = +0.205/+0.210/+0.219 at ridge α = 1e1/1e2/1e3, permutation p = 0.020/0.005/0.030**; `projection` null throughout (R² < 0 at every α, p ≥ 0.665). Permutation shuffles answers *within* level, preserving the confound and destroying only the within-level association. **A LEAKAGE BUG WAS CAUGHT AND FIXED HERE.** Residualising with level means computed over the whole dataset makes the test vacuous: within a level of size k the residuals sum to zero, so a held-out residual is exactly determined by the other k−1, and any model that can identify the level reconstructs it. That gave LOO R²≈0.95 for **real and permuted labels alike** — the tell being a permutation null scoring +0.946, which is impossible. Level means are now fitted inside each fold; null means are −0.69…−0.001 as they should be. The script's docstring documents the trap. **WHY THIS IS NOT A FINDING.** (a) **Fragile**: leave-one-level-out drops R² from +0.219 to **−0.009** when `n_ops=24` alone is removed (it stays +0.08…+0.29 for the other seven). (b) **Small**: N=38, modest effect. (c) **Selection**: five α values were swept, so quoting the minimum p=0.005 is selection-biased; the defensible statement is that p<0.05 at *every* α in 1e1–1e3, and Bonferroni over 2 tasks × 5 α gives 0.05 — exactly borderline. (d) **One task of two**, with no account of why `projection` differs. **RELATION TO D26(2).** That entry reports converged states do *not* cluster by answer geometrically. No contradiction: a nearest-neighbour test with 1–3 same-answer pairs per stratum has near-zero power and only sees information visible as proximity, while a linear probe can find a direction carrying the answer amid other variance. D26(2) should be read as "no evidence", which it already says. **WHAT IT WOULD MEAN IF IT REPLICATES.** That the converged answer-token state — the very state this project characterised as "merely converging", where all geometric metrics are dominated by the convergence — nonetheless carries answer information at fixed prompt length. That is the first evidence here of the *computation* rather than of the settling. It needs a properly powered replication (more seeds per level) before it is anything more. |
| D28 | **RESOLVED: there is no rotational signal in winding. Under an unbiased on-manifold null the effect flips sign with `num_steps` — a recording-budget parameter — in both tasks.** This closes the project's central metric question. | `scripts/run_manifold_null.py` -> `results/manifold_null.csv` (provenance-tagged); 140 trajectories × 100 surrogates × 2 arms | **Verified-live, 2026-07-26. Supersedes D22 and the retracted D25(2).** **THE NULL IS UNBIASED.** A calibration arm feeds a surrogate back in as though it were data; an unbiased procedure must beat its own null at the nominal rate. Observed: **6.4%** against 5% nominal, mean z **+0.03**, mean effect **+7.6e-5**. So the real arm is interpretable — unlike every previous winding null in this project, none of which was ever calibrated. **THE EFFECT HAS NO CONSISTENT SIGN.** Real arm pooled: 62.1% beat the null, mean z +1.51. Stratified by `num_steps`: **ns=64 → +0.0127 (mean z +10.97, n=60); ns=128 → −0.0091 (mean z −5.58, n=80)**. The flip holds in **both** tasks independently — count_ones +0.0149 (z +13.35) → −0.0150 (z −8.92); projection +0.0104 (z +8.59) → −0.0032 (z −2.24) — and the strata differ at Mann-Whitney **p = 1e-11**. The pooled figure is an average over two cancelling strata, which is why the pooled effect is +0.0002, i.e. zero. **WHY.** `num_steps` is a *recording budget*, not a property of the model or the prompt. The same prompts and the same weights, recorded longer, reverse the comparison. What changes is how much arithmetic-noise regime enters the statistic: ns=64 carries ~50 post-floor steps, ns=128 ~110. **VERDICT: |winding| does not measure rotation in Huginn's trajectories; its comparison to any null is governed by how much noise regime was recorded.** D22's headline ("82.1% wind LESS than their null") is **deleted** — its null was off-manifold — and nothing replaces it. Combined with D26(1) (winding does not track difficulty under an assumption-free permutation test) and D24(3) (steps_to_settle measures ρ), **the project's three answer-token geometric metrics are now all accounted for**. This is also the cleanest demonstration of the audit's central theme: a statistic averaged over a window mixing signal with arithmetic reports the window. **Method note:** this result was reached only because an underpowered version claiming the opposite (D25(2), 47×40 unstratified) was retracted rather than published; the full run needed 140×100×2 and stratification by a variable D24(12) had already flagged as mattering. |
| D29 | **Plan Step 5 executed: the invalidated metrics re-derived floor-aware wherever raw paths survive — and the sign of `cos` is shown to be set by the RECORDING LENGTH, on the project's own data.** | `scripts/run_regime_rederivation.py` -> `results/regime_rederivation.csv` (provenance-tagged); 155 trajectories | **Verified-live, 2026-07-26.** **(1) SIGNAL FRACTION SHRINKS WITH RECORDING LENGTH.** Mean converging-regime length is ~19 steps regardless of budget, so the fraction of a recording that is signal is **52% at ns=16, 30% at ns=64, 15% at ns=128**. Recording longer does not add information; it adds arithmetic. Every whole-path average is therefore progressively more contaminated the longer the run. **(2) THE HEADLINE — `cos` tracks the recording budget, not the model.** Legacy `consecutive_step_cosine` (second-half average): **+0.258 at ns=16, −0.324 at ns=64, −0.368 at ns=128**. At ns=16 the path has not reached the floor, so the second half is still signal and the cosine is *positive*; once the budget is long enough to include the floor it goes negative. Restricted to the converging regime: **+0.305 (ns=64), +0.093 (ns=128)**, and **140/155 = 90.3%** of trajectories flip from negative to positive. This reproduces D24(2) independently, from `convergence.csv`'s own ns=16 vs ns=64 rows, and it is the same structural result as D28 for winding: **a statistic whose value is set by how long you recorded is not measuring the computation.** **(3) MAGNITUDE OF THE CORRECTIONS.** `conv_rate` legacy **−0.0501** vs regime **−0.1691** — the legacy fit through the floor understates contraction by a factor of **3.2**. `drift_to_loop_ratio` legacy **+16.92** vs regime **+4.19** — inflated **4.04×**, so its stated reading ("≫1 = a transient arc, not a loop") is substantially manufactured by dividing by noise-floor steps. **(4) CAVEAT ON THE CORRECTED METRIC.** The regime-restricted cosine is itself not constant across budgets (+0.305 / +0.093 / −0.0001 at ns=64/128/16); at ns=16 only ~8 signal steps remain, too few for a stable estimate, and the ns=64 and ns=128 groups differ in composition. The robust claim is the *sign* and the *direction* of the correction, not a single corrected value. **(5) WHAT CANNOT BE RE-DERIVED, AND WHY THAT IS ITSELF A FINDING.** Only `convergence.csv` and `homology.csv` have surviving raw paths (verified: legacy metrics reproduce from `trajectories/` to the 4 decimals the CSVs store, rank-corr +0.999/+1.000/+1.000 — an initial "0/15 reproduce" was my own tolerance error, not a data problem). **16 of the 18 invalidated results files have no raw paths on disk**, so their conclusions cannot be corrected without a GPU re-run. The project stored derived scalars and discarded the data they came from; that is the concrete cost of the provenance gap D24(4)/D24(5) documents. |
| D30 | **GATE PASSED: the post-convergence residual IS arithmetic — confirmed on real GPU across three dtypes. And a second, larger finding: bf16 rounding makes Huginn appear to converge ~4.6x sooner than it does.** | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-precision-gate` (self-contained, log in `scratch/kaggle_precision_gate/out/`); 3 prompts x 3 dtypes | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-07-26. This is `docs/plan_forward.md` Step 1, the gate for everything downstream.** **(1) THE GATE.** Residual radius after convergence, same prompts, same seed, only the compute dtype varying: **bfloat16 (8 mantissa bits) 1.1895; float16 (11 bits) 0.1548; float32 (24 bits) 0.000290**. The design was strengthened from the planned single bf16/fp32 ratio to a three-point regression, so the prediction is a *slope*, not a number: if the residual is rounding noise then `residual ∝ ulp = 2^-mantissa_bits`, i.e. slope −1 in log2. **Observed bf16→fp16: 7.7x against 8x predicted, slope −0.981 — a 4% match.** **(2) WHY THE fp32 POINT IS SHALLOWER (and why that is not a contradiction).** bf16→fp32 gives 4099x against 65536x predicted. The reason is visible in the same table: `regime_end` jumps from **20.7 (bf16) to 32.3 (fp16) to 96.3 (fp32)** out of 127. In fp32 the path is **still converging at step 96** and never reaches its floor inside the budget, so the measured fp32 "residual" is still-decaying transient — an *upper bound* on the true floor, which necessarily flattens the fitted slope. The clean segment (bf16→fp16, both of which do converge in budget) matches the prediction almost exactly. **(3) THE BIGGER FINDING — "settling" is itself a precision artifact.** The project's central structural fact, that trajectories settle at t≈14–21, holds only in bfloat16. In float32 the same prompts keep converging to **t≈96**, roughly **4.6x longer**. So bf16 rounding noise does not merely add a floor: it *masks ongoing computation*, cutting the informative regime short. Implications: (a) D24(3)'s "`steps_to_settle` is a proxy for ρ" sharpens to "a proxy for where the bf16 floor truncates convergence"; (b) the ~15-step signal regime that motivates `metrics/regime.py` is a bf16 quantity, and in higher precision there is **5x more signal to analyse**; (c) it bears directly on Huginn's own test-time-compute scaling — unrolls past ~20 do little in bf16 but demonstrably still move the state in fp32, which is a testable claim about the published architecture, not just about our metrics. **(4) TWO INDEPENDENT CORROBORATIONS in the same run.** `bf16_exact` was True for the bfloat16 run and False for the other two — validating the dtype-detection method used throughout the audit (D24(1)). And the state norm is **76.34–76.37 in all three dtypes**, so the sphere (D24(8)) is architectural, not a precision artifact. **CONSEQUENCE: `metrics/regime.py`, rigor_audit §§1–3 and §6, and D24/D25/D29 are all confirmed rather than needing re-derivation. Steps 2–4 are justified to proceed.** |
| D31 | **ρ(∂ₕR) measured EXACTLY on real Huginn for the first time — the quantity A2/D21 record as never measured. The recurrent map is dominated by ROTATING modes. And my own log-spiral rotation prediction is falsified.** | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-jacobian-spectrum` v2 (log in `scratch/kaggle_jacobian/out/`); implicitly-restarted Arnoldi on autodiff Jacobian-vector products, float32, 3 prompts | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-07-26.** `docs/plan_forward.md` Step 2. **(1) H3's PREMISE CONFIRMED EXACTLY: ρ = 0.7935 / 0.8042 / 0.8083** at n_ops = 64 / 32 / 8 (mean **0.8020**). This is the first exact measurement of the contraction rate in this project — every prior figure fitted a statistic to an orbit. It is also **independent corroboration of D24(7)**, whose two-orbit estimate of 0.84–0.90 came from entirely different data by an entirely different method. And ρ varies by under 2% across an 8× range of difficulty, confirming D24(7)'s null (contraction does not track difficulty) from an exact method. **(2) THE MAP IS DOMINATED BY ROTATION.** The leading eigenvalue is **complex in 3/3 prompts**, and the top eigenvalues come in **complex-conjugate pairs** (0.808, 0.808, 0.802, 0.802, 0.775, 0.775 …) with **8–10 oscillatory modes** each. So rotation is genuinely present *in the recurrent map* — which is notable given D28 found no rotational signal in the trajectories' winding. Those are consistent: the map rotates, but the observable this project built to detect it does not see it. **(3) MY PREDICTION IS FALSIFIED, and the diagnosis matters more than the retraction.** `plan_forward` Step 2 predicted |arg λ| ≤ 0.78 rad from the log-polar slope. Measured: **1.945 rad (111°/step)**, 2.5× larger. Two causes, both real: **(a) I measured the wrong operator.** Perturbing and reading the last position gives the DIAGONAL BLOCK `J_TT`, but the answer-token state also evolves under attention from every other position, so the orbit obeys the full coupled operator. The evidence is internal: `J_TT`'s ρ=0.802 decays *faster* than the observed orbit decay of 0.84–0.90, which is impossible if `J_TT` governed the orbit, since the leading eigenvalue is by definition the slowest mode. **(b) I asserted an upper bound I had not proved** — the log-polar θ was cumulative *unsigned* azimuth of the direction `q_t`, not signed rotation in a fixed plane, and those are not related by the inequality I claimed. **(4) TECHNICAL NOTE, recorded because it will recur.** Forward-mode AD is unusable here: torch's efficient SDPA kernel has no forward-AD rule, and forcing the MATH backend OOMs on a T4. The kernel falls back to **reverse mode**, which is exact for this purpose since `eig(Jᵀ) = eig(J)` — only eigenvectors differ, and they are not used. Cost: 498–1786 matvecs per prompt. **NEXT:** measure the FULL operator (all positions perturbed and read) rather than the diagonal block, which is the version whose spectrum should match the observed orbit decay. |
| D32 | **Barannikov's Task a and Task b, run: the register content IS linearly decodable from the latents for both — but the winding readout shows NO quantisation, so a rotation register is not supported.** | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-register-probe` v2, float32, m=64 fixed, 12 seeds/condition (log in `scratch/kaggle_register/out/`) | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-07-26.** `docs/plan_forward.md` Step 4. **(1) TASK A — POSITIVE, position-controlled.** The raw probe for the running count `y_i` gives LOO R² = **+0.892** — and that number is worthless, because `R²(y_i ~ i) = 0.985`: position alone explains 98.5% of a running count, position is trivially decodable from positional embeddings, and indeed the probe scores R² = **+0.898 for position itself**. The meaningful quantity is `y_i` *after removing its linear dependence on position*: **R² = +0.601** on residuals of sd 1.06 counts. So the latent tracks deviations of about ±1 from what position alone predicts — evidence that a running count is maintained, not merely that the model knows where it is in the string. **Leakage caveat, quantified:** the position fit used all 64 points, so a held-out residual is mildly informed by the others — measured at **max 0.196 counts, 18.5% of a 1.06 sd**. This is NOT the exact determination that invalidated the earlier answer probe (D27), where k=5 level means made residuals sum to zero; the result stands with a mildly optimistic number. **(2) TASK B — POSITIVE, and cleaner than Task a.** Nesting depth is decodable at **R² = +0.590 (balanced) / +0.718 (unbalanced)**. This needs no residualisation because depth is a *bridge* — it starts and ends at 0 — so **`R²(depth ~ position) = 0.160`** for balanced strings against the probe's 0.590. Position is not the explanation here. **(3) NO QUANTISATION — the rotation-register prediction FAILS.** `docs/register_geometry.md` argued that a balanced string closes the curve, making position-indexed winding a genuine integer invariant, and predicted near-integer values. Measured distance to the nearest integer: **balanced 0.281, unbalanced 0.353**. The pre-registered test (balanced closer than the control) passes at p=0.0005 — **but that verdict is misleading and I am overriding it**: against the actual no-quantisation reference (uniform winding gives mean distance 0.250), balanced strings are **not** closer (one-sided p = **0.9954**). The p=0.0005 is driven by *unbalanced* strings being anomalously **far** from integers, not by balanced ones being close. **My pre-registration was inadequate** — it specified the comparison against the control but not against the null, and a control comparison can pass for the wrong reason. **(4) WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE CURATOR'S QUESTION.** The register *content* is present and linearly decodable in both tasks — an affirmative answer to "is the count/depth represented in the latents". But the specific geometric realisation this project proposed (a rotation, read off as an integer winding number) is **not supported**: winding shows no quantisation even where the curve should close. Consistent with D28 (no rotational signal in winding) and D31 (rotation *is* present in the recurrent map's spectrum) — the map rotates, the register is decodable, and winding sees neither. The honest synthesis: **probe the latents linearly; do not read the register off a winding number.** |
| D33 | **H2 restated behaviourally: required recurrent depth DOES scale with difficulty (rho=+0.626, p=0.0014) — but on a length-confounded task, exactly as pre-registered. Plus an intuitive-level finding: extra depth is not used productively.** | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-depth-threshold` (log in `scratch/kaggle_depth/out/`); float32, teacher-forced margin, 33 instances | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-07-26.** `docs/plan_forward.md` Step 3. **(1) THE INTUITIVE OBSERVATION FIRST.** Before any threshold statistic: does more depth help at all? Mean margin **+0.555 at unroll 1**, **+1.502 at its maximum**, **+0.153 at the end**, and it increased from first to last unroll in only **14/33** instances. So the model's preference for the correct answer **peaks mid-unroll and then decays** — extra recurrent depth is not used productively on this task. That is a plain, statistics-free fact worth stating before any test. **(2) THE TEST, and a power mistake of mine.** I first correlated *level means* of r* against n_ops: rho=+0.714, p=0.111, N=6 levels — reported as "no evidence". That was wrong, for a reason the project's own critical-value table makes obvious: at N=6 significance needs rho≥0.886, and the per-level counts were **[3,6,5,1,2,6]** — one level had a single instance. Correlating the **23 individual instances** instead: **rho = +0.626, p = 0.0014**. The level-mean convention threw away most of the power. Mean r* rises 12.0 → 10.0 → 9.8 → 20.0 → 17.5 → 25.5 across n_ops 2→64. **(3) THE CEILING, PRE-REGISTERED AND NOW BINDING.** `scripts/run_depth_threshold.py`'s docstring states this design "can falsify H2 but cannot confirm it", because `count_ones` has rank-corr(n_ops, seq_len) = **exactly 1.000** (D25/§21). So r* scaling with n_ops is **indistinguishable from r* scaling with prompt length**. This is a real positive on the depth axis and it is **not** yet evidence about computation. **(4) THE FIX IS RUNNING.** `arsen4ikvar/geometry-depth-fixed-length` re-runs the identical measurement on Barannikov's design — **m=64 fixed**, difficulty varied by the number of ones via p(1) ∈ {0.15,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.85}, 40 seeds each — so every prompt has the same token count and only the answer varies. The kernel asserts and prints the set of distinct prompt token lengths; a single value means the confound is broken by construction. That version can confirm H2, not merely fail to falsify it. |
| D34 | **SUPERSEDED BY D50 (2026-08-04): the direction is the current-token contrast and `register_r` is a window artefact. The register claim survives; this measurement of it does not.** **Barannikov's T_1 found directly: a single fixed direction v, obtained by a mean difference, carries the RUNNING COUNT beyond both position and current-token identity. 16/16 strings agree in sign, p=9.2e-10.** | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-position-states` (raw per-position latents dumped once, then analysed offline); 16 independent 64-symbol strings, float32 compute | **Verified-live, 2026-07-26. Prompted by the note that an intuitive observation can usefully precede a complex test — this needed no probe, no cross-validation and no surrogate.** **THE CONSTRUCTION.** For each string, take per-position states, form the step `Δ_i = h_{i+1} − h_i`, and set `v = mean(Δ | bit=1) − mean(Δ | bit=0)`. That is literally "what direction does seeing a 1 move the state in" — Barannikov's `T_1`, as a subtraction. **(1) v IS SHARED ACROSS STRINGS.** Pairwise cosine between the `v` derived independently from 16 different strings: **mean +0.9199** (sd 0.035, min +0.785). Random directions in 5280 dimensions have cosine sd `1/√5280 = 0.0138`, so this is **66.8σ**. The digit-region offset was selected by maximising ‖v‖, and all **16/16 strings chose the same offset (4)** — the selection found the real digit region, not per-string noise. **(2) THE TOKEN-IDENTITY CONFOUND, which nearly sank it.** A difference between `1`-positions and `0`-positions trivially contains the *token embedding* difference — that is "the symbol here is a 1", not a register. Controls: `|corr(proj, current bit)| = 0.302`; `|corr(proj, y_i)|` = only **0.072** raw, rising to **0.333** once position is removed (the projection carries a large position trend that masks it). Controlling position **and** the current bit: **0.269**. And correlating against **`y_(i-1)`, the count BEFORE the current symbol — which token identity at position i cannot explain — gives the same 0.269.** **(3) SIGN CONSISTENCY is what makes it a direction.** All **16/16** correlations positive (range +0.119 to +0.407), mean **+0.2686**, one-sample t = **+13.43, p = 9.15e-10**, Wilcoxon p = 3.05e-05. The same `v`, derived independently per string, points the same way every time. **(4) MAGNITUDE IN CONTEXT.** |r| ≈ 0.27 from one hand-derived direction against R² = 0.601 (|r| = 0.78) from the full ridge probe (D32) — expected, since the probe optimises over all 5280 dimensions and this uses a single vector. The two are consistent, not competing, and the simple version is the more convincing because it has no fitted parameters at all. **(5) PROVENANCE FIX.** This analysis was possible only because the kernel dumped the raw per-position latents once (48 arrays + `meta.json` with token ids, targets and prompts) instead of returning summary numbers. D32's probe had to re-run the model for every new question — the conclusions existed but the data behind them did not. Every further analysis of these states now runs offline. |
| D35 | **SCOPED BY D54 (2026-08-04): the effect is in the answer process, not in when the count becomes decodable; and the untrained control that deflated D40/D41/D48/D53 cannot be applied here, because the measurement presupposes a working readout.** **H2 CONFIRMED WITH THE LENGTH CONFOUND REMOVED: required recurrent depth scales with the count at CONSTANT prompt length. rho=+0.225, p=1.3e-3, n=200.** This is the result the whole project was after, and the first time any positive here cannot be prompt length. | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-depth-fixed-length` (log in `scratch/kaggle_depth_fixed/out/`); Barannikov's design — m=64 fixed, difficulty varied by p(1)∈{0.15,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.85}, 40 seeds each, float32 | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-07-26. Supersedes D33, whose positive was length-confounded by construction.** **(1) THE CONFOUND IS BROKEN BY CONSTRUCTION, and the kernel checks it.** All 200 prompts tokenise to **exactly 77 tokens** — the kernel prints the set of distinct lengths and it has one element. Answers span **2..58**. So `rank-corr(difficulty, seq_len)` is undefined rather than 1.000 (D25/§21), and any depth scaling cannot be length. **(2) THE PRE-REGISTERED TEST.** Complete cases: rho = **+0.1752, p = 0.0437, n=133**. Bootstrap 95% CI [+0.012, +0.334]. This was specified in the kernel before it ran, so it carries no multiplicity penalty. **(3) CENSORING, WHICH BIASED IT DOWNWARD.** 67/200 instances never resolve within 64 unrolls, and those have **higher** counts (36.5 vs 30.0, Mann-Whitney p=0.0136) — so dropping them removes exactly the largest r* values. Treating them as right-censored at the bound and using all 200: **rho = +0.2253, p = 1.34e-03**, bootstrap CI [+0.086, +0.355]. The complete-case figure was a lower bound, as the bias direction predicted. **(4) AN INDEPENDENT SECOND SIGNATURE.** The *censoring rate itself* rises with the count (rho=+0.175, p=0.013) — harder instances are both slower to resolve and likelier never to resolve. Two readouts of the same effect from one run. **(5) A HYPOTHESIS OF MINE, REJECTED TWICE.** The binned r* dips at the highest counts (50.2 → 36.6), which suggested difficulty is about *balance* — a string of 58 ones is as uniform as one of 6. Tested: entropy H(p) gives rho=+0.139 against count's +0.225, and in a joint rank regression entropy adds **+0.020 R²** over count alone. Rejected on complete cases and again on the full censored-inclusive data. The dip is sampling noise; **the count is the operative variable**. **(6) EFFECT SIZE, STATED PLAINLY.** rho≈0.23 is modest — mean r* rises from 28.9 (counts 2–15) to 50.2 (counts 38–50). Real, pre-registered, confound-free, and not large. **CONSEQUENCE: H2 survives in its behavioural form.** Latent *geometry* does not encode reasoning depth (D28, D26(1), D29) — but required *depth itself* does scale with the amount of computation, measured behaviourally at fixed prompt length. The original hypothesis was right about the phenomenon and wrong about where to look for it. |
| D36 | **SUPERSEDED BY D50 (2026-08-04): computed along a direction that is 97% the current-token contrast, so the ℤ-action results describe token identity, not a register.** **The register is confirmed on BOTH curator tasks and behaves as a genuine ℤ-action: `(` and `)` displace the state by +v and −v, and a matched pair returns it to where it started.** Also: one of my own tests was vacuous and is retracted. | `scratch/kaggle_states/out2/` — 48 raw per-position latent arrays + `meta.json`, analysed entirely offline | **Verified-live, 2026-07-26. Extends D34 from Task a to Task b.** **(1) THE INCREMENT DIRECTION EXISTS ON BOTH TASKS.** `v = mean(Δ|'(') − mean(Δ|')')`, computed independently per string, has pairwise cosine **+0.9383 (68.2σ)** across 16 balanced strings and **+0.8847 (64.3σ)** across 16 unbalanced ones — against a random-direction sd of `1/√5280 = 0.0138`. Task a's count direction gave +0.9199. **(2) IT CARRIES ACCUMULATED DEPTH, controlling for position AND token identity.** Correlating the projection against `depth_(i−1)` — the depth *before* the current symbol, which token identity at position i cannot explain — with position and current symbol partialled out: **b_bal r = +0.2248, 16/16 same sign, p = 2.4e-06; b_unbal r = +0.2890, 16/16, p = 9.5e-11** (Task a: +0.2686, 16/16, p = 9.2e-10). Task b is the harder test: depth is a *signed* sum that rises and falls, so `R²(depth ~ position) = 0.160` — a direction tracking "how far along am I" cannot fake it. **(3) THE TASKS USE SEPARATE AXES.** Cosine between Task a's `+1` direction and Task b's `(` direction: **+0.058** — essentially orthogonal. The model does not reuse one increment axis; each task gets its own register. **(4) A GENUINE GROUP ACTION.** Two-step net displacement along v, by symbol pair: **`((` +31.6, `))` −29.7, and matched pairs `()`+`)(` average to −0.44 (t=−0.14, p=0.891)**; unbalanced gives `((` +30.3, `))` −31.8, matched +1.55 (p=0.666). So `(` and `)` move the state symmetrically and **a matched pair returns it to its starting point** — that is `T_(−1) = T_(+1)^(−1)`, Barannikov's ℤ-action, measured. Nothing in the construction of v forces this. **Deviation from pure additivity, stated:** `()` reads +16.4 and `)(` reads −17.3 rather than each being 0, so the *earlier* symbol of a pair dominates the two-step difference by roughly ±16. Additive only in the mean, not term by term. **(5) RETRACTION — my first symmetry test was an algebraic identity.** I measured `−pc/po` where `po`,`pc` are displacements relative to the mean step, and reported 0.9688 for balanced strings as a 3% asymmetry. It is exactly `n_open/n_close`: with `base·v = (n_o a + n_c b)/N`, `po = n_c(a−b)/N` and `pc = −n_o(a−b)/N`, so the ratio is `n_o/n_c` independent of the model. The balanced strings have 31 opens / 32 closes after dropping the first symbol, and 31/32 = **0.96875** — matching the "measurement" to five decimals. The tell was sd = **1.9e-16** across 16 random strings, which no model property can produce. Replaced by the matched-pair test above, which is not an identity. |
| D37 | **A sharp dissociation: the counting register's CONTENT is fully present after ONE unroll and does not grow with depth, while its DIRECTION rotates monotonically and locks by r≈16. Recurrence does not build the register — it rotates it into a stable frame.** My pre-registered prediction (that decodability would rise with depth) is falsified. | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-register-vs-depth` (log + raw states in `scratch/kaggle_register_depth/`); float32, states captured at every unroll in ONE forward pass, 12 seeds × 2 tasks × 7 checkpoints | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-07-27.** **THE QUESTION.** D35 (depth scales with difficulty) and D34/D36 (a register exists) stood as unrelated facts. If unrolling *constructs* the register, its decodability should rise with unroll count. **(1) IT DOES NOT.** Register correlation by unroll — Task a: **0.2480, 0.2568, 0.2415, 0.2550, 0.2512, 0.2502, 0.2495** at r = 1,2,4,8,16,32,64. Task b: 0.1797…0.2068. **rho(unroll, register_r) = +0.006 (p=0.958) and +0.022 (p=0.842)** — dead flat. The count is available after a *single* core-block application, which is unsurprising in hindsight: attention can sum bits in one pass. **(2) BUT THE DIRECTION ROTATES, MONOTONICALLY AND TIGHTLY.** `|cos(v_r, v_final)|` — Task a: **0.454, 0.562, 0.727, 0.906, 0.992, 1.000, 1.000**; Task b: 0.429, 0.495, 0.706, 0.916, 0.993, 1.000. **rho(unroll, |cos|) = +0.9903, p = 5.5e-72** for both. Across-seed sd shrinks 0.014 → 0.008 → 0.002 → 0.000, so all 12 seeds follow the same rotation trajectory, not an average artifact. At r=1 the increment axis is only **45%** aligned with its final form. **(3) THE READING.** The recurrence is not computing the count; it is moving the count's representation into a stable basis. Content immediate, frame convergent. **This dissociates D35 from register construction**: since the register is complete at r=1, whatever extra depth harder instances require in D35 is *not* being spent building it — more plausibly on the readout or the answer formation. That is a testable next question, not a claim. **(4) CAVEAT, stated because it limits the strong reading.** `|cos(v_r, v_final)| → 1` is partly definitional as r → 64, since v_final is measured there. The informative content is the SHAPE of the approach (0.45 → 0.73 → 0.91 → 0.99 over r = 1→16), not the endpoint. A cleaner version would compare against a v estimated from an independent longer run. **(5) DESIGN NOTE.** v is **refit at each depth** rather than held fixed at its r=64 value. Holding it fixed would understate early depths and manufacture exactly the rising trend I predicted; refitting is why the magnitude came out flat and the prediction was cleanly falsifiable. `v_cos_to_final` is recorded precisely so the reader can see how much refitting mattered — and it mattered a great deal. |
| D38 | **What the extra depth is FOR: readout. The register exists from unroll 1, but getting it into the answer token improves from R²=0.675 to 0.993 over ~24 unrolls.** Completes the mechanism D37 opened. | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-readout-vs-depth` (log + r=64 state dump in `scratch/kaggle_readout/`); 220 prompts, float32, m=64 fixed | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-07-28.** **THE SETUP.** D37 left a gap: the per-position register is complete at r=1 and only its frame rotates, yet harder instances demand more depth (D35). Neither can be about *building* the register. The hypothesis tested here is READOUT — the register is distributed across 64 positions and the answer is one token; aggregating it is a different job from computing it. **(1) READOUT IMPROVES WITH DEPTH, strongly.** Decoding the total from the ANSWER-TOKEN state alone: **R² = 0.675 (r=1), 0.904 (r=2), 0.972 (r=4), 0.986 (r=16), 0.992 (r=32), 0.993 (r=64)**. `rho(unroll, R²) = +0.9833`, and every point beats its permutation null at p<0.001 with null means −0.10…−0.27 (so no leakage). Change from r=1 to r=64: **+0.318**. **Contrast with D37 on the same model and the same depths: the per-position register is FLAT (rho=+0.006) while the answer-token readout rises (rho=+0.983).** The two measurements differ only in *where* the state is read, so the difference is about location, not method. **(2) THE CONFOUND IS BROKEN.** All 220 prompts tokenise to **exactly 74 tokens**; totals span 4..56. Nothing here can be prompt length. **(3) THE MECHANISM, now complete.** The count is computed immediately — attention can sum bits in one pass (D37). Its representational frame then rotates into a stable basis over ~16 unrolls (D37). And it is progressively transferred into the answer token over ~24 unrolls (here). D35's depth requirement is about the last of these. **(4) THE DIRECT LINK TO D35 IS NOT THERE — tested at every depth, and this CORRECTS the framing above.** A second run measured per-instance CV readout error at all nine checkpoints, precisely to test whether hard instances are read out worse *while the curve is still climbing*: rho(count, |error|) = **−0.023 / −0.068 / +0.112 / +0.163 / −0.092 / −0.034 / −0.018 / −0.017 / −0.023** at r = 1…64, p = 0.730 / 0.314 / 0.097 / **0.016** / 0.175 / 0.615 / 0.796 / 0.800 / 0.741. One nominal hit at r=8 does not survive Bonferroni over nine tests (0.14), and the signs alternate. **Readout quality is not difficulty-dependent at any depth.** Mean |error| does fall steeply with depth (6.94 → 3.29 → 1.80 → 1.28 → 0.93 counts), matching the R² curve. **SO THE HEADLINE CLAIM NEEDS SPLITTING.** Depth buys readout *in aggregate* — that is solid (rho=+0.967 to +0.983 across two runs). It does **not** explain why HARDER instances need more depth. The resolution is that the two measurements ask different questions: a linear probe asks whether the count is PRESENT in the answer-token state (yes, R²=0.65 already at r=1), while D35's r\* asks when the MODEL'S OWN logit margin saturates. Information availability and the model's use of it are not the same thing, and the difficulty-dependence of r\* lives in the latter, which the probe bypasses entirely. **That is now the open question, stated precisely rather than papered over.** **(4b) RUN-TO-RUN VARIATION, recorded.** The two runs give R²=0.6754 and 0.6545 at r=1 (0.9928 at r=64 in both). Cross-validation is seeded identically, so the difference is GPU float32 non-determinism in the forward pass. Small, but it means single-decimal agreement should not be expected from a re-run. **(5) HONEST SCALE.** R²=0.675 at r=1 is already high, so most of the readout is immediate too; the depth-dependent part is the final +0.318. And the curve saturates by r≈24-32 while D35's hard instances have r* up to ~50, which is consistent (R² is averaged over instances) but not itself evidence. |
| D39 | **"Geometry encodes the computation" is TRUE — at the answer token, across prompts. The count occupies a ~3-dimensional subspace of the answer-token state.** The original framing was right about the phenomenon and wrong about where to look. | Offline PCA of `scratch/kaggle_readout/out/answer_states_r64.npy`, 220 prompts all at 74 tokens, totals 4..56 | **Verified-live, 2026-07-28.** **THE REFRAME.** This project spent months asking whether the SHAPE OF A TRAJECTORY over unrolls encodes reasoning depth. It does not (D28, D26, D29 — winding tracks the recording window; H1's classifier had one reachable output). Asking the same question of the ANSWER-TOKEN STATE ACROSS PROMPTS gives a different answer. **(1) THE ANSWER CODE IS LOW-DIMENSIONAL.** PCA of 220 answer-token states: variance **36.1% / 22.7% / 13.7% / 5.0% / 3.7%**, participation ratio **3.88 of 10**. **(2) THE COUNT LIVES IN IT.** PC1 correlates with the total at pearson **+0.7273** (spearman +0.7547), PC3 at −0.4745, PC2 at −0.3978. Cumulative R² for the count: **0.529 (1 PC), 0.687 (2), 0.912 (3), 0.924 (5), 0.977 (10)**. So the count occupies roughly a **3-dimensional** subspace — PC1 is the dominant axis but carries only about half of it alone. (My first phrasing, "a single principal direction carries most of the count", was wrong and is corrected here: one direction gives R²=0.53.) **(3) STILL ON THE SPHERE.** State norms 76.37 ± 0.0007, a **0.0010%** variation — tighter even than the 0.0073% measured over unrolls (D24(8)), confirming the RMSNorm constraint holds across prompts as well as across depth. **(4) LENGTH CANNOT EXPLAIN IT.** All 220 prompts are exactly 74 tokens. **(5) WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE PROJECT'S THESIS.** The hypothesis was "latent geometry encodes reasoning depth". Measured as trajectory shape over unrolls it is false. Measured as the geometry of the answer representation across problem instances it is true, low-dimensional, and quantified. The original instinct was sound; the index was wrong — the same correction D26(5) reached for winding (across token positions, not across unrolls) and D38 reached for depth (readout, not register construction). |
| D40 | **THE CONTROL THAT SHOULD HAVE BEEN RUN FIRST: an UNTRAINED model with random weights reproduces the project's positive findings, most of them MORE strongly. The "counting register" is architectural, not learned.** | Kaggle T4 kernel `arsen4ikvar/geometry-untrained-baseline` v2 (log in `scratch/kaggle_baseline/out/`); identical architecture, config, tokenizer and RMSNorm, `from_config` with `torch.manual_seed(0)`, float32, 60 prompts, num_steps=32 | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-04.** ⚠ **THE INFERENCE BELOW WAS OVER-STATED AND IS QUALIFIED — see (7). The raw numbers stand; what they license does not.** An adversarial review (2 independent agents) found two defects I missed, both correct. **(1) THE COMPARISON.** ``trained → untrained``: `v_pairwise_cos` **0.7996 → 0.9991**; `register_r` **0.2822 → 0.2608**; `register_sign_frac` **1.000 → 1.000**; `readout_r2_count` **0.9773 → 1.0000**; `positional_k1_resid_r2` **−0.2325 → +0.5272**. A network that has never been trained decodes the total count from its answer-token state at **R² = 1.0000**, produces a *more* consistent "register direction" than the trained one, and shows *more* positional structure. **(2) WHAT THIS MEANS.** The count is a linear function of the input tokens; random mixing plus a residual stream preserves linear functions of the input in linearly-decodable form. This is a random-features / reservoir property of the architecture, not evidence of a learned counting mechanism. **D34, D36, D39 and the endpoint of D38 must be reread as facts about a normalised residual stream, not about Huginn's training.** The skeptic's first objection — "attention computes sums, this is the expected result" — is confirmed at full strength. **(3) WHAT THE CONTROL DOES *NOT* TOUCH, stated so the retraction is not over-broad.** The baseline measured the **endpoint at r=32 only**. D37 (register decodability flat in r while its direction rotates) and D38 (readout R² rising 0.65→0.993 with depth) are claims about the **depth trajectory**, and that comparison was **not made**. An untrained model's readout may be flat at 1.0 from r=1 — which would kill them — or may show the same rise — which would make the *dynamics* a trained property even though the endpoint is not. **This is now the single most informative unrun measurement in the project.** Also untouched: D30 (bf16 masks 4.6× of convergence — a precision claim), D31 (ρ=0.802 by Arnoldi — a measurement, not a claim about learning), D35 (required depth scales with difficulty — a claim about the model's own *logit margin*, which an untrained model has no meaningful version of), and every negative result (D26, D28, D29). **(4) ONE APPARENT DISCREPANCY, DIAGNOSED.** `readout_r2_signed` reads 0.38 here against 0.13 in the original control. Cause: this kernel uses a **single** random signed vector; the original averaged **12 draws** (mean 0.133, sd 0.153), so a single draw at 0.38 is ~1.6 sd out. Variance, not a bug — but it is a real weakness of this kernel and the comparison should be re-run with averaging. **(5) THE ONE PLACE TRAINING SHOWS UP.** Trained is *less* consistent (`v_pairwise_cos` 0.80 vs 0.999) and has *less* linear positional structure (−0.23 vs +0.53). Training appears to make the representation more context-dependent and less like a fixed random projection. Whether that is interesting or an artifact of the untrained network being closer to a pure linear map is untested. **(7) ⚠ TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY ADVERSARIAL REVIEW — the inference is qualified.** **(a) The control cannot reproduce ground truth on the model where ground truth is known.** The "trained" column here is a *fresh* n=60 / num_steps=32 run, not the original figures. Comparing like for like: `positional_k1_resid_r2` reads **−0.2325** here against **+0.585** originally (n=220, steps=64) — a **sign flip**; `readout_r2_signed` reads **0.3803** against **0.133**. **The n=60 pipeline fails to reproduce the trained model on 2 of 6 metrics**, so it is measuring in a higher-noise regime (5-fold CV on 60 samples leaves ~12 held-out points per fold) and cannot be trusted to *equate* two models. **(b) The discriminating measurement was omitted.** For the trained model `|cos(v, e('1')−e('0'))| = 0.0078` — v is NOT the embedding contrast. **The same quantity was never computed for the untrained model.** If v_untrained *is* the contrast, the two models reach similar summary statistics by **different mechanisms**, and "untrained reproduces it" is a false equivalence rather than a refutation. **(c) A mechanism I should have seen:** Huginn re-injects the prompt embeddings *every unroll*, so `count·e('1') + (64−count)·e('0')` is additively re-added to the residual stream for **any** weights. That alone predicts near-perfect count decodability in an untrained net — and predicts untrained *beating* trained, since trained weights may spend capacity repurposing rather than preserving that signal. It explains `readout_r2_count` and is silent on `register_r`. **WHAT SURVIVES THE REVIEW:** `register_r` (0.282 vs 0.261) and `v_pairwise_cos` are stable across the sample-size drop and are the part of the control that holds; `readout_r2_count` was arguably never strong evidence of a learned mechanism in either model, per (c); `readout_r2_signed` and `positional_k1_resid_r2` are **uninterpretable at n=60** and license nothing. **A matched re-run is now queued** — n=220, num_steps=64, signed-sum control averaged over 12 draws, and `cos(v_untrained, embedding contrast)` measured — which is the single test that decides between "same phenomenon" and "different mechanism, similar summary". **(6) THE PRACTICE LESSON, which is the whole point.** This control costs one GPU hour and was available from day one. It was run in week six, after twelve refinements of a geometric metric and four probing experiments. **An untrained baseline should be the first control for any representational claim**, and its absence here invalidated more work than any other single omission. |
| D41 | **Decodability and capability move in OPPOSITE directions: the untrained model decodes the count 20× more precisely than the trained one and cannot count at all. Training makes the representation coarser, earlier, and usable.** This partially rescues D38 and is the project's cleanest result. | Kaggle T4 kernels `geometry-readout-vs-depth` (trained) and `geometry-untrained-depth` (random weights), identical protocol: 220 prompts, all exactly 74 tokens, float32, states captured at every unroll in one forward pass | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-04.** D40 showed an untrained net reproduces the *endpoint*; it did not test the *trajectory*. Running the identical depth sweep on random weights: **(1) THE CURVES ARE DIFFERENT, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS A TRAINING SIGNATURE.** `readout R²` by unroll — trained **0.675, 0.904, 0.972, 0.967, 0.986, 0.992, 0.992, 0.993, 0.993**; untrained **0.200, 0.415, 0.851, 0.997, 1.000, 1.000, 1.000, 1.000, 1.000**. **At r=1 the trained model is 3.4× better (0.675 vs 0.200).** The untrained net needs ~8 unrolls of random mixing before the count becomes linearly available; the trained net has it after **one**. So D38's "readout improves with depth" holds for both, but its *shape* does not — and the r=1 advantage is not architectural. **SCOPING CORRECTION, from (8) below: at r=1 BOTH models sit below the level-only ceiling R²=0.9237, so the 3.4× gap is a gap at COARSE resolution and is not evidence that either model "has the count after one unroll". The defensible version of "earlier" is the ceiling-crossing in (8), not the r=1 ratio.** **(2) AT SATURATION THE UNTRAINED MODEL IS 20× MORE PRECISE.** Mean absolute decoding error: untrained **0.046 counts**, trained **0.934 counts** (R² 1.0000 vs 0.9928), on totals spanning 4–56. **(3) CORRECTION, 2026-08-04 — THE ACCURACY FIGURES WERE NOT MEASURED IN THESE RUNS, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS ROW OVERCLAIMED.** I originally wrote "untrained 0%, trained ~10%" and built the headline on it. Neither kernel scored accuracy at all. The ~10% came from `results/counting_accuracy.csv`, a *different* configuration (n_ops 2–48, 56 rows), and broken down it is **37.5% at n_ops=2, 25% at n_ops=4, and 0% at n_ops=8, 16, 32, 48** (one 12.5% blip at 24) — the 10.7% average is carried entirely by the short sequences. **D41's task is M=64 bits, past every point where accuracy is already 0%.** So the trained model almost certainly scores ~0% here too, and **there is no measured capability contrast between the two models on the task the decodability was measured on.** "Decodability and capability move in opposite directions" is **RETRACTED as stated**. **(3b) WHAT IS ACTUALLY ESTABLISHED, AND IS STILL WORTH HAVING.** A near-perfect linear representation of the count coexists with no ability to report it — **R²=1.0000 / 0.046 counts** untrained, **R²=0.9928 / 0.934 counts** trained, at a length where the trained model's measured accuracy is 0% and the untrained model cannot exceed chance. **Probe R² is uninformative about capability here, and the more precisely decoded model is not the more capable one.** That is the classic "the information is present and the model cannot use it" result, with an unusually clean pair. What it is *not* is a demonstration that training trades precision for usability — that needs a task where the trained model is measurably better, and this is not one. **(3c) THE MEASUREMENT THAT WOULD SETTLE IT** is queued: `scratch/kaggle_rho_direct/` already loads all ten checkpoints, so scoring counting accuracy alongside ρ at each gives accuracy-versus-training and decodability-versus-training on the *same* task. If accuracy rises while decodability falls, "opposite directions" is earned rather than asserted. **(4) MECHANISM.** Huginn re-injects the prompt embeddings every unroll, so in an untrained net `count·e('1') + (64−count)·e('0')` accumulates essentially losslessly — a perfect linear accumulator that is behaviourally useless. The trained net computes the count in one attention step but does not preserve it losslessly, presumably because the residual stream capacity is spent on something the readout can actually consume. **Untrained = lossless and useless; trained = lossy, fast, and marginally usable.** **(5) WHY THIS IS THE PROJECT'S CLEANEST RESULT.** It is a naturally-occurring, quantitative counterexample to the inference "high probe R² ⇒ the model represents the quantity in a usable way", on a real architecture with a *meaningful* target, where the better-decoded model is not the more capable one. Most statements of that critique rest on synthetic random-label control tasks; here both models carry the count essentially perfectly (R² 1.0000 and 0.9928) at a length where the trained model's measured accuracy is 0% — see (3) for the retraction of the stronger reading. **(6) WHY `R²=1.0000` IS NOT AN INTERPOLATING PROBE**, which is the first thing to attack given d=5280 ≫ n=220. The readout is `StandardScaler → Ridge(alpha=1e3)` scored by 5-fold `cross_val_predict` — every prediction is out-of-fold, so an interpolating fit would score *worse*, not better. The decisive check is the label-permutation null run alongside it: it lands at **−0.05 to −0.19**, i.e. reliably NEGATIVE at every unroll. A probe with enough capacity to interpolate would drive the permuted null toward 0 or above; a null that is negative is the signature of an honest held-out score. The untrained state really does carry the count almost losslessly in a linear subspace. **(8) THE CONFOUND THAT WOULD OTHERWISE SINK THIS, AND THE THRESHOLD IT SUPPLIES.** Prompts are generated at five bit-rates `p_one ∈ {0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8}`, 44 seeds each, so `count` is strongly determined by `p_one` and a probe could score high by decoding the *generative level* rather than the count. Computed exactly over the 220 realised prompts: pooled within-level sd of count = **3.851**, overall sd = 13.94, so a decoder that knows only `p_one` caps at **R² = 0.9237** with error 3.851 counts. Measured errors: trained **0.934** (4.1× finer than the level structure), untrained **0.046** (**83.7× finer**). Both R² exceed 0.9237, so **both models resolve count within level and neither is decoding `p_one`** — and the untrained one resolves it 84× finer than the level structure that generated it, which is what "near-lossless accumulator" means quantitatively. **(9) THE CEILING DATES "EARLIER" PROPERLY.** Below R²=0.9237 a curve is consistent with level-only decoding; above it the probe *must* be resolving count. Log-interpolating the crossings: **trained crosses at r ≈ 2.4, untrained at r ≈ 5.7 — 2.3× later.** This is the defensible form of the earliness claim: it is anchored to a threshold with a meaning (the point where the readout provably carries more than the task's generative parameter) rather than to the arbitrary first unroll. **(10) WHAT IT DOES NOT RESCUE.** D34/D36/D39 (the register direction, the count subspace) were endpoint measurements and remain subject to D40's qualified verdict; the matched n=220 control is queued. D37's flat-register/rotating-direction claim was not measured on the untrained model and remains untested. |
| D42 | **Training SLOWS the contraction, ρ ≈ 0.66 → 0.91, lengthening the depth time-constant 2.4 → 10.6 unrolls. This — not accumulation — is why D41's two curves differ, and it is what makes test-time depth do anything at all.** | Re-analysis of the D41 curves through this project's own derived law (`src/traj_geom/observable_convergence.py`, eq. 3); no new compute | **Derived, then tested, 2026-08-04.** **(1) THE MECHANISM I PROPOSED IN D41(4) IS WRONG, AND I TESTED IT RATHER THAN ASSERTING IT.** D41(4) claimed re-injection makes the untrained net a coherent linear accumulator. That story has a closed-form consequence: signal accumulating coherently against noise accumulating in quadrature gives `SNR_r = A√((1−ρ^r)/(1+ρ^r))`, hence `R²_r = A²u_r/(1+A²u_r)`. Fitted to both curves it **fails**: both push ρ to the 0.999 boundary, fit R² is only 0.846 (untrained) and 0.884 (trained), and the residuals are systematically S-shaped (−0.17, −0.13, +0.15, +0.17, +0.10 …) — a misspecified shape, not noise. **The accumulator mechanism is retracted.** **(2) THE PROJECT'S OWN LAW FITS, AND FITS THE UNTRAINED CURVE BEST.** `observable_convergence.py` eq. (3) says any fixed quantity already present in the state has `R²_∞ − R²_r ~ C·ρ^{2r}`. Log-linear fits: **untrained ρ̂ = 0.6618, fit R² = 0.9808**; **trained ρ̂ = 0.9155, fit R² = 0.8972** — **both superseded by the direct measurement in D44 (0.7150 / 0.8866); the direction is confirmed but these inferred values overstate the gap by 1.5×** (full precision, read from the kernel JSONs; the first version of this row quoted 0.662/0.910 from 4-decimal transcriptions, and the regression tests in `tests/test_contraction_findings.py` were written to read the files precisely so that drift could not persist). **TWO CAVEATS THE TRANSCRIBED NUMBERS HID.** (i) `observable_convergence.py` states "treat a fit R² below ~0.9 as the law not applying rather than as a noisy estimate" — the trained all-r fit is **0.8972, marginally below its own bar**. The residuals localise the problem to r=1 (+1.24 in log-gap), where the state is not yet in the linear regime the law assumes; dropping it gives ρ̂=0.9263 at fit 0.9494, and r≥4 gives 0.9330 at 0.9558. (ii) The untrained curve saturates so fast that only **4 points** sit above the floor, so its ρ rests on r∈{1,2,4,8}. **THE SEPARATION IS ROBUST TO BOTH.** untrained−trained gap: **+0.2537** (all r), **+0.2880** (r≥2). No window choice removes it. **(3) THE ESTIMATOR IS CALIBRATED ON THIS EXACT MODEL.** Its trained output, 0.910, agrees with three independent measurements of the trained ρ obtained by unrelated methods — two-orbit convergence 0.85–0.90 (D24(7)), Arnoldi on Jacobian-vector products 0.79–0.81 (D31), feature rotation 0.861/0.868 — so the 0.6618 is not an artefact of the estimator. **(4) THE RESULT.** Time constant `τ = −1/ln ρ`: untrained **2.42 unrolls** (95% converged by r≈7.3), trained **11.33 unrolls** (95% converged by r≈34.0). **Training moves the recurrence closer to the edge of stability.** That is precisely what buys usable test-time depth — a fast-contracting map is done after ~8 unrolls no matter how many you give it — and D41 shows it is bought with representational fidelity (fixed-point linear count content 1.0000 → 0.9928). **(5) THIS SUBSUMES D41'S "EARLIER" AND "COARSER" INTO ONE PARAMETER.** The untrained model looks "later" (crossing the level ceiling at r≈5.7 vs 2.4) *and* "more precise" because it is a **faster** contraction onto a **better-conditioned** fixed point. Neither curve shows a feature being built; both are pure convergence, exactly as `observable_convergence.py` warns. **(6) LIMITATION, STATED.** ρ_untrained = 0.662 is inferred from an observable, not measured on the untrained operator. The trained ρ has three direct confirmations; the untrained has none yet. The direct check — consecutive-state orbit convergence on random weights — is cheap and queued behind the two occupied Kaggle slots. Until it lands, D42(4) is "the untrained readout converges at 0.662", and "the untrained *operator* contracts at 0.662" is the inference. |
| D43 | **RETRACTED 2026-08-04 (see (6)). Was: "the contraction rate predicts how deep Huginn can usefully think." The direct ρ of D44 flips the verdict, and the claim turns out to depend on two arbitrary analyst choices.** | Derivation from D42 + Geiping et al.'s reported numbers as recorded in `docs/deep_research_huginn_literature.md:101–116` (direct read of the full paper, 2026-07-23); no new compute | **Derived and checked, 2026-08-04.** **(1) THE BOUND.** Once the latent state has converged, no further unroll can change the output. So the depth at which *accuracy* saturates is bounded above by the depth at which the *state* converges. D42 measures the state; the paper measures the accuracy; the two have never been compared. The bound is falsifiable in one direction: any task saturating LATER than the state settles kills it. **(2) THE NUMBERS.** Trained ρ=0.9155 → τ=11.33 unrolls → **95% settled by r=34.0, 99% by r=52.2**. (Corrected from 31.8/48.8, which came from 4-decimal transcriptions; the bound holds under every fit window — 95%-settle is r=34.0 at all-r, 39.2 at r≥2, 43.2 at r≥4, all ≥ 32.) Geiping et al. report saturation at ~8 (HellaSwag), ~8–12 (ARC-C no few-shot), **~32 (ARC-C with 25–50 few-shot)**, and GSM8K rising 9–10% at r=4 to 28–38% at r=32. **Every reported saturation point is ≤ 32, against a 95%-settle depth of 34.0. The bound holds under every fit window, and the hardest condition saturates just below where the state does.** Their own stated principle — "saturation is highly task-dependent, on easier tasks the model saturates quicker" — is what a one-sided bound predicts: tasks may finish early, none may finish late. **(3) TWO INTERNAL CHECKS ON A DIFFERENT OBSERVABLE.** The same τ predicts D41's readout curves, which played no part in fitting it: untrained τ=2.42 → 95% by r=7.3, and D41's untrained readout reaches 0.997 at r=8; trained τ=11.33 → 99% by r=52.2, and D41's trained readout rises through r=48 then is flat at 0.9928 to r=64. Both match. **(4) SO ONE MEASURED PARAMETER ACCOUNTS FOR THREE THINGS IT WAS NOT FITTED TO** — the untrained readout saturating at r≈8, the trained readout saturating at r≈48, and the outer envelope of the published accuracy curves. That is the strongest form of evidence this project has produced for any mechanistic claim. **(6) RETRACTION, 2026-08-04.** D44 measured ρ directly and got **0.8866** (orbit) / **0.8646** (step), not the inferred 0.9155 this row used. Re-running the bound: at the 95%-settled threshold the state converges by **r=24.9** (orbit) or **r=20.6** (step), while ARC-C with few-shot saturates at **r≈32** — **the bound is VIOLATED**. It survives only at a 99% threshold, and under the step estimator not even there (r=31.7 < 32). So the verdict flips with (a) which ρ estimate is used and (b) how converged "converged" is taken to mean, neither of which has a principled answer. **A quantity that changes sign under two arbitrary choices is not a bound and was never entitled to be called a prediction.** **WHAT SURVIVES, AS AN OBSERVATION ONLY:** at r=32 the trained state is ~98% of the way to its fixed point, which is the right order of magnitude for saturation points reported in the 8–32 range. That is a consistency remark, not a bound, not fitted-free evidence, and it cannot discriminate between mechanisms. **(7) WHY I RECORDED IT TOO STRONGLY.** The row claimed "one measured parameter accounts for three things it was not fitted to" — but two of those three (D41's readout curves) share the *same* observable and the *same* estimator as the ρ being tested, so they were never independent confirmations. Only the published saturation points were external, and they are what fails. **(5) LIMITATIONS, STATED.** (a) The bound is *one-sided*: state convergence is necessary for accuracy saturation, not sufficient, so agreement at the top (34.0 vs ~32) is one data point and "tight" is suggestive, not established. (b) ρ was measured on **counting prompts only**, while the published saturation points are GSM8K/ARC-C/HellaSwag — cross-task constancy of ρ is an assumption, not a measurement, and is the obvious next control. (c) D42's ρ itself is still observable-inferred for the untrained arm; the operator-level sweep is queued. |
| D44 | **D42 CONFIRMED ON THE OPERATOR: the untrained map contracts at ρ=0.7150±0.0069, the trained at ρ=0.8866±0.0362. Training slows the contraction. The estimator was **biased outward at both ends** — 7.4% low on the untrained, 3.3% high on the trained — so it exaggerated exactly the gap it was used to measure, by **1.5×**. `observable_convergence.py` claims ~1.3% mean relative error from synthetic validation; on real data it is 3–7% and directionally systematic.
