(Trained F=0.82, p=0.52, but two of its twelve fits are unusable, see D44(4).) So "not the prompt" is false as stated. Two further defects: family and prompt length are collinear by construction, so "ρ does not track length" and "ρ does not track family" are one test reported twice; and with **n=3 per family** the design cannot place a tight bound anyway. **WHAT SURVIVES:** the *absolute* across-family variation is small — **0.0151 (untrained), 0.0474 (trained)** — against a training gap of 0.159, so ρ varies by roughly a third of the effect across tasks. That is enough to say a ρ measured on one family is the right order of magnitude for another; it is not enough to call ρ a property of the operator, and **backlog 5.4 stays open**. | D46 | **D40 CONFIRMED at matched n=220, and the omitted control comes out AGAINST the deflationary reading: the register direction is NOT the tokenizer's embedding contrast.** | Kaggle T4 `geometry-untrained-baseline` v3, 220 prompts, trained and random-weight Huginn, identical protocol | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-04.** The adversarial check on D40 raised two objections: the n=60 control could not reproduce the trained ground truth (`positional_k1` flipped sign, +0.585 → −0.2325), and `cos(v, embedding contrast)` was never measured. Both are now settled at n=220. **(1) EVERY METRIC REPRODUCED OR EXCEEDED BY RANDOM WEIGHTS** — `v_pairwise_cos` 0.8057 → **0.9991**; `register_r` 0.2882 → 0.2663; `register_sign_frac` 1.0000 → **1.0000**; `readout_r2_count` 0.9929 → **1.0000**; `readout_r2_signed` 0.1445 → **0.3333**; `positional_k1_resid_r2` 0.5851 → **0.9203**. The sign flip was an artefact of n=60; at n=220 the untrained value not only reproduces but **exceeds** the trained one. D40 stands, strengthened. **(1b) DEFECT FOUND 2026-08-04, VERIFIED DIRECTLY: `register_r` IS COMPUTED ON AN OFF-BY-ONE WINDOW.** The kernel slices `seg = st[4:4 + n]` (`scratch/kaggle_baseline/main.py:88`), but tokenising the actual prompt shows the 64 digit tokens occupy indices **3..66**, not 4..67. So the window drops the first digit and includes the trailing `.` token. Both `register_r` values (0.2882 / 0.2663) are computed on misaligned data and **must be recomputed before either is cited**. Whether the misalignment *creates* the correlation or merely perturbs it is NOT established — a running count shifts by at most 1 per position, so a cumulative-count correlation would largely survive a one-token shift. Flagged, not diagnosed. **(2) THE OMITTED CONTROL — CONCLUSION WITHDRAWN PENDING RECHECK.** `|cos(v, e('1') − e('0'))|` = **0.0058 (trained), 0.0023 (untrained)** — both essentially zero. I concluded `v` is **not** the tokenizer's embedding contrast. **That conclusion is withdrawn.** In 5280 dimensions two unrelated unit vectors have E|cos| ≈ √(2/(π·5280)) ≈ **0.0110**, so 0.0058 and 0.0023 are *ordinary draws from the null* — the test returns "near 0" for any pair of unrelated vectors and therefore discriminates nothing. Worse, `v` was compared against the contrast in the **embedding table** `wte`, while `v` lives in the **residual stream**; those are different spaces, and the comparison should be made where `v` actually lives. The deflationary explanation is **not** dead; it is untested. The register is architectural (D40) but it is not trivial. **(3) THE UNTRAINED DIRECTION IS MORE CONSISTENT, NOT LESS**: `v_pairwise_cos` 0.9991 vs 0.8057 means the untrained model uses nearly the *same* direction for every prompt while the trained model's varies. Another metric on which the untrained model "wins". |
| D47 | **The causal patching experiment is BROKEN, not null — the intervention never reached the output. Recorded so it is not cited as a negative result.** | Kaggle T4 `geometry-causal-patch`, 120 prompts | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-04; result discarded. DIAGNOSIS CORRECTED same day — my first one was wrong on two counts of code-reading.** The pre-registered test was: patch the state along the count-readout direction and see whether the emitted number shifts. Outcome: **the model emitted `1.00` in all 21 conditions** — every α ∈ {−8,−4,−2,0,+2,+4,+8}, along the count direction, a random control, and an unrepresented control alike, with slope exactly +0.0000 and R²=0.000 in all three. **A ±8 perturbation along a random direction producing byte-identical output is not a null result; it is a dead intervention.** **CORRECTION.** I wrote that the intervention "never reached the output". That is wrong: `scratch/kaggle_causal/main.py:102` computes `logits = coda_head(store["h"], freqs)`, a hand-rolled coda applied directly to the **patched** tensor, so the patch does reach the logits by construction — and `alpha*unit` shifts `w·h` by exactly alpha by design. I also cited the constant patch norm as evidence of a bug; the print at line 134 hardcodes `8*unit` and sits *outside* the alpha loop, so its constancy means nothing. Both were my code-reading errors. **THE CORRECT DIAGNOSIS IS THAT THE EXPERIMENT HAS NO POWER**: the outcome variable has **zero dynamic range before any intervention**. The α=0 row *is* the unintervened model, and it emits `1.00` for all 120 prompts at 0% accuracy. A causal effect on an output that never varies cannot be detected, so this run distinguishes "the direction is not used" from "the readout is degenerate on this task" **not at all**. (Note also `main.py:129-130` contains an `if False else` branch and an unused `ybad`, so the script was not reviewed before I cited it.) **No causal conclusion may be drawn from this run in either direction.** **THE ONE SALVAGEABLE NUMBER, WITH A CAVEAT I MISSED:** unpatched accuracy is **0/120 at M=64**. But every ground-truth answer here is two-digit and the parse takes the first digit group, so this is a weaker measurement than `counting_accuracy.csv` and is not strictly comparable to it. It is consistent with D41(3)'s retraction rather than independent confirmation of it. |
| D48 | **The geometric form of D41: the untrained model's count representation is literally ONE-DIMENSIONAL (participation ratio 1.0, PC1 correlates +0.999973 with the count); the trained model's is smeared over ~5 effective dimensions. The apparent parabola in the PC1–PC2 plane is that same 1-D curve seen in two dimensions — corr(PC2, count²) = **−0.9957**. So "R² = 1.0000" is not a probe finding a faint signal; the state *is* the count, up to a smooth reparameterisation. **(3) THE TRAINED "TWO LOBES" ARE NOT A SEPARATE FACTOR.** They track the count itself (point-biserial r=−0.567, p=4e-20) and the generative rate (χ²=75.4, p=2e-15), i.e. they are the count gradient spread out, not a discrete grouping. **(4) WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN THE R² NUMBERS.** R² 1.0000 vs 0.9928 is a difference that looks negligible and is easy to dismiss as noise; PR 1.0 vs 4.8 is the same fact stated in a form where the gap is a factor of five. The trained model does not hold a slightly worse copy of the untrained representation — it holds a structurally different one, distributed across a subspace no single direction spans. **(5) SCOPE.** Both are *decodability* statements about the counting task at M=64, where the trained model's measured accuracy is ~0 (D41(3)). This says nothing about what either model uses. Figures: `figures/answer_manifold_pca.png`, `figures/readout_regimes.png`. |

At r=64: **trained 74/220 resolved, median error 0.780, and 99 instances in the 0.25–1.0 band — sitting on the decision boundary**; **untrained 220/220 resolved, median 0.044, none on the edge.** **(1) THE TWO POPULATIONS BEHAVE QUALITATIVELY DIFFERENTLY WITH DEPTH.** The untrained instances descend as a *coherent bundle* — near-parallel on log-log, crossing the boundary together at r≈8 and flattening onto a hard floor. The trained instances are a *diffuse scatter* whose median plateaus at ~0.8 from r≈24 and never crosses; which instances resolve is not stable with depth (curves cross repeatedly). **(2) THIS IS WHY A MEAN R² WAS THE WRONG SUMMARY.** R²=0.9928 sounds like "almost perfect"; it is in fact a population where two thirds of instances fail to pin the integer and half sit on the boundary. **(3) IT ALSO ANSWERS "IS THERE AN EDGE OF CORRECTNESS?" FOR THE PROBE** — yes, a wide one, but only for the trained model; the untrained model has no edge because every instance clears the boundary by an order of magnitude. Recomputing per trajectory: **correct window r = +0.0485 ± 0.1355 and NOT sign-consistent; buggy window r = +0.2686 ± 0.0800, all 16 positive** (Wilcoxon p = 3.1e-05). The published 0.2686 reproduces the buggy value exactly. **`register_r` also rises monotonically with further misalignment — 0.049 (off 3), 0.269 (4), 0.296 (5), 0.310 (6) — which is the signature of an artefact, not of a signal.** **(2) WHY THE SEARCH PICKED THE WRONG OFFSET.** ‖v‖ does spike over the digit region (42.6 at offset 3, 46.3 at 4, ~11–25 elsewhere), so the search finds the right *region*; it then prefers 4 over 3 by only **9%**. A 9% margin was never enough to fix an alignment, and nothing checked it against the token ids that were sitting in `meta.json` the whole time. **(3) `v` IS THE CURRENT-TOKEN CONTRAST.** At the correct offset, |cos(v, mean state at '1' − mean state at '0')| = **0.9738 ± 0.0104**, i.e. **88.7× chance**. So the accumulated count is linearly decodable at R²=0.78 after the confounds that D34/D36's r=0.27 was supposed to control for. **(5) THE TWO FACTS TOGETHER.** |cos(v, the decoder's direction)| = **0.0004**, *below* chance. The count and the token contrast are essentially perpendicular: D34/D36 measured the one they did not want, controlled for it with a one-token-shifted covariate, and read the leakage as a register. **(6) WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT OVERTURN.** Overturned: `register_r` as a measurement, the ℤ-action / pair-displacement results of D36 (computed along the same `v`, so they describe the token contrast), and D46(1)'s comparison of the two arms. Not overturned: the *claim* that Huginn carries a linearly-decodable counting register — that is now better supported than before. Whether the register is architectural still needs the untrained arm rerun with the corrected window, which D46's saved states cannot supply. |

But the rotating mode survives only ~4 turns and was sampled at ~3 points per turn, so winding was measuring a near-aliased signal over a window 5–9× longer than the rotation lives. The phenomenon is real; the metric was wrong.** | Re-analysis of `scratch/kaggle_jacobian/out/` — Arnoldi on Jacobian-vector products, 3 prompts (n_ops 8/32/64), reverse-mode AD; **no new compute, the numbers were already on disk** | **Derived 2026-08-04 by applying `docs/observability.md` §1 to existing data.** **(1) THE QUESTION FIVE STATISTICS COULD NOT SETTLE.** Winding, chord-to-arc, H₁ persistence, participation ratio and subspace dimension all bear on rotation, and all failed together (D22, D26, D28, D32) because each needs a well-chosen recording window and none of them had one. The Jacobian's eigenvalue **arguments** answer it with no trajectory, no window and no null: a contracting map rotates iff its spectrum is complex, at a rate given by the argument. **D31 computed this spectrum and reported only the magnitudes.** The arguments sat unexamined in the log. **(2) THE ANSWER.** `lead_real` is **false in 3/3 prompts**; **27/30** of the reported top modes are complex. Leading-oscillatory `|arg|` = **2.397, 2.388, 1.050 rad**, i.e. rotation periods of **2.62, 2.63, 5.99 unrolls**. The state genuinely spirals. **(3) WHY WINDING NEVERTHELESS FAILED, QUANTITATIVELY.** With ρ=0.802 the oscillatory mode has time constant 4.53 unrolls, so it decays to 5% after ~13.6 unrolls, completing only **~4.2 turns**. Winding was computed over windows of **64–128 unrolls — 5 to 9× longer than the rotation survives** — so the statistic averaged ~4 real turns against 50–120 steps of arithmetic noise (D24, D30). **And the sampling is near the aliasing limit**: at unit step the rotation is sampled **3.2 times per turn** against a Nyquist floor of 2.0, and for two of three prompts only **2.6**. A discrete winding computed from consecutive samples at 2.6 points per turn cannot reliably resolve even the *direction* of rotation — **which is exactly the instability D28 observed, where the sign flipped with `num_steps`.** **(4) WHAT THIS RESTORES AND WHAT IT DOES NOT.** Restored: the project's original intuition that the latent trajectory rotates. **Not** restored: any winding-based claim — D22 stays deleted and D28 stays as recorded, because the metric is near-aliased and window-governed regardless of the phenomenon being real. **(5) LIMITS.** n=3 prompts, one seed, reverse-mode AD (`eig(Jᵀ)=eig(J)`, so the spectrum is right but eigenvectors are not comparable). `|arg|` varies 1.05–2.40 across three prompts, so "period ≈3 unrolls" is a rough characterisation, not a constant. A proper sweep is cheap and now clearly worth running. **(6) METHODOLOGICAL NOTE.** This is the observability principle paying for itself immediately: *prefer a metric that depends on the operator over one that depends on a sampled trajectory, because the operator has no recording window.* The decisive number cost zero GPU time and had been sitting in a log for over a week. |
| D56 | **H1's settle/loop/drift regimes are VACUOUS: the three classes have statistically indistinguishable winding (Kruskal-Wallis p=0.48), and every trajectory has winding ≈0.66 regardless of its label. And accuracy was never measured on 8 of the 10 task families whose geometry was.** | `results/h2_loops.csv`, `phase.csv`, `homology.csv` and a census of all `results/*.csv`; no new compute. Full inventory in `docs/task_and_metric_status.md` | **Derived locally, 2026-08-04. Closes the `rigor_audit` §18 note that "H1 was never tested" — it now has been, and it fails.** **(1) THE REGIMES DO NOT SEPARATE.** settle **0.6618 ± 0.0047** (n=27), loop **0.6606 ± 0.0054** (n=31), drift **0.6595 ± 0.0003** (n=2). Kruskal–Wallis **H=1.48, p=0.48**. Between-regime spread **0.0023** against a within-regime sd of **0.0041** — the classes are *closer together than their own scatter*, ratio 0.57. `drift` has **n=2**, which is not a class. **The labels partition the trajectories without separating them**, so H1 as operationalised was never capable of being confirmed or refuted by this metric. **(2) THE LARGER FINDING, WHICH IS STRUCTURAL.** A census of `results/*.csv` shows **geometry was measured on 8 task families and model correctness on 1**. `switch` (parity), `maxtask` (running max), `projection`, `three_scale`, `three_scale_modk`, `running_count`, `nesting_depth` and `count_ones` all have winding/steps-settle rows and **no accuracy column anywhere**. Where accuracy *was* measured it is ~0 at any real difficulty: counting is 0% at n_ops ∈ {8,16,32,48}; `v6_correctness_probe.csv` shows the model exactly correct in **4/24** cases; the causal kernel gives **0/120** at M=64. **So for 8 of 10 tasks we do not know whether the model can do the thing whose "reasoning trajectory" was being characterised** — and where we do know, it largely cannot. Those trajectories may be the geometry of a model *failing*, which is a legitimate object but is not what the project set out to describe. **(3) PERSISTENT HOMOLOGY IS UNINTERPRETABLE, NOT NEGATIVE.** `homology.csv` holds **15 trajectories**, H₁ counts 0–26, 9/15 nonzero — with **no null, no surrogate and no follow-up run**. A random walk on a sphere also has H₁ cycles at some scale. H₁ was the most distinctive thing the project set out to look at and it is the least developed metric in it. **(4) WHAT THIS DOES NOT OVERTURN.** Nothing measured. It is a statement about coverage: the sophisticated results (D50–D55) sit on a base layer with large untested holes, and the cheapest fix — one generation pass per task — decides whether eight tasks' worth of geometry describes reasoning or failure. |
| D57 | **The Caesar screen is 0% in every cell — but the trained model answers almost every prompt with the memorised pangram "The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog", and Huginn ships a CHAT TEMPLATE that no task in this project has ever used. The screen cannot distinguish "cannot do Caesar" from "was never asked in the format it expects", and neither can any other accuracy number here.** | Kaggle T4 `geometry-caesar-screen` (6 cells × 20 items × 2 arms); `tokenizer_config.json` at revision `bb6621b6…` | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-04. This qualifies every accuracy measurement in the project.** **(1) THE SCREEN.** Trained exact-match **0.0% in all 6 cells**; untrained 0.0%. Per the pre-registered rule (directions.md B1) that means drop the family — but the *outputs* say the test is wrong, not the model. **(2) WHAT IT ACTUALLY EMITS.** For 5 of 6 cells the trained model returns *"The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog."* verbatim, whatever the ciphertext. That is a memorised pangram **replacing** the task, not an attempt at it. In the sixth (`named_only_s3`, minimal framing) it **echoed the ciphertext** — `banana`→`edqdqd` is the input, unchanged. So a full instruction triggers retrieval and a bare framing triggers copying; neither is computation. **(3) `char_acc` IS NOT MEASURING COMPETENCE HERE.** Untrained char-accuracy (5.8%) *exceeds* trained (0.0%) in 4 of 6 cells, because the trained model's long pangram misaligns while untrained gibberish occasionally coincides. A partial-credit metric was added specifically so a near-miss would not be recorded as zero (observability D3); it instead produced a reversed ordering. **Recorded as a case where a well-motivated softer metric was worse than the hard one.** **(4) THE PROMPT-FORMAT PROBLEM, WHICH IS THE REAL FINDING.** `tokenizer_config.json` contains a **chat template** using `<|begin_header|>`, `<|end_header|>`, `<|end_turn|>` and a `Huginn` assistant role. **Every task in this project — counting, parity, running-max, three-scale, the Barannikov tasks, ParaRule, and this one — was prompted as raw continuation text.** A pangram is exactly what a chat-formatted model produces when handed a base-format prompt. **(5) WHAT THIS QUALIFIES.** Every accuracy figure here is now "accuracy under raw-text prompting", including D35's depth-scaling (measured on answer stabilisation), D41(3)'s retraction, D47's 0/120, D54 and D56(2). It does **not** touch any geometry, probe or ρ result, which do not depend on the model answering correctly. **(6) THE TEST.** Re-run identical items with and without the chat template on the trained model; that separates the two explanations in one kernel. Until it runs, "Huginn cannot do task X" is not established anywhere in this project. |
| D58 | **The log-linear fit used for every ρ in this project is biased +0.033 at Huginn's own ρ and rotation period — an order of magnitude worse than the +0.002…+0.015 I claimed — because it fits an oscillating decay through its troughs.** | Synthetic validation in `scratch/kaggle_eps_sweep/body.py`; prompted by the external review in `files/deep_research_A/` | **Derived 2026-08-04.** D55 established the leading eigenvalue is complex (period 2.6–6.0 unrolls), so the orbit separation oscillates around its exponential envelope. Fitting log-linearly across a non-integer number of periods is biased, and my stated estimator bias never captured it. Recovery of a **known** ρ from an oscillating decay: at **ρ=0.86, period 3.2** — Huginn's actual regime — the plain fit errs **+0.0330** while an envelope fit through the local maxima errs **+0.0018**, an 18× improvement; at ρ=0.90, period 4.0 the plain fit returns **nan** while the envelope gives +0.0007. **(1) THIS PARTLY EXPLAINS THE 0.058 ESTIMATOR GAP** between two-orbit (0.8577, D52) and Arnoldi (~0.80, D31) that an external reviewer correctly flagged as blocking — a gap twice the 0.03 we declared detectable, which I had recorded without confronting. **(2) D52'S ρ IS LIKELY BIASED HIGH BY ~0.03.** The *separation* (untrained 0.7048 vs trained 0.8577, complete, d=13.2) is unaffected if the bias applies to both arms, but the absolute values are not trustworthy to better than ~0.03 and no downstream claim may rest on them at that resolution. **(3) A SECOND FIX IS ALSO OWED**: the orbit difference was never projected onto the sphere's tangent space, so a degenerate radial component may contaminate it. **TESTED AND REFUTED, 2026-08-05 (`geometry-eps-sweep`): the median radial fraction of the orbit difference is 0.0000 and tangent-projected ρ equals ambient ρ to four decimals (0.8866 both). The difference was already entirely tangential.** **(3b) AND THE ENVELOPE FIX DID NOT TRANSFER TO REAL DATA.** On the real orbits the peak-based fit succeeded for only **3 of 12 prompts**, and where it did the peak spacing implies a period of **~14.4 unrolls against Arnoldi's 2.6–6.0** — so the peaks being found are not the rotation. The synthetic +0.033 bias is real for a strongly oscillating decay; the real separation does not oscillate strongly enough for it to bite. **The 0.058 gap between two-orbit (0.887) and Arnoldi (0.80) therefore remains UNEXPLAINED after all three candidate fixes**, and is now the outstanding methodological problem. The paired design in D59 sidesteps it — a *difference* in ρ is measurable even while its absolute value is disputed — but it does not resolve it. Both fixes are implemented in the queued `geometry-eps-sweep`, which reports plain and envelope, ambient and tangent, side by side. **(4) THE ENVELOPE FIT HAD ITS OWN BUG, CAUGHT SYNTHETICALLY**: a leading-contiguous-run floor rule terminates at the first *trough* of an oscillating series, finding zero peaks at exactly ρ=0.86/period 3.2. Fixed by applying the floor cut to the peaks themselves. |
| D59 | **ρ IS STEERABLE by a gradient-free static rescale — paired slope +0.2853 ± 0.0450, r=+0.913, p=2.3e-04, 9/12 prompts monotone. But the pre-registered prediction Δρ ≈ ρ·ε is REFUTED: the measured slope is 0.32× predicted, >13 SE away. The contractive deficit is NOT branch-dominated.** | Kaggle T4 `geometry-eps-sweep`; `attn.proj` and `mlp.proj` of all 4 core blocks scaled by (1+ε) for ε ∈ [−0.10, +0.13], 12 prompts × 4 families, float32, no gradients | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-05. The instrument came from an external research pass (`docs/briefs/`, `files/deep_research_A/`), which argued a scalar rescale beats PEFT for *moving* ρ predictably. It does move it — but not by the predicted amount.** **(1) THE INTERVENTION WORKS.** Because the same 12 prompts are measured at every ε, the analysis is **paired**, which cancels the between-prompt sd entirely — the right analysis given (3) below. Mean per-prompt Δρ rises monotonically from −0.032 (ε=−0.10) to +0.034 (ε=+0.13), total swing **+0.0658**; slope **+0.2853 ± 0.0450**, p=2.3e-04. This is the project's **first intervention** on ρ; every prior ρ result was observational. **(2) AND THE PREDICTION FAILS, WHICH IS THE INFORMATIVE PART.** The a-priori claim, recorded in the kernel before it ran, was `dρ/dε = ρ = 0.8866` on the assumption that the contractive deficit is dominated by the branch Jacobian, which the out-projections left-multiply. Measured **0.2853**, ratio **0.322**, prediction outside a 2-SE interval by a wide margin. **So the branch carries only ~⅓ of the contraction; the skip/RMSNorm structure carries the rest.** That is a quantitative decomposition of where Huginn's contraction lives, obtained from a failed prediction — and it is a stronger result than the prediction succeeding would have been, because the succeeding case was consistent with a one-line linearisation. **(3) REACHING ρ=0.92 NEEDS A BIGGER EDIT THAN ANYONE PREDICTED.** At slope 0.285, Δρ=+0.06 requires **ε ≈ +0.21** — beyond the +0.13 hard-abort band the external review set on the assumption of a steeper slope. Any attempt to hit 0.92 this way must re-derive its safety margin. **(4) LIMITS.** ε is a single scalar applied to 8 matrices at once, so this measures the branch's *aggregate* share, not attn vs mlp separately — the obvious next split. 3/12 prompts are non-monotone. |
| D60 | **Prompt format matters enormously — a trivial copy task goes 15% → 100% under Huginn's own chat template — but it does NOT rescue counting or Caesar, which stay at 0% under raw, chat AND few-shot. D57's qualification is largely lifted: the capability failures are real.** | Kaggle T4 `geometry-chatfmt`; identical items under 3 prompt formats, trained model, with a copy control | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-05. Resolves D57(6).** **(1) THE FORMAT EFFECT IS REAL AND LARGE.** `copy` ("Repeat this word exactly"): **raw 15%, chat 100%, few-shot 100%.** Under raw prompting the model *continues* rather than answers — `banana` → *"The word 'banana' in the word list is a noun."* — which is exactly the base-format failure D57 diagnosed. So every accuracy figure in this project was measured in a format that costs ~85 points on a task the model can do perfectly. **(2) BUT IT CHANGES NOTHING ON THE REAL TASKS.** caesar **0.0% / 0.0% / 0.0%**; counting **0.0% / 0.0% / 0.0%**. **(3) THE FAILURE MODES SHOW COMPREHENSION WITHOUT COMPETENCE.** Chat-formatted counting answers *"There are 19 ones in the sequence."* for a gold of 37 — correct form, wrong number. Chat-formatted Caesar answers *"Original word: ONAN"* — it engages with the task and does not decode. Few-shot Caesar returns *"hello"*, the first shot's answer, copied. **The model understands what is being asked and cannot do it**, which is a much better-founded capability statement than the raw-prompt zeros were. **(4) WHAT THIS SETTLES AND WHAT IT COSTS.** D35, D41(3), D47, D54 and D56(2) survive as capability statements — the near-zero accuracies are not a prompting artefact. But their *magnitudes* were measured under a format that demonstrably suppresses performance, so any absolute accuracy number here is a lower bound, and the copy control shows the gap can be 85 points. **(5) A CAPABILITY CONTRAST FINALLY EXISTS**, though a trivial one: `copy` at 100% trained under chat format, which random weights cannot do. If D41's retracted "decodability vs capability" claim is ever revived, this is the only task in the project currently able to support it. |
| D61 | **When Huginn cannot do a task it does not fail randomly — it RETRIEVES something adjacent, by one of at least seven distinct routes, and which route fires is set by the prompt FORMAT and FRAMING rather than by the input.** | Qualitative taxonomy over the ~27 logged outputs of `geometry-caesar-screen` and `geometry-chatfmt`; n=3 samples per cell | **Derived 2026-08-05. Qualitative and small-n — `geometry-caesar-instrumented` records every item and will make this countable.** **CORRECTION 2026-08-05: the first version listed the seven modes without saying which TASK each came from, which hid the real structure. Modes 1–5 are all CAESAR, mode 6 is COUNTING, mode 7 is COPY. Attributed properly, a pattern appears that was invisible before: every echo/retrieval mode (1–5) belongs to Caesar — the only task with a conspicuous input to echo and a famous name to retrieve. Counting, whose input is a bit-string that is not a plausible answer, instead collapses to a fixed prior (6). Copy degrades to talking about the task (7). AND ALL OF 2, 4, 7 ARE RAW-FORMAT ONLY, 5 is few-shot only, and 1 and 3 come from the raw-only screen — so with the single exception of mode 6, THE RETRIEVAL REPERTOIRE IS LARGELY A RAW-PROMPTING PHENOMENON, which is consistent with D60's finding that raw prompting costs 85 points on a task the model can do perfectly.** The modes, each with its verbatim evidence: **(1) VERBATIM INPUT ECHO.** `named_only_s3` (minimal framing, no instruction): `banana` → **`edqdqd`**, which is exactly the shift-3 ciphertext it was shown. **(2) UPPERCASED INPUT ECHO.** raw Caesar: `banana` → **`ONANAN`** = the ROT13 ciphertext `onanan`, uppercased. Chat Caesar: `Original word: ONAN` — the same echo, truncated. **(3) MEMORISED-CORPUS SUBSTITUTION.** Five of six screen cells return *"The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog."* regardless of ciphertext. Note this is itself a **pangram** — a string containing every letter — which is plausibly what cipher/alphabet contexts co-occur with in a web corpus. **(4) CANONICAL-EXAMPLE RETRIEVAL.** raw Caesar, with **no few-shot examples present**: `puzzle` → **`HELLO`**. `hello` is the canonical ROT13 demonstration (`rot13('uryyb') = 'hello'`), so this is corpus memory, not prompt leakage — confirmed by the fact that it appears in the arm that had no examples. **(5) FEW-SHOT ANSWER COPYING.** With examples present, all three items return **`hello`** — shot #1's answer, never shot #2's (`world`). It copies the first demonstration's *output*, ignoring its own input. **(6) CONSTANT-ANSWER COLLAPSE.** Chat counting returns *"There are 19 ones in the sequence."* for golds 37, 29 **and** 30 — the same number three times. Few-shot counting returns **16** three times. This is not copying the input; it is emitting a fixed prior. **(7) METALINGUISTIC DESCRIPTION INSTEAD OF EXECUTION.** raw `copy` — a task the model does at **100%** under chat format — instead produces *"The word 'banana' in the word list is a noun."*, *"The word 'orange' is repeated exactly as it occurs in the text."*, *"The word 'puzzle' is a valid Scrabble word."* The second **describes the task it was asked to perform** rather than performing it. Also observed: **prompt-label copying** (`puzzle` → *'The original word is "Ciphertext"'* — it returns the literal label from the prompt) and **empty output**. **WHY THIS MATTERS.** (a) It explains why `char_acc` inverted in D57(3): a partial-credit metric scores retrieved English against gold English above chance while the trained model's pangram misaligns, so untrained gibberish beat it. **A softer metric is only safe when failure is noisy; here failure is *structured*.** (b) Mode (7) is the sharpest: the same model, same task, same weights, scores 0% or 100% depending only on whether the prompt is chat-formatted — so "capability" here is partly a property of the elicitation, and D60's 85-point copy gap is the size of that. (c) None of the seven routes is an attempted computation, which is the substantive point: the model is not counting badly, it is not counting at all. |
| D62 | **The task-accuracy run is INVALID: `batched_generate` returned an empty string for every prompt. The `copy` control caught it immediately — and my own verdict logic excluded `copy` from its check, so the control fired and nothing read it.** | Kaggle T4 `geometry-task-accuracy`; `scratch/kaggle_taskacc/out/task_accuracy.json` | **Verified 2026-08-05; result discarded.** **(1) THE EVIDENCE.** Every cell of all 7 tasks × 5 sizes × 2 formats × 2 arms reads 0%, including `copy` — which D60 measured at **100%** under chat format on the same model. The recorded samples are `'bcclfx' -> ''`, `'even' -> ''`, `'92' -> ''`: empty output everywhere. The run also OOM'd on the second format arm. **(2) THE CAUSE IS MY OWN SPEEDUP, AND MY FIRST DIAGNOSIS OF IT WAS WRONG.** D60 used the hand-rolled per-token generator; this run used `batched_generate`, which called the model's `generate_minimal`. I recorded the mechanism as "`generate_minimal` returns a dict, iterating a dict yields keys, which decode to nothing". **Checked against the source: REFUTED — it does `return input_ids`, a plain tensor, unless `return_dict_in_generate` is set, which I never set.** A second candidate, that the chat prompt's own leading `<|begin_text|>` (65504, which IS in the hardcoded stop set `{65504, 65505, 65508}` = begin_text/end_text/end_turn) trips the finish check, is **also refuted**: the check reads `next_token[i, 0].item() in stop_tokens` — the newly generated token only. **So the model genuinely emits a stop token as its FIRST output for every prompt under this path, and `skip_special_tokens=True` renders that as `""`.** What remains untested is the cache+batch path itself, which is the only thing differing from D60's working batch-1 uncached loop. **Mechanism NOT established. Rather than debug a borrowed decode loop on a borrowed GPU, `batched_generate` now keeps the generator known to work and takes the speedup from batching alone** — bucket sizes 8–16, so most of the win survives. **(3) THE TESTING FAILURE, WHICH IS THE REAL LESSON.** `batched_generate` has two unit tests and both pass. They assert the *length-bucketing I designed* — that no batch mixes token lengths — and **neither asserts that the function returns correct text.** I tested the part I was pleased with and not the part that had to work, against fakes rather than the model. A block that talks to a real model needs a real smoke test. **(4) THE CONTROL WORKED AND I IGNORED IT.** `copy` was included at every ladder rung precisely so a row of zeros could be told from a broken harness. The final verdict then computed `live = [k for k, v in t.items() if k != "copy" and ...]` — **excluding `copy` from the check it existed for** — and printed "D56(2) is confirmed" on data that shows only that the generator is broken. **A control is worthless if the analysis does not gate on it.** **(5) D56(2) THEREFORE REMAINS OPEN.** Whether the eight task families are within the model's reach is still unmeasured; the earlier `counting_accuracy.csv` figures stand as the only evidence. |
| D63 | **The attn-vs-mlp split is UNDERPOWERED and its own pre-registered gate says do not interpret it: the reproduction arm missed D59 by 0.095, larger than either component slope, and neither component is significant.** | Kaggle T4 `geometry-eps-split`; 12 prompts, paired, 5 ε values per arm | **Verified-live on GPU, 2026-08-05. Reported as a null by the rule written before it ran.** Paired slopes: **attn +0.0225 ± 0.0828 (p=0.80)**, **mlp +0.1294 ± 0.0781 (p=0.20)**, both (2-point) **+0.1908**. **(1) THE GATE FAILS.** Prediction 3 required the `both` arm to reproduce D59's +0.2853; it gave **+0.1908, deviation −0.0945**. The kernel's own docstring states that if this fails "the cross-run comparison is invalid and nothing here can be attributed to the split". It failed, so nothing is. **(2) WHY — A DESIGN ERROR I MADE.** D59 fitted 10 ε values; here the reproduction arm was economised to **two points**, and a 2-point slope on data with per-arm SE ≈ 0.08 is fragile. I cut corners on precisely the arm whose job was to certify the rest. **(3) THE "CONFIRMED" VERDICT THE KERNEL PRINTED IS WRONG.** It reported prediction 2 (MLP dominates) as CONFIRMED on `mlp/attn = 5.75` — a ratio of two statistically null slopes. **A ratio of noise is not a confirmation, and the verdict rule should have required both terms to be significant first.** **(4) WHAT IS ACTUALLY LEARNED.** With 5 ε points and n=12 prompts the design cannot resolve a +0.285 aggregate effect into two components; per-arm SE ≈ 0.08 against components plausibly ≈ 0.1–0.2. A usable split needs more ε points per arm, not more arms. D59's aggregate result is untouched — it used a different, better-powered design. |
| D64 | **THE FIRST TOPOLOGICAL POSITIVE: H1 loop COUNT beats a manifold-matched null in 9/9 trajectories that have any loops at all (real 8.7 vs surrogate 1.9). But the excess is in the NUMBER of low-persistence cycles, not their prominence — the most persistent cycle is no more persistent than the surrogate's — and the statistic only exists at all when ≥64 unrolls are recorded.** | `scripts/run_homology_null.py` on the 15 saved trajectories, 40 surrogates each; `results/homology_null.csv`; no GPU | **Derived locally, 2026-08-05. Closes backlog B4.8, the last untested topological claim and the project's original distinguishing idea.** H1 was computed once on 15 trajectories and never given a null; a random walk on a sphere also has cycles, so a bare count was uninterpretable in either direction. **(1) THE NULL IT NEVER HAD.** `manifold_matched_surrogate` preserves the sphere, the radial profile and the angular step sizes, randomising only rotational freedom — verified: radius 76.365 vs 76.365, mean step-norm 6.895 vs 6.896, matched to three decimals. **Real trajectories exceed ALL 40 surrogates in 9 of 9 cases that have loops** (p=0.024 is the floor at n=40); mean count 8.7 real against 1.9 surrogate, a **4.6× excess**. **(2) THE PERSISTENCE ALSO DIFFERS — CORRECTED TWICE BEFORE IT WAS RIGHT.** Diameter-normalised max persistence: **real 0.0021, surrogate 0.0005, ratio 4.2**. So the excess is in both cycle *count* and cycle *prominence*, not count alone. **My first two attempts at this number were both wrong and one produced the opposite conclusion**: `h1_persistence` already *returns* the diameter-normalised persistence as its second value, and dividing `life.max()` by it just recovers the diameter (~88) — which is equal across arms by construction, since both clouds sit on the same sphere with the same radial profile. That produced a spurious "persistence does not differ, ratio 1.003". Returning the raw lifetime instead left it unnormalised. **BUT THE ABSOLUTE SCALE IS THE POINT:** the most persistent real cycle spans **0.2% of the point-cloud diameter** against the surrogate's 0.05%. Every cycle involved is at the arithmetic-noise scale (D30), consistent with the saved `homology.csv`'s 0.0000–0.0090. **(3) AND THE METRIC IS STILL BUDGET-GOVERNED, WHICH IS D28's FAILURE MODE.** Loop count by recording budget: `num_steps=16` gives **0 cycles in all 6 runs**, `num_steps=64` gives **7–26 in all 9**, on the same tasks (n_ops=48/track: 0,0,0 vs 10,9,15), Mann-Whitney p=6e-04. A statistic that is identically zero at one recording length and 7–26 at another is reporting the window, exactly as winding did. **(4) HONEST VERDICT.** This is a real, calibrated positive and the first one a topological metric has earned here — but it is a positive about *counts of noise-scale cycles that only appear past a recording threshold*, not about the shape of a computation. **(5) THE SMOOTHNESS CAVEAT IS CLOSED, AND THE RESULT STRENGTHENS.** The manifold surrogate randomises each step's direction independently, giving a consecutive-step cosine of −0.13 against the real trajectory's −0.07…+0.16 — so a cycle-count excess could in principle have come from path smoothness rather than computation. A **block-permutation surrogate** (step vectors permuted in blocks of 3, re-integrated, re-projected to the sphere) preserves direction correlation *within* blocks while destroying the global path, and is far better matched on exactly that statistic: **real +0.106, block +0.055, manifold −0.165**. Against this stronger null the real trajectories still exceed **all 40 surrogates in 9/9** cases — and the block null produces **fewer** loops than the manifold one (1.0 vs 3.1), so the excess *grows* to **14.4 vs 1.0, a 14× ratio**. **The H1 excess is not a smoothness artefact.** It remains an excess of noise-scale cycles that only exist past a recording threshold — (2) and (3) are unaffected. |
| D65 | **H1, H2 and H3 ALL RETURN TO OPEN. "The measurement failed" is not "the phenomenon is absent."** | Re-reading of D26/D28/D32/D56 against D55; `CLAUDE.md` §5 instrument rule; no new compute | **Derived 2026-08-05.** **(1) H1 (settle/loop/drift) WAS KILLED BY A METRIC I HAD ALREADY PROVEN BROKEN.** D56 declared the regimes vacuous because they do not separate *on winding* (Kruskal–Wallis p=0.48). Decoding top-1 says why. At r=4 the model emits the ANSWER ('1','7','5','8'). D60's 85-point swing from 'Reply with only the answer' is that instruction fighting this convergence. And the project-wide 0% exact-match is the first generated token being 'The'. **(5) WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED.** That generation accuracy would be higher at r=4 — this measures teacher-forced rank at the FIRST answer position, not a decoded string, and a model that says 'The number is 7' is not ignorant. Geiping et al.'s GSM8K rising 9-10% at r=4 to 28-38% at r=32 is not contradicted: their scoring extracts an answer from prose. The causal test is whether a constrained prompt restores rank 1 at r=64; not yet run. **(6) IT GIVES B6 ITS FOOTHOLD.** `echo_digit` at r=4 is **the first cell in this project where the trained model demonstrably succeeds** (96%), so geometry can at last be measured on correct versus incorrect trajectories at matched difficulty instead of on failures only. |

| D70 | **6.1's CONFOUND IS NOT SUPPORTED: the content gap is ~0 even where the trained model is 100% capable — but the content probe is AT CEILING in both arms, so the test has little power, and the real lesson is that the probe measures INPUT RECOVERABILITY, not computation. So there is a genuine capability contrast — and `add1` at 100% is a task this project had never shown the model to do. **(2) AND THE CONTENT GAP IS ~0 ANYWAY — P4, NOT P3.** Trained-minus-untrained R²: **+0.0390, −0.0002, −0.0055, −0.1290**. On `add1`, where the model is 100% capable, untrained decodes the target at **0.9966 against trained's 0.9964** — identical. So on this evidence **"content is architectural" SURVIVES its strongest challenge**: the nulls of D40/D41/D48/D53 are not explained by the model being unable to do the task. **(3) BUT THE PROBE IS AT CEILING, SO POWER IS LOW.** Every R² >= 0.8709 and the untrained arm hit **exactly 1.0000 twice**; headroom from the ceiling is 0.129, so the gap could not span more than that whatever the truth. Because Huginn re-injects the prompt embeddings at EVERY unroll through the adapter on `[h, e]` (source, verified). The probe targets — a count, a digit, a sum — are linear functions of the input token bag, and that bag is present in every state by architecture. **So this probe measures whether the INPUT is linearly recoverable, not whether the model COMPUTED anything**, and both arms pass trivially. That is a different explanation for D40/D41/D48/D53 than 6.1 proposed: near-tautological not because the model cannot do the task, but because the target rides in the re-injected input. D48's "untrained count representation is literally 1-dimensional (PR 1.0)" is the same fact seen from another angle. **A content probe for this architecture must target something NOT linearly present in the input.** **(5) MY VERDICT WAS WRONG A THIRD TIME, THE SAME WAY.** The kernel printed "content gap tracks capability (Spearman rho = +0.95): CONFIRMED" because rho > 0 — a rank correlation over four gaps spanning [−0.129, +0.039], i.e. ordering noise. That is exactly D63 ("a ratio of noise is not a confirmation") and exactly what the pre-registration warned about at N=4. Two gates passed (cv_r2 nulls all negative, untrained at chance) and a THIRD was missing: **the moderated variable must have dynamic range**. Now `has_dynamic_range` in `_lib`, tested against these very numbers — and its first draft, which tested level (`min > 0.95`) rather than HEADROOM, failed to catch this case and was rewritten. |

The monotone reading stands, but it is weaker than the row's headline count implies. **(9) A DETAIL THAT STRENGTHENS D70(4).** `last` is a POSITION target, and the untrained arm gets 0.7528 on it -- exactly what re-injection predicts, since the final token's embedding is present in every unroll. So the untrained profile is coherent: it recovers what is linearly present in the re-injected input (bag sum, final-position embedding) and collapses only on quantities that must be COMPOSED over order. |

Participation ratio: trained **8.553 -> 4.889** (delta -3.621, down in **149/149**, p = 2.8e-45) against untrained **9.553 -> 9.104** (delta -0.450, 68/84, p = 8.5e-09) -- **the trained orbit sheds directions 8.0x faster**. Step cosine: trained **+0.276 -> +0.715** (delta +0.438, 144/149, p = 1.7e-36) against untrained **-0.365 -> -0.374** (delta -0.013, 51/84, **p = 0.063, not significant**) -- the untrained path does not straighten at all. So random weights DIFFUSE at roughly constant effective dimension and constant negative step alignment, while the trained orbit COLLAPSES onto a dominant mode as depth increases. That is D76(3) seen as a depth profile rather than a window sweep, at n=6 weight-sets, and it converts (3)'s 'the mechanism is ordinary' into a statement about learning: training installs the spectral structure that makes the recurrent orbit converge, and the depth law is the clearest expression of it. **A correction found while planting the synthetic control for this**: `precision_floor` returned 0.0 for genuinely-fp32 data, so fp32 orbits got no storage floor at all. fp32's step-rounding scale is ~3.7e-06 -- 32768x below bf16's -- which is why it does not bite on the banked orbits (their steps plateau at ~9e-05, a factor of 14 above it, and every window here is byte-identical before and after the fix), but it does bite on any trajectory run far enough to converge. Where it matches, the real orbit's shape demonstrably carries nothing else; where it does not, something in the orbit is unaccounted for. Both halves happened here. **(2) THE PR MATCH IS DISCRIMINATING**, because the isotropic null at the same sample count sits at 63-105 while both the real orbit and the surrogate sit at 2-6. That is a factor of ~30, so 5/7 inside the band is not a match anything would have passed. **(3) THE COSINE MISMATCH IS THE INFORMATIVE HALF.** The surrogate's cosine tracks cos(arg lambda_1) closely (e.g. 0.482 against cos(1.1007) = 0.454), so it is faithfully reproducing the measured spectrum -- and the real orbit does not follow it. That happens in **2 of 7** prompts (`local_n4`, `track_n32`) -- enough to matter for those, not enough to explain the other five. **(5) THE TRUNCATION IS NOT THE CAUSE.** Extending the 8 measured modes with 24 geometrically-continued ones moves the cosine by 0.024 and PR by 0.13 (medians). So 'Arnoldi returned too few modes' does not account for a cosine gap of 0.2-0.4. **(6) WHAT REMAINS OPEN, AND IT IS THE INTERESTING PART.** Either the 8 dominant Arnoldi modes are not the ones the orbit actually follows, or the map is not linear at the scales recorded -- and the latter would put D52's rho and every Jacobian-derived quantity in question. This run cannot separate them: it needs a spectrum computed at the same state the orbit visits, with enough modes to span D74's ~13 active directions. **(7) LIMITS.** Seven prompts, one run, one token position, 8 modes of a 5280-dimensional operator. (b) **D58 established that this project's log-linear fit is biased +0.033 at Huginn's own rho and rotation period**, because it fits an oscillating decay through its troughs -- and that bias applies to THIS row's 0.855 exactly as it applies to D52's. The genuinely independent route is **D31's Arnoldi/JVP spectral measurement, which gives rho = 0.7935, 0.8042, 0.8083.** So the correct statement is: **raw pair-based rho = 0.855; bias-corrected by D58's +0.033 it is ~0.822; D31's independent spectral estimate is 0.79-0.81; the two agree to within ~0.02 rather than coinciding.** That is still cross-method support for "the map contracts at roughly 0.8", and it is materially better agreement than the uncorrected 0.855-vs-0.80 gap -- but it is not the clean convergent validation originally claimed, and the residual ~0.02 is unexplained. **Every rho quoted from this row must carry either the raw value with the +0.033 bias named, or the corrected ~0.82.** **(4) WHAT IT DOES TO THE SUPERVISOR'S OPEN QUESTION.** The concern recorded in `docs/PLAN.md` section 1 -- that "contraction might not be the single best regime", and that the H1 argument risks circularity by ASSUMING a contraction rather than measuring one -- is answered on the measurement side: on this model, at this read position, over 16 prompts and 960 pairs, the map is a contraction and nothing in the data hints at a loop or drift regime hiding in the h_0 ensemble. **This does not make H1's argument non-circular by fiat; it removes the empirical premise the circularity worry was about.** The autonomous-map fact (`block_idx` reaches only the KV-cache slot) plus rho ~ 0.855 together mean loop/drift cannot persist here, and now BOTH conjuncts are measured rather than assumed. **(5) THE FLOOR HAS STRUCTURE WORTH A LOOK, FLAGGED NOT CLAIMED.** The residual gap is ~100x larger for the counting families (`count4` 2.15e-01, `count16` 1.46e-01, `count8` 9.98e-02) and `echo_word` (1.30-1.50e-01) than for the arithmetic/comparison ones (`add_2d` 1.8-2.3e-03, `compare_i07` 2.6e-03). Orbits from different h_0 converge to genuinely different endpoints on some tasks and near-identical ones on others, by two orders of magnitude. That is unexplained, was not predicted, and is the kind of thing D90's h_0-sensitivity result would care about -- but it is one run, unpermuted, with no control for prompt length or gold value, so it is recorded as an observation and not as a finding. **(6) LIMITS, INCLUDING A PSEUDOREPLICATION CORRECTION.** **"0.0% of ~960 pairs" overstates the evidence: whether the map contracts is a property of the PROMPT, so n_eff is 16, not 960.** The ~60 pairs per prompt are replicates of one condition, and this project has been burned by exactly this shape before. By the rule of three, 16 prompts with no exception excludes a per-prompt non-contraction rate above about **19%**, and no better -- so "no loop or drift anywhere" is bounded at that resolution rather than established absolutely. Also: one model, one read position (`core_block[-1]`), one depth schedule, 16 prompts drawn from the D93 boundary set (deliberately selected near the decision boundary, so NOT a random sample of prompts). rho is fitted on the log-gap decay above a floor multiple k=3.0; pairs whose gap never clears the floor return NaN and are dropped, which biases toward pairs that separate enough to be fittable. |

Recorded as VOID on its primary question.** **(1) THE GATE IS THE RESULT, AND IT WAS ONLY VISIBLE IN THE RAW LOG.** `out/manifest.json` contains the pairs and patches and looks like a clean 12-row dataset; the `P1 GATE ... VOID` line exists only in `out/geometry-patch.log`. An analysis reading the manifest alone would have reported a confident 0/12 causal null. **This is CLAUDE.md section 4 exactly ("a status field is not the result") and section 5 exactly ("the measurement failed" and "the phenomenon is absent" are different findings), and it nearly went the other way in the write-up.** **(2) THE INSTRUMENT ITSELF IS SOUND, WHICH IS WHAT THIS RUN ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES.** The kernel's P2 sanity check -- replay with the hook installed but no substitution -- reproduced the original rank curve exactly on both usable items (`PASS`, both). So the patching mechanism works and is ready; what failed is the SUPPLY of items on which a flip could be observed. **(3) THE DESIGN-LEVEL FINDING, WHICH IS REAL AND TRANSFERS.** Only **2 of 9** items split within a 20-seed search; 7 exhausted the cap with the donor class never appearing. D90 found 3 of 8 splitting at 10 draws; this run finds 2 of 9 at 20. **The h_0-flip handle is rarer than the design assumed, and both survivors come from ONE family (`add1`).** Any powered version needs far more aggressive boundary selection (rank-1-vs-2 candidates specifically), many more seeds per item, or a different source of within-prompt variance. This bounds the whole D90-derived family of experiments and is the main thing to carry forward. **(4) A SECONDARY OBSERVATION -- AND MY FIRST INTERPRETATION OF IT WAS WRONG IN TWO WAYS, CORRECTED 2026-08-09 AFTER AN EXTERNAL METHODOLOGICAL REVIEW.** The observation itself stands: on the 2 usable items the patch's effect on the rank curve decays monotonically with the depth at which it is applied (max deviation 17 -> 3 -> 1 -> 0 -> 0 -> 0 on `add1_i10`), reaching identity at r >= 32, and no patch changed the FINAL rank (12/12). **What I wrote about WHY was wrong.** (a) **The attenuation direction was stated backwards.** A perturbation injected at unroll r and carried to R = 64 is attenuated by rho^(R-r), so the exponent SHRINKS as r grows: 22603x at r = 0, 1843x at r = 16, 150x at r = 32, and only **12x at r = 48**. **Late patches survive; early patches decay.** This row originally listed "12x by r = 16 ... 1843x by r = 48", which inverts it. (b) **So the decay to zero at late r cannot be attenuation -- it is the opposite of what attenuation predicts, and the real cause is the design.** Donor and recipient are the SAME PROMPT with different h_0. Both converge to the same attractor h*(e), so by late r the two states are already nearly identical and **there is nothing left to import -- the patch is a no-op by construction**, not an intervention that got erased. At early r the states genuinely differ, the patch does move the trajectory (deviation 17), and *that* is what the contraction then crushes. **Same-prompt state patching is therefore dead at both ends by construction, and this null was predictable before the GPU was spent.** It is not evidence about causal structure at all. **(4b) THE REPAIR, WHICH FOLLOWS FROM THE ARCHITECTURE AND IS NOW THE PRIORITY.** `block_idx` reaches only the KV-cache slot, so the unroll is an autonomous map in which the re-injected prelude output **e is the map's PARAMETER and h is its STATE**. Perturbing h perturbs an initial condition of a contraction -- it decays. **Perturbing the e-stream moves the fixed point h*(e) itself, so it persists.** The e-stream is therefore the correct handle, and cross-PROMPT patching (rather than same-prompt/different-seed) is the correct contrast. A potency-vs-r calibration curve on cross-prompt e-patches is an instrument null in the CLAUDE.md section 5 sense and must be run BEFORE any patching null is interpreted again. **(5) THE ONE 'FLIP' IS TRANSIENT-ONLY.** `add1_i08` at r = 0 moved best_rank 2 -> 1, which the kernel records as `flipped=True`; the FINAL rank was 16 before and after. best_rank is a minimum over the curve, so the patch changed when the answer was briefly available, not what the model ends up saying -- the D86 trap. **Any future write-up quoting a flip rate must state which of the two it means.** **(6) WHAT TO DO NEXT, GIVEN THE GATE.** Do not re-run this design unchanged. Either (a) select candidates by the D90 criterion directly -- prompts whose gold rank sits at 1-6 AND whose rank varies across draws -- with >= 64 seeds, or (b) abandon h_0 as the handle and patch between DIFFERENT PROMPTS at matched token count (the D87/lenmatch design), where the contrast is guaranteed by construction rather than hunted for. |

This row CORRECTS the scope of D94 and D97, which are this project's two strongest H1 results.** **(1) WHAT WAS AT STAKE AND WHY THE RUN EXISTED.** D94 (rho = 0.855, 0 of ~960 orbit pairs at rho >= 1) and D97 (179 of 179 token positions `settle`) both read `core_block[-1]` and nowhere else. A published mechanistic analysis of looped reasoning LMs (arXiv:2604.11791, `[T]`) reports that *each layer in the cycle converges to a distinct fixed point; consequently, the recurrent block follows a consistent cyclic trajectory in the latent space.* If that is what "loop" means, then every block converging is CONSISTENT with a cycle rather than evidence against one, and a single-block read samples one point of it forever. **The pre-registered prediction, written before the run, was that the fixed points ARE distinct -- i.e. that this run would partially undercut the project's own headline. The scale check rules out a numerical artefact: separation is ~52% of the state norm, not a rounding-level difference. And the cycle is not a small wobble around the endpoint -- its perimeter EXCEEDS the total distance travelled from h_0 to convergence by 60%. **(3) SO BOTH READINGS ARE SIMULTANEOUSLY TRUE, and the apparent contradiction with the literature dissolves.** At a fixed block, the iteration-to-iteration map is a contraction with no loop or drift (D94, D97) -- that stands, unchanged, as measured. Across blocks within an iteration, the trajectory traverses a large, stable, period-4 cycle -- and it does so forever, because each block's fixed point is distinct. **These are different objects, and this project spent its H1 effort measuring the first while the hypothesis and the literature may mean the second.** **(4) WHAT MUST BE RESTATED, PRECISELY.** D94 and D97 may no longer be quoted as "no loop/drift regime exists on this model". This is a scope correction, not a retraction: no number in D94 or D97 changes. **(4b) THE TAXONOMY THAT DISSOLVES THE APPARENT DISAGREEMENT WITH THE SUPERVISOR, adopted 2026-08-09 from a sourced literature review and now the project's standard vocabulary.** "Loop"/"orbit" in this literature means three different things, and conflating them is what made our null look like it contradicted the hypothesis:
  * **sense (i) -- a genuine NON-CONTRACTING limit cycle**, \|lambda\| = 1, the state at a fixed layer returning to itself across recurrences. **No published paper measures this in a trained looped LM**, and D94/D97 are nulls about exactly this sense. Reporting them is a clean replication of the field's empirical norm, not a discovery.
  * **sense (ii) -- a cycle ACROSS layers within one iteration**, each layer converging to its own distinct fixed point, fully compatible with per-layer contraction. This is Blayney's "cyclic fixed point" (Prop. 4.1), and **this row is an independent measurement of it on Huginn.**
  * **sense (iii) -- a damped rotation**, complex leading eigenvalue with \|lambda\| < 1, an inward spiral that shape heuristics label "settle". **D55 already measured this on Huginn** (complex leading Jacobian eigenvalue in 3/3 prompts, 27/30 top modes complex, period 2.6-6.0 unrolls). **(6) WHAT IT OPENS, AND IT IS THE MOST PROMISING H2 LEAD THE PROJECT HAS HAD.** If there is a cycle, it has a geometry -- perimeter, area, planarity, orientation -- and H2 ("more reasoning steps, more turning") becomes askable about THAT object rather than about a converging path where every instrument was reading a clock (D80). The cycle's perimeter is already measured here and is strikingly stable across 6 prompts (1.602-1.643), which is itself a lead: **a quantity that barely moves across tasks is not obviously carrying task information, so the honest next test is whether ANY cycle statistic varies with difficulty at matched length.** **(7) THE CONTROL THIS ROW OWES, LAUNCHED THE SAME HOUR (`scratch/ds_cyclenull/`, job `bt102roip8snofu5kosh`).** Four blocks with four DIFFERENT random matrices will have four different fixed points for a trivial reason. **If an untrained model shows the same cycle, the EXISTENCE of the cycle is architectural and this row is a much weaker claim than it reads as.** The pre-registration says exactly that: existence is expected to be architectural, and the real test is whether the cycle's GEOMETRY (separation/residual ratio, residual drop, planarity, irregularity, size relative to distance travelled) separates trained from three independently seeded untrained draws. **Until that returns, D98 must not be cited as evidence about what training built.** **(8) LIMITS.** 6 prompts, one h_0 draw each, one model, answer position only, `num_steps=48`. The four fixed points are irregular, not a regular polygon (pairwise distances span 22-58), and no test yet distinguishes "cycle" from "four distinct attractors visited in a fixed order" -- which may be a distinction without a difference here, since the visiting order is imposed by the architecture rather than chosen by the dynamics. |

Best cases: `caesar_k` median-pairwise rho = -0.366 (p = 0.060), `nth_item_k` median-pairwise rho = +0.273 (p = 0.196). Perimeter is nearly invariant within a family across the whole difficulty range (`binsearch_depth` 144.14-144.24, a **0.07%** spread over levels 1-4). | Yandex DataSphere job `bt17gufvdcnlp777c5cg`, 63 forwards, all four core blocks hooked; `scratch/ds_cyclegeom/` + `out/cyclegeom.json`; pre-registered prediction was NEGATIVE and is recorded in the job docstring | **Verified-live on GPU 2026-08-09.** **(1) THE PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION HELD, AND IT CLOSES H2's LAST OPEN OBJECT.** Every H2 instrument before this read a CONVERGING PATH, and D80 showed those were reading a clock. D98 found a genuine period-4 cycle across the blocks that all of them were blind to, which reopened H2 on a new object. This tests it there and finds nothing: the cycle's perimeter, vertex separation, planarity, area and radius are all flat in difficulty at matched token count. **With D28, D74(6), D83 and now this, H2 has returned nothing from every instrument and every object this project has been able to construct.** **(2) THE CYCLE IS REAL STRUCTURE, WHICH THIS RUN ESTABLISHES WITH THE NULLS D98 LACKED.** Planarity is **0.933** across all three families and every difficulty level, against a simulated null for four random points in 5280-D of **0.676 +/- 0.0033** -- **78.5 standard deviations above chance**. Combined with D99's vertex separation at 0.35x random, the four per-block fixed points form a genuinely near-planar, tightly-clustered quadrilateral rather than four scattered attractors. **A quantity this stable across tasks and difficulties is structural, and that is precisely why it carries no task information.** **(3) THE INCIDENTAL RESULT IS THE MOST USEFUL THING IN THE RUN: `nth_item_k` IS A WORKING DIFFICULTY LADDER.** Accuracy falls monotonically 100% -> 33% -> 33% -> 33% -> 33% -> 0% -> 0% -> 0% as the requested index goes 1 to 8, at a **verified-constant 37 tokens** with a single-digit gold (so free of the D89 defect), rho = -0.580, p = 0.0030. **This is the first task family in the project that varies difficulty, spans the measurable band, and holds token count fixed -- the exact combination two failed experiments (D47, D95) died for want of.** It should be the default substrate for the next causal design. **(4) THE OTHER TWO LADDERS FAILED, IN INFORMATIVE WAYS.** `caesar_k` is at **0% for every k from 1 to 9** -- a flat floor, not a ladder, confirming the battery's read on that family and making it useless for anything requiring outcome variance. `binsearch_depth` is erratic (0%, 100%, 67%, 100%) and **must not be interpreted**: the fixed-body construction admits only 1-4 distinct target indices per search depth, so level 1 is a single prompt measured three times. The deep-research pass named this construction the most valuable available property; it is length-perfect but item-poor, and a usable version needs a longer list so each depth has many distinct targets. **(5) LIMITS.** 3 items per cell, 1 h_0 draw each, one model, answer position only; cycle statistics read at the final unroll. No permutation null beyond the Spearman p-values, and the Bonferroni correction is over 15 cells only. |

---

**Bottom line, if you need one sentence per hypothesis (updated 2026-07-18):**
- **H1** (settle/loop/drift regimes) — loop and drift *are* observed in this
  project's own data (B4: 13 loop + 1 drift out of 96 trajectories in
  `forceloop.csv`), but only under an artificially starved compute budget
  (num_steps=16); at full budget only "settle" is seen, and the study is
  underpowered to see loop/drift at the natural rates the literature reports
  there (B8, B9). The compute-budget-forced version (B4, Fisher p=0.01) is
  the project's cleanest positive result and is currently missing from
  README's own Results section.
- **H2** (winding/depth) — **null on the answer token**, two independently
  implemented metrics agreeing (B1, C4) — a real convergent finding in its
  own right, not just a footnote. **Real coefficient, independently
  reproduced, on content tokens** in the unmerged band test (C3) — but as of
  2026-07-18 this is confirmed, not just inferred, to fail the design's own
  stated length-matching bar (its own generating notebook says so, using its
  own printed numbers), and a second replication attempt on a different task
  design (C6) found no dissociation at all. Treat the "content tokens carry
  the signal" framing as an unresolved, promising thread specific to one
  branch/metric combination, not a validated cross-branch synthesis — the
  cross-branch comparison itself has three uncontrolled confounds (different
  `num_steps`, different and differently-sourced loaders, C6 as direct
  counter-evidence). Sensitivity check on the coefficient itself: interaction
  term drops it from +1.30 to +1.09, p<1e-4 to p=0.025.
- **H3** (contraction forces state-holding into loop/drift) — theory is sound
  (A1); empirical signal on real Huginn is small and seed-unstable (B3c) on
  answer-token geometry, and doesn't replicate under the two-scale metric
  (C6). **New (A6, 2026-07-18):** a from-scratch toy-model test (no GPU
  available, so not on real Huginn) found the mechanism does *not* show up
  on a recurrent-over-depth architecture (matching Huginn's own information-
  flow pattern) even under strong contraction, but *does* show up sharply on
  a recurrent-over-time counterpart built to match what the theorem's own
  worked example assumes. If this generalizes, H3's applied claim may need
  to be scoped to architectures where state-tracking depends on surviving
  h_0 — worth the team's own scrutiny, not yet independently reviewed.
  Overall: still not settled either way on the real model; ρ(∂ₕR) has never
  been measured on actual Huginn (A2).
